# Backlog

GENERATED by `npm run backlog:render` from `docs/backlog.yml` - do not
hand-edit this file. `npm run backlog:check-generated` fails the moment it
drifts from a fresh render (Ruling BA-6). Edit `docs/backlog.yml` instead,
then re-render.

Closing an item DELETES its row from `docs/backlog.yml` - there is no
"done" state and no archive (Ruling BA-8); git history is the record.

Three sections, one per `kind`: Bugs, Features, Chores, in that order,
always all three even when empty. `area` (first column) clusters related
rows by adjacency, in the order fixed by `src/tools/backlog/areas.ts` -
never alphabetically. `state` (a column, not a section) is one of:
- `actionable`: Worked by an agent. Leaves this section when `verify` exits 0 and actually runs at least one assertion (Ruling BA-2).
- `owner`: Never worked by an agent. Needs a human decision; the owner answers.
- `verification`: Never worked by an agent. Needs a live system or a real credential; the owner reports.
- `unscoped`: Lacks `owns`/`verify` (or both). Nobody works it until someone gives it both - fabricating either is worse than leaving it here (Ruling BA-2).

Owner ruling (2026-09-15): a row that is both a live defect and an open
owner question reads under Bugs - not under a separate section and never
left blank.

kind section.

### A3: Persist AskAiModal answers so a later session reads them back. The ideation pass ranked this FIRST; the check demoted it, because as scoped it is not the corpus class - it asserted the WRITE and left the read-back unguarded, and docs/loop/leverage.md:61 strikes generic persistence except where a record is "genuinely read back later, not for merely writing a row". Delete the read-back and the proposed test stays green.

TWO NEW OWNER ITEMS 2026-09-23, both BEFORE the migration lands, since migrations auto-apply on push here. (1) PRIVACY, and the design created it: fixing the blocker required storing the model's ANSWER, and an answer can quote roster names back, because the roster is sent verbatim (course-facts.ts:109-110). So this design digests the roster precisely in order to store NO NAME, and then stores an answer that may contain names anyway. There is no instrument for it in this checkout - no database, no model call - and the scope says so rather than inventing one. Options: store the answer as designed and accept it; store only a digest of the answer, which costs the panel its usefulness; or redact before storing, which needs a detector nothing here can test. MY RECOMMENDATION: store it, because the answer is the instructor's own material about their own course on their own account, and the roster it could quote is data they supplied - but this is yours, not mine, because it is the one place the feature writes student names to a durable store. (2) On a facts-shape version mismatch, refuse all comparison, or compare the labels present in both and report the change separately? RECOMMENDATION: compare what is comparable - refusing everything turns the feature dark exactly when an instructor most wants it, after something changed. Both are shaped so either answer ends the activity. ANSWERED 2026-09-23: the owner took the recommendation - REDESIGN AROUND PROVENANCE, dropping recall as the goal, with the retrieval interaction part of the deliverable rather than a follow-up. See docs/owner-decisions-2026-09-23.md DECISION 11. The existing scope was written against the old premise and must be re-scoped from this one. OPEN, AND docs/loop/leverage.md FORBIDS AN AGENT TO DEFAULT IT. Re-measured 2026-09-23: the 2026-09-15 check was right and is still right on today's tree, so this row's premise does not survive. Nothing persists an Ask AI answer - AskAiModal.tsx:44-45 holds it in useState, CoursesTab.tsx:448 unmounts the modal on close and destroys it, and the action at llm-content.ts:855-921 documents itself as deliberately free of the storage row shape. TWO THINGS KILL THE ROW AS WRITTEN. First, the value it claimed is gone: a39-research.md section 5 established that a chat CAN persist across sessions via Projects, so remembering an answer is not an advantage. What survives is narrower - PROVENANCE, which facts produced which answer - and that is a different feature from the one this row proposes. Second, the one existing precedent for reading an answer back (CourseIntelHistory.tsx) costs a tab switch plus an expand click plus an unscoped scan, which is MORE expensive than re-asking - the same failure shape measured for the rubric this week. A stored answer that costs more to find than to re-ask is worse than not storing it. THE FORK IS YOURS: (a) REDESIGN around provenance - store which facts produced an answer and show that, dropping recall as the goal; (b) ACCEPT the cost and build recall anyway, with a retrieval interaction cheaper than re-asking specified FIRST, not as a follow-up; or (c) REJECT the row and delete it. COST OF BEING WRONG: (a) is a different and probably smaller feature than what was filed and is the one I recommend; (b) is the expensive branch and it must beat re-asking on clicks or it ships a surface nobody uses; (c) is the only one-way door, though the row is recoverable from git. WHAT IS NOT BLOCKED: nothing else on that surface, and the A4 question about the same modal's chips is independent of this one.

### A41: A zip whose filenames do not follow the four-part studentname_date_time_filename convention silently collapses multiple students into ONE row, with no error and no visible signal beyond a graded table shorter than the class. matchStudentFileConvention (grade/utils.ts:87-107) requires at least four underscore-separated parts (:95-97); when a filename does not match - including the common case of a generic, non-underscored title such as "essay.docx" or "Homework Final.docx" - parseSubmissionFileName (:176-260) falls through, in order, past a per-file model inference and a base-name model guess (both unused at the real call site) and a zip-crossing-chain check (empty on a flat zip), all the way to leafStemFallback (:121-126), which takes the LEADING ALPHANUMERIC RUN of the filename stem as the student identity. groupSubmissionsByStudent (:290-316) then merges every file whose computed key collides into one entry's file list with no distinct-identity safeguard, so two or more different students whose files happen to share that leading run - which does not require identical names, only a shared prefix before the first non-alphanumeric character - are graded and returned as a single row. GradingTab.tsx:240 is the UI's entire statement of the input requirement: "Upload a zip archive that contains the student submissions," naming no naming convention at all.

A41's filed silent-collapse defect is CLOSED: since it was filed, A44 shipped collisionRefusal.ts (wired into extractStudentEntries extraction.ts:167-170, gradeSubmissions engine.ts:384-387, gradeAction grading.ts:929-936), which now THROWS a loud refusal - reaching the instructor on both path A (GradingTab.tsx:311-313) and path H (steps.grading-cartridge.ts:108-117) - instead of silently merging two students into one row. Verified by execution: collisionRefusal.wiring.test.ts runs A41's own worked example (Homework Final.txt / Homework Draft.txt, both keying to homework) and asserts the throw with zero LLM calls (scope docs/a41-scope.md 9c7b6608, check confirmed 3 ways). No agent-buildable in-repo work remains. The only reachable gap is gradeZipViaEngine (grading.ts:672-707, provider other, the external Deterministic Grading API), which ships the raw zip to an external service and never runs the local collision guard - but whether that service has A41's defect is unverifiable here (no network/key), and adding a local pre-flight guard there risks false-positive refusals of zips the external service grades fine. TERMINATING QUESTION: (a) CLOSE A41 as fixed-by-A44 (refuse-is-sufficient), spinning R1 out as its own owner-only row only if you later confirm the external other-provider path is affected; or (b) keep A41 open to SUPPORT THE CONVENTION (grade the non-conforming zip correctly rather than refuse it); or (c) keep it open specifically for the external-API gap. Recommended: (a). Non-agent residuals if kept: R1 (external-API behavior, needs the live service), R2 (refusal legibility on both surfaces, needs a rendered walk). NOTE the row's GradingTab.tsx:240 citations are stale (that line is now the res-fill-13 auto-scroll effect); the upload copy moved to GradingTab.tsx:373.

### A43: OWNER ASKED 2026-09-27: generate a SLIDE DECK from an uploaded content source - a repo, chapter objectives, a homework, anything - with the flexibility of an LLM and the structure of a deterministic method. The requirement that makes it hard is the conjunction: the owner must be able to upload and ask for things conversationally WHILE the output is guaranteed to use a predefined template THEY uploaded. Not a template the app ships, and not a layout the model imitates - the owner's own file, obeyed.

ANSWERED 2026-09-27: the scope recommended routing the conversational ask OUT of this row, on the measured ground that no function or tool calling exists anywhere in the app. THE OWNER SAID KEEP IT, so A43 is the whole request. See docs/owner-decisions-2026-09-27.md DECISION 15, which records what that costs: the mechanism does not exist, so building it is now the largest single thing in this row - an operation set, a schema per operation, a validating dispatcher and a refusal path - and the row cannot close until it ships. THE MITIGATION IS SEQUENCING, NOT SCOPE: the template half ships first and completely, because it is the half with a structural guarantee, and the conversational layer is built against something that already conforms. THREE THINGS THAT DO NOT RELAX TO FIT IT: the guarantee stays structural, so a conversational request may change CONTENT and may never reach the writer, the template or the layout - the operation set is the enforcement point; the interaction budget stays, so no required step, mode switch or prerequisite is added to a surface measured at three warm interactions with no course prerequisite; and the inability to add a slide stays, so an ask to add one must refuse in a way that says why rather than silently producing nothing. STILL OPEN FOR THE REVISED SCOPE, deliberately not decided: whether this is a general tool-calling mechanism or a deck-specific operation set - reusable and much larger, against smaller and certain to be re-implemented. The scope must argue it with a recommendation rather than default to whichever is easier to write.

### A45: Corroborate student identity from OUTSIDE the zip file paths. Split out of A44 by RULING 89 on 2026-09-27, because the folder-aware refusal has a MEASURED soundness hole that no rule reading only the file paths can close. The hole: on a zip that has per-student folders AND one shared folder, the run-level gate opens, the colliding group is folder-homogeneous, amnesty from refusal is granted, and a real cross-student blend is SILENTLY ALLOWED - 2159 sets, 79.1% of that shape harm. The one further attempt the iteration cap allows was made and it changed KIND rather than strength (amnesty requires two or more distinct folders each carrying a collision): that variant is SOUND, zero unsound, and takes the conservative false-refusal rate to 24.0%, worse than the 14.5% baseline A44 exists to improve. So one rule is unsound on one shape and the other fails the row own condition, NEITHER DOES BOTH, and cap 1 forbids a third attempt on that class. THE WAY OUT IS A DIFFERENT KIND OF EVIDENCE: the app already holds a roster, studentRepos usernames and a Canvas user list, and THE ZIP PATH READS NONE OF THEM. Corroborating a folder segment against a known student list decides the shared-folder case that file paths cannot.

A45's scope + check confirmed (verified against the tree, 172/172 green): the pure-function CORE is agent-buildable (an optional knownStudents param on decideCollisionRefusal + the shipped matchNameAgainstRoster + the AC-1..AC-6 oracle over the A44 generator; real frozen literal is 2254 of 19936, the row's 2159/6.4% are stale), BUT it CANNOT DELIVER ITS VALUE as scoped because there is NO course/roster context anywhere on the zip-upload surface - gradeAction (grading.ts:714) has no courseId, the form submits only provider/studentSubmissions/canvasUrl/assignmentInstructions/rubric/rubricFile, and GradingTab's gradingTarget is a Live-Feed-only display string. So A45 reframes from "thread the roster in" to "ADD COURSE SELECTION TO THE UPLOAD FLOW" - a product/interaction-cost decision only you can make. Building the core now ships dead code (no list to feed it), the exact reachability trap. TERMINATING QUESTION: (a) ADD a course/roster selector to the zip-upload surface so identity can be corroborated (bigger, adds a click - and from which source: studentRepos[].username [authoritative, binder Tier 1] or the free-text course.roster? - RES-A45-3); or (b) DEFER A45 (leave A44's file-path refusal as the ceiling, accept the 2254-set shared-folder hole); or (c) some other evidence source. Recommended: decide (a)-with-studentRepos vs (b). No agent-buildable work until you pick - the core is not worth building without a list source. NOTE: AC-1 as written feeds the matcher the generator's concatenated owner values ("AlvarezMaria"), so it passes with a comparator that fails on an ordinary-form roster ("Maria Alvarez") - AC-1 must add an ordinary-form roster case (folded into whatever build follows a yes).

### G4: An LLM call with no timeout runs under the platform default, not 60s. callLlm takes no AbortSignal, so nothing bounds a first attempt; learning-resource-links.ts:55-66 documents a 90-100s case and says the mitigation is RETRY_BUDGET_MS = 32_000 (:121), which gates RETRY decisions and does not bound the first attempt. Compounding it: src/app/page.tsx exports NO maxDuration (grep -n maxDuration src/app/page.tsx returns nothing), and this repo already records in its own comments (src/app/api/automations/run-now/route.ts:35-37, course-intel/ask/route.ts:48-59) that Next honours maxDuration for Server Actions only at the PAGE level. So these actions run under the platform default rather than the 60s Hobby figure. When the platform kills the invocation the action's own catch never runs, so the client gets a raw transport failure instead of the {error} string the action would have returned. Reachable today via discussion-replies.ts:786 and learning-resources-generator.ts:229. Recorded because two separate G3 design passes found it and filed it nowhere.

G4 is a genuine owner cost/reliability FORK (AC docs/g4-acceptance-criteria.md, SHIP). Measured: callLlm (llm.ts:375) takes no AbortSignal; 135 call sites, and 53 Server-Action files call it with ZERO bounding, so an LLM call reached from the use-client page runs under the unknown platform ceiling, not a declared sub-60s bound (the recent PRES-2 pipeline calls ARE bounded via withDeadline - not part of the unbounded set). Two named reachable+unbounded user-facing paths: discussion-replies.ts and learning-resources-generator.ts. DECISION (every answer ends it): (A, RECOMMENDED) migrate those two paths to Route Handlers that declare maxDuration<=60 and race callLlm via withDeadline (follows 16 existing routes; changes how the client calls them) - only a Route Handler can declare the ceiling a Server Action lacks; (B) accept the unknown ceiling + raw transport failures, no code, record the exposure; (C) add an OPTIONAL deadline/signal to callLlm as its own 135-site chunk - benefits all 53 action files but does NOT by itself close the Server-Action-has-no-ceiling harm. Recommend A (two paths only).

### N15-PHOTO-DOWNSCALE: Client-side downscale for the N15 picture-upload surface: a typical phone photo (the owner's stated PRIMARY input) is 3-5MB and EXCEEDS maxFileBytesForWireBudget() = 2752512 bytes, so N15 W3 (f4081cb3) currently REFUSES it with a clear oversize message. The feature works for screenshots/small images today; a canvas downscale-to-budget before base64 would restore full value for phone photos.

Measure one real phone photo of a rubric page: does it exceed 2752512 bytes after your phone/browser saves it? If yes, I build the client-side downscale; if it compresses under the budget, this row closes WONT-DO (the current refusal path never triggers for real inputs).

### N15a: The owner's one-upload-per-student workflow on the assignment-upload tool: tick a checkbox meaning each upload is one student, then upload repeatedly, each upload recognized as a different student and graded as it lands. FORMERLY TWO ROWS (N15a dispatch, N15b queue) - MERGED by Ruling N15-3 after the scope check showed the seam was cut through the middle of one correctness property: per-upload dispatch WITHOUT the queue is not a smaller working feature, it is an unserialized dispatcher, because getGeminiInterRequestDelayMs is applied only BETWEEN entries inside one gradeStudentEntries call (engine.ts:207-209), so N one-entry calls pay it zero times. What survives from the scoping pass: gradeEntries (engine.ts:333-341) really is a generic per-entry wrapper, and canvasWorkToEntry (extraction.ts:153) is a real precedent for building one entry directly.

OPEN 2026-09-23, and one reading is already disqualified by measurement. This row admits TWO different features and the pass costed both against docs/a39-census.md's own formula. (a) REPEATED SINGLE UPLOADS that append rows: 2 interactions per student, which by the census's crossover rule NEVER beats the chat baseline and is strictly worse than the zip path sitting beside it. (b) ONE MULTI-SELECT UPLOAD of N files: matches the zip path's profile. MY RECOMMENDATION IS (b), or dropping the row if the zip path already serves it. WHAT MAKES THIS MORE THAN A COST QUESTION: under (a) the naming hazard is worse than A41's. A filename stem is safe within ONE request, but across repeated uploads two students with generic filenames produce an IDENTICAL student string, which is used as a live React key and a dictionary key across seventeen lines of keyed state - silent cross-wiring into a gradebook rather than a visible failure. No instructor-typed name field and no roster-match utility exist in the tree, verified with canaries, so (a) also needs an identity source built. COST OF BEING WRONG: choosing (a) builds a flow that is measurably slower than what the app already has, plus an identity mechanism to make it safe. Choosing (b) is close to the existing zip path and may make the row redundant, which is a cheap thing to discover. NOTHING IS BLOCKED: A39's own waves continue regardless.

They differ from the three chartered sites in being DIRECT-ONLY - each checks one file's own source rather than anything transitive - which makes them cheaper to fix and weaker to begin with. They are recorded here so the next reader knows the family is wider than the row that fixes part of it. THE REAL POINT, and it is the argument for the shape A23 chose: a guard that enumerates spellings of a BANNED import fails SILENTLY, because an un-enumerated form is simply permitted. Five independent authors wrote five variants of that same pattern and all five have the same hole, which is what a class looks like when the mechanism is wrong rather than the instance. SO THE MEASURED TRUTH IS: the two guards DO share a corrective rule, which is what this row said; what they do not share is a single hole, which is what the correction said. Do not conclude a pattern is absent from a bash grep alone. WHY THIS IS ITS OWN ROW AND NOT PART OF A22. A22 closes a NAMED, VISIBLE carve-out in the grading-results sweep, and it REJECTED building a type-aware classifier precisely because this one exists and is broken - the rejection is the evidence. Folding the fix into A22 would entangle a hygiene chore in one directory with a live defect in another, and A22's own recommendation is a construction (set equality) that does not need a classifier at all. WHAT THIS ROW MUST NOT DO: reach for a better regex. A12/A13's Ruling U3 already replaced one regex guard here with a WALLED-SET check after the regex was shown to miss export-from, export-star, require and dynamic import; that is the shape to follow. FIRST STEP, owed before any fix: find out what the guard was protecting and whether the false negative has already let something through. A guard broken in the permissive direction does not fail loudly - it passes, which is this repo's single most repeated defect class. Check whether any file in repo-grades currently carries a multi-line value import of the barrel; if one does, the guard has been decorative and the row is a bug fix, not a hygiene chore. Found 2026-09-21 by the A16 wave 3 scoping pass as a pre-existing defect - NOT introduced by A16 and NOT EXECUTED, only read. | - | - | - | Read, not executed, 2026-09-21: the guard at useRepoGradesBulkGrade.ts:174 reads a value that can be stale across the await that resolves the rubric, so a second click inside that window sees the pre-run state. This is a READING of the code, and the claim has not been reproduced - nothing renders under vitest here and the handler is executed by no test, so the first job of this row is to establish whether the race is real rather than to fix it. | A16 wave 3 scoping pass, docs/a16-wave3-scope.md, 2026-09-21 | SCOPED TOGETHER WITH A27 2026-09-21 TO docs/a26-a27-scope.md, AND BOTH ARE NOW PROVEN, NOT READ: the real handler was driven in a node test with a stand-in React and a stubbed grading action. A26: two clicks while the rubric fetch was pending sent SIX grading calls for three repos - every repo graded twice, possibly with different scores - and a control proved the harness does refuse a second click once the flag is visible. A27: one rejected call left every later render showing the column as running, the progress label frozen, the activity log and status line never written, and the failed cell stuck on Grading. The race began at 85fa958, when the rubric picker added an await before the run starts. THE FIX HAS THREE PARTS AND EACH WAS PROVEN NECESSARY by a broken variant its own test catches: a lock held in a ref and claimed at click time with the rubric fetch moved under it; a finally that releases it (claiming at click WITHOUT this creates a new stuck path - exactly the second stale-state path these rows warned about); and a catch on each grading call so a rejection becomes an ordinary failed repo. IT MUST AMEND A16 WAVE 3 PINS: one of them pins the stale read that IS the bug, so the amendment replaces it with a ref-based check and adds a check that the old guard fails - amending a guard openly, not loosening it. SEQUENCE: after the A16 wave 3 detector fixes land, since both edit the same wiring test. A MISLEADING COMMENT at useRepoGradesRubricSource.ts:339-343 claims the rubric-loading state disables every Grade button; it does not, and anyone trusting it would think this row was already fixed. RESIDUALS: RR-1, real browser timing of the race - owner, a real browser, after the fix ships; RR-2, the actual error text a platform failure produces - owner, same pass; RR-3, a misleading comment in A16's classTrendsFolderEntry.ts - fold into the build; R-6, the per-cell Grade button has the same stuck-on-rejection defect - separable, and in scope only if the build stays small. RECORDED AS ITS OWN ROW BECAUSE IT IS OUT OF A16's SCOPE AND A16's WAVE 3 SHOULD NOT FIX IT SILENTLY - a concurrency fix inside a feature wave is the kind of change that ships unreviewed. WHY IT MATTERS IF REAL: two concurrent bulk runs over the same column would double-grade every repo and double the model spend, and once A16 wave 3 lands, two runs would race to set the column's trends. SEQUENCE: establish the race first, by measurement, before anyone designs a fix - a reading of a stale-closure hazard has been wrong before in this repo, and the correct response to an unexecuted claim is to execute it. | Grading runs: bounds, failures, not-attempted rows, class-trends signal | A41 | owner | A zip whose filenames do not follow the four-part studentname_date_time_filename convention silently collapses multiple students into ONE row, with no error and no visible signal beyond a graded table shorter than the class. matchStudentFileConvention (grade/utils.ts:87-107) requires at least four underscore-separated parts (:95-97); when a filename does not match - including the common case of a generic, non-underscored title such as "essay.docx" or "Homework Final.docx" - parseSubmissionFileName (:176-260) falls through, in order, past a per-file model inference and a base-name model guess (both unused at the real call site) and a zip-crossing-chain check (empty on a flat zip), all the way to leafStemFallback (:121-126), which takes the LEADING ALPHANUMERIC RUN of the filename stem as the student identity. groupSubmissionsByStudent (:290-316) then merges every file whose computed key collides into one entry's file list with no distinct-identity safeguard, so two or more different students whose files happen to share that leading run - which does not require identical names, only a shared prefix before the first non-alphanumeric character - are graded and returned as a single row. GradingTab.tsx:240 is the UI's entire statement of the input requirement: "Upload a zip archive that contains the student submissions," naming no naming convention at all. | - | - | - | RE-MEASURED 2026-09-23 directly against the tree; every claim below opened via the Read tool, and the collapsing behaviour was additionally executed with node -e against the real regex and function logic copied verbatim from source (not simulated from memory). src/lib/grade/utils.ts: matchStudentFileConvention at :87-107, the four-part requirement at :90 (parts = name.split("_")) and :95-97 (if (parts.length < 4) return null); leafStemFallback at :121-126, regex /^([A-Za-z0-9]+)/ against the extension-stripped stem at :123. THE CENSUS'S OWN WORKED EXAMPLE DOES NOT HOLD AND IS CORRECTED HERE: docs/a39-census.md section 4 (P7) and the brief that filed this row both state that "essay1.docx, essay2.docx, essay3.docx" collapses into one student called "essay". Running the extracted leafStemFallback logic in node shows each of essay1.docx/essay2.docx/essay3.docx produces a DISTINCT key (essay1, essay2, essay3), because the trailing digit is itself alphanumeric and the regex captures the whole leading run, not just the letters - so that specific example does NOT collapse and must not be cited as a demonstrated failure. The regex DOES collapse files that share an identical leading run up to the first non-alphanumeric character: the same node run confirms "Homework Final.docx" and "Homework Draft.docx" both key to "homework", and "Essay Submission.pdf"/"Essay Reflection.pdf" both key to "essay" - two differently-named, plausible student submissions producing the same key, which is the shape that actually collapses. THE FALLTHROUGH IS REACHED IN PRACTICE, not merely possible in theory: parseSubmissionFileName's priority order, read at :176-260 (step 1 byRaw :188-198, step 2 leaf-convention :200-208, step 3 zip-crossing-chain :210-226, step 4 byBase :228-239, step 5 innermost-crossing stem :241-251 only when zipChain is non-empty, step 6 leafStemFallback :253-259), and the real production call site, src/lib/grade/extraction.ts:138, calls groupSubmissionsByStudent(submissions, undefined, rawData, zipParents) with inferredLookup hard-coded to undefined - confirmed by grep -n "groupSubmissionsByStudent" src/lib/grade/extraction.ts, one call, second argument literally "undefined" - so steps 1 and 4 (the only ones inferredLookup feeds) never fire on this path, and a flat zip (no nested per-student zip, so zipParents[filePath] is empty or absent) skips step 3 and step 5 too, landing on step 6 for every non-conforming file. groupSubmissionsByStudent's merge at :301-316 keys a Map by inferred.key with no check that colliding keys came from the same student (:304-315: grouped.get(inferred.key), and on a hit the file is simply pushed onto existing.files at :315) - confirmed no dedup or disambiguation logic exists between :301 and :316. GradingTab.tsx:240 quoted verbatim, read directly: "Upload a zip archive that contains the student submissions." - no mention of any naming convention, checked against the surrounding :228-242 block for any nearby requirement text; none exists. | docs/a39-census.md section 4, prerequisite P7 (the paragraph beginning "P7 is the one that deserves its own paragraph") and residual RES-A39-4 (section 8), commit 66e8104. Filed as a row 2026-09-23 per the census's own instruction that an unfiled residual does not exist (docs/DEV_LOOP.md step 0); the census's own illustrative filenames were re-run against the real source and found not to demonstrate the collapse - see instrument for the corrected example. | THE MORE SERIOUS OF THE TWO ROWS FILED FROM THIS CENSUS PASS: this defect silently produces a WRONG result (fewer students than submitted, one student's grade and feedback built from a mix of different people's work) rather than no result, and nothing in the UI or the returned table distinguishes a correctly-parsed roster from a collapsed one - the only symptom is a table shorter than the class, which an instructor grading forty submissions has no reliable way to notice. THE HONEST REMEDY SPACE, stated without choosing among them because that choice belongs to the architect pass, not this row: (1) REFUSE the zip outright when a meaningful fraction of entries fail the naming convention, forcing the instructor to fix names or re-export before any grade is produced - simplest, but rejects real zips that would have parsed fine except for a few stray files. (2) DISCLOSE the parse result before grading - show the instructor the student list groupSubmissionsByStudent actually produced, with a count, before any model call is spent, so a collapsed roster is caught at zero cost instead of after N grades are already written. (3) SUPPORT the simple convention outright - detect the common real-world shape (one file per student, filename is the student's own name or a bare Canvas-export name with no assignment-convention suffix) as a first-class case rather than routing it through a fallback built for a different failure mode. NONE OF THESE IS PREFERRED HERE. WHAT THE SCOPE MUST ALSO SETTLE: this defect is shared by path A (Upload ZIP) and path H (cartridge drop) - both call the same groupSubmissionsByStudent through extraction.ts:138 - so a remedy scoped to only one UI surface leaves the other exposed; the scope must name both call sites or say explicitly why only one is being fixed. ANY USER-FACING SENTENCE the remedy adds may assert only what holds on every caller and every reachable state (A31 ruling 1, carried forward by A38's note) - in particular, a disclosure message must not claim a student count is authoritative when inferredLookup is unpopulated at the only real call site (see instrument), since that is exactly the condition under which the fallback silently fires. NOT IN SCOPE FOR THIS ROW: the A14 rulings' own known-open item (a sanitized-name collision inside the CONVENTION-MATCHING path itself, utils.ts:76-85, "a known-open item again, not fixed here") is a related but textually distinct defect in a different branch of the same function and is not re-litigated or re-filed by A41. NOTHING RENDERS UNDER VITEST HERE (docs/loop/this-repo.md section 6): the collapse itself was confirmed by executing the real fallback logic directly in node against copied source, which is stronger than a pure reading claim but still short of observing the actual grading UI; whether the returned table's row count is what an instructor actually sees on screen remains a reading claim like the rest of this census and is not settled by this row. OWNER: the chunk whose write set includes grade/utils.ts or GradingTab.tsx. INSTRUMENT: a unit test over groupSubmissionsByStudent (or parseSubmissionFileName directly) asserting the returned entry count for a fixture of three files sharing a leading alphanumeric run but no other content in common - RES-A39-4's own instrument, carried forward, corrected to use a real colliding fixture rather than the census's non-colliding one. DIRECTION OF FAILURE: RED when three distinct-content files collapse to fewer than three returned entries, which is today's measured behaviour. STEP: the first A39-family implementation chunk that touches ingestion, no later than the next change to groupSubmissionsByStudent or matchStudentFileConvention. | A41's filed silent-collapse defect is CLOSED: since it was filed, A44 shipped collisionRefusal.ts (wired into extractStudentEntries extraction.ts:167-170, gradeSubmissions engine.ts:384-387, gradeAction grading.ts:929-936), which now THROWS a loud refusal - reaching the instructor on both path A (GradingTab.tsx:311-313) and path H (steps.grading-cartridge.ts:108-117) - instead of silently merging two students into one row. Verified by execution: collisionRefusal.wiring.test.ts runs A41's own worked example (Homework Final.txt / Homework Draft.txt, both keying to homework) and asserts the throw with zero LLM calls (scope docs/a41-scope.md 9c7b6608, check confirmed 3 ways). No agent-buildable in-repo work remains. The only reachable gap is gradeZipViaEngine (grading.ts:672-707, provider other, the external Deterministic Grading API), which ships the raw zip to an external service and never runs the local collision guard - but whether that service has A41's defect is unverifiable here (no network/key), and adding a local pre-flight guard there risks false-positive refusals of zips the external service grades fine. TERMINATING QUESTION: (a) CLOSE A41 as fixed-by-A44 (refuse-is-sufficient), spinning R1 out as its own owner-only row only if you later confirm the external other-provider path is affected; or (b) keep A41 open to SUPPORT THE CONVENTION (grade the non-conforming zip correctly rather than refuse it); or (c) keep it open specifically for the external-API gap. Recommended: (a). Non-agent residuals if kept: R1 (external-API behavior, needs the live service), R2 (refusal legibility on both surfaces, needs a rendered walk). NOTE the row's GradingTab.tsx:240 citations are stale (that line is now the res-fill-13 auto-scroll effect); the upload copy moved to GradingTab.tsx:373. |
| Grading runs: bounds, failures, not-attempted rows, class-trends signal | RES-FILL-11 | unscoped | GradingResults.tsx:220-230's seven edit resets do not fire when canvasUrl changes and run does not, while the persistence effect at :235-237 re-persists edits under the NEW url anyway - so one assignment's feedback can be saved under another assignment's key; pre-existing, preserved bit-for-bit by RULING 131's construction | - | - | - | docs/a39-incremental-fill-architecture.md section 13 RES-FILL-11, citing GradingResults.tsx:220-230 (the seven resets, keyed off something other than canvasUrl alone) and :235-237 (the persistence effect, which re-persists edits under whatever canvasUrl currently is). | docs/a39-incremental-fill-architecture.md section 13, RES-FILL-11 (round 2 of two; uncommitted in the working tree at filing time - a transient push failure per the filing brief, content read directly from disk per docs/DEV_LOOP.md step 0); filed as a row 2026-09-28. | WHAT IT IS: GradingResults.tsx's seven edit-state resets (:220-230) do not re-fire on a bare canvasUrl change when run stays the same object, but the separate persistence effect at :235-237 DOES write edits under whatever canvasUrl is current - so typing a new Canvas URL into the field while a run's edits are still in memory can persist that assignment's feedback text under a DIFFERENT assignment's storage key. This is PRE-EXISTING: RULING 131's identity construction (this design's section 5.7, which fixes the per-arrival-wipe defect B1) preserves this behaviour bit-for-bit in both directions - it neither creates nor fixes it. WHY DEFERRED: the two candidate fixes trade against each other - adding canvasUrl to the reset dependency would make the seven resets fire on every keystroke in the Canvas URL field, which the design doc calls a WORSE defect than the one it would fix. This needs a decision about what SHOULD happen, not a mechanical patch. OWNER: the chunk that next owns GradingResults.tsx, after an owner ruling on the intended behaviour. INSTRUMENT: a gradingResultsHelpers.test.ts case over gradingResultsEditsKey and loadGradingResultsEdits with two different canvasUrl values and one unchanged run object, asserting which storage key actually gets written. STEP: after the A39 fill ships - not inside it, since the fill's own charter does not include this file's edit-persistence design. WHAT BREAKS IF IGNORED: an instructor's typed feedback for assignment A can silently land in assignment B's saved edits the moment they change the Canvas URL field without the run object changing - the same general hazard class as A36 (which this repo already shipped a fix for, on the cross-surface collision variant of the same key), but on the cross-URL-change variant within one surface. | - |

The design's own W4-9 press-twice case (useIncrementalGradingRun.lifecycle.test.ts) covers the incremental branch only, not a fdWithoutCanvasUrl()/non-gemini fixture. | docs/a39-incremental-fill-architecture.md section 13, RES-FILL-2 (round 2 of two; uncommitted in the working tree at filing time - a transient push failure per the filing brief, content read directly from disk per docs/DEV_LOOP.md step 0); filed as a row 2026-09-28. | WHAT IT IS: on the SYNCHRONOUS whole-run path, useIncrementalGradingRun.ts's finally block (:173-180) releases startLockRef before startReview returns, so two dispatches inside one render both fire - a pre-existing hazard the fill neither fixes nor worsens in mechanism, but WIDENS in surface: beginWholeRun (the fill's one whole-run door, section 5.3) now also carries Auto Grade's dispatch, which previously went through a separate path. WHY DEFERRED: the fill's own charter is the incremental/whole-run state-machine merge, not this pre-existing lock defect - fixing it is real scope, not a byproduct. OWNER: the chunk that next owns useIncrementalGradingRun.ts. INSTRUMENT: extend the existing W4-9 press-twice test case to a fdWithoutCanvasUrl() / non-gemini fixture and assert the total dispatch count is 1, not 2. STEP: before any change that makes the whole-run branch reachable without disabled={pending} guarding the control in front of it - today A7's disabled={pending} (LiveFeedPanel.tsx) is the only thing standing between this and a double-grade, and the fill adds Auto Grade as a second entry point behind that same guard. WHAT BREAKS IF IGNORED: a double-press during a whole-run grade re-dispatches gradeAction twice, spending two model-call budgets for one instructor action and racing two writes into the same run state - the same class of defect A26 already shipped a fix for on a sibling surface (the repo grader lock). FIXED 2026-09-28 (pending commit): confirmed root cause by reading + red proof - startReview's whole-run branch has no await before returning, so lock-set/beginWholeRun/return/finally-release all run in one synchronous turn and a same-render second call sees the lock already false and double-dispatches; the incremental branch is immune because it awaits prepareGradingRunAction before finally. Fix: the finally releases startLockRef via queueMicrotask (deferred one microtask), so a same-render second call still sees the lock held on BOTH branches while the incremental branch's timing is unobservably unchanged (its W4-9 test stayed green). New whole-run press-twice test (fdWithoutCanvasUrl) proven red against the in-line release. Out of scope and still open: beginWholeRun's DIRECT-call door (GradingTab handleAutoGrade), guarded only by disabled=pending in LiveFeedPanel, not by startLockRef. Suite green at 23453. COMMITTED 2036c381 (the note above said pending; recording the hash now). | - |
| Grading runs: bounds, failures, not-attempted rows, class-trends signal | RES-FILL-4 | unscoped | A partial incremental run's GradingRun holds only graded rows, while a deadline-stopped whole run holds not-attempted rows with empty rubricAreas - engine.ts:296-298 turns every counted class-trends clause off for the latter but not the former, and which behaviour the trends panel should have is undecided | - | - | - | docs/a39-incremental-fill-architecture.md section 13 RES-FILL-4, citing engine.ts:296-298's clause-switching-off behaviour for not-attempted rows with empty rubricAreas, and stating neither behaviour was measured by this design pass ('I measured neither'). | docs/a39-incremental-fill-architecture.md section 13, RES-FILL-4 (round 2 of two; uncommitted in the working tree at filing time - a transient push failure per the filing brief, content read directly from disk per docs/DEV_LOOP.md step 0); filed as a row 2026-09-28. | WHAT IT IS: an incremental run and a deadline-stopped whole run represent an incomplete run DIFFERENTLY - one omits not-attempted rows entirely, the other includes them with empty rubricAreas - and engine.ts:296-298 makes class-trends read that difference as a reason to switch every counted clause off for the whole-run case, while the incremental case (having no such rows) does not trigger the same suppression. The design pass states plainly it measured neither behaviour and does not have a recommendation beyond naming the fork. WHY DEFERRED: both behaviours are individually defensible (suppress a possibly-misleading partial signal, vs. show whatever signal exists) and NEITHER loses a grade, so this is explicitly not a blocker on flipping INCREMENTAL_ROUTE_ENABLED - the design's own section 11 closing note excludes it from the flip-blocking set. OWNER: repo owner first (which behaviour is wanted), then whoever owns class-trends.ts to implement the answer. INSTRUMENT: toClassTrendsEntry run over two fixtures - one partial incremental run, one deadline-stopped whole run - asserting which clauses fire for each, to make the divergence visible before anyone picks a side. STEP: the design names this as one of the owner-walk items in its section 12 (the design's own citation is item 4, though section 12 item 4 as currently numbered reads as the stopped-vs-complete distinguishability check - this row transcribes the design's citation as written rather than correcting it, since correcting the source document is outside this filing's write set; whoever picks this up should re-locate the intended owner-walk item by content, not by number, before relying on the citation). WHAT BREAKS IF IGNORED: nothing breaks today - the risk is an instructor reading two functionally-similar incomplete runs and getting inconsistent class-trends signal with no stated reason, which erodes trust in the panel's numbers generally, not just for these two cases. | - |
| Grading runs: bounds, failures, not-attempted rows, class-trends signal | RES-FILL-6 | verification | RULING 57's rule that every GradingRun producer stamps rubric provenance lives only in a comment (rubric-provenance-stamp.ts:1-18) and in no test - the fill's new run-header.ts caller is therefore unenforced in both directions, same as the six existing non-test call sites | - | - | - | docs/a39-incremental-fill-architecture.md section 13 RES-FILL-6, citing rubric-stamp.wiring.test.ts:72-73 (which asserts gradeEntries's behaviour, not the call-site set) and the six non-test call sites measured today: grading-run-mapping.ts:42, embedded-grader/discussion.ts:498, embedded-grader/index.ts:227, engine.ts:360, engine.ts:411, engine.ts:466. The comparison commands: grep -rnE ")\s*:\s*(Promise<)?GradingRun>?\s*\{" src --include=*.ts \| grep -v "\.test\.ts" against grep -rn "stampRubricProvenance" src --include=*.ts \| grep -v "\.test\.". | docs/a39-incremental-fill-architecture.md section 13, RES-FILL-6 (round 2 of two; uncommitted in the working tree at filing time - a transient push failure per the filing brief, content read directly from disk per docs/DEV_LOOP.md step 0); filed as a row 2026-09-28. | WHAT IT IS: the rule that every function producing a GradingRun must stamp rubric provenance (rubricUsed/rubricFingerprint) is written down only as a comment (rubric-provenance-stamp.ts:1-18), never as an executing test over the call-site SET - the one test that touches this area (rubric-stamp.wiring.test.ts:72-73) asserts a specific function's behaviour, not that every producer calls the stamp. The A39 fill adds a new producer path (run-header.ts) that is therefore just as unenforced as the six existing non-test call sites, in both directions - a producer could stop stamping, or a new producer could be added without stamping, and nothing would go red. WHY DEFERRED: writing the enforcing test is a real chunk of work (deriving the producer set from the GradingRun return-type pattern, matching it against stamp call sites) that this design pass explicitly did not attempt - it updates the comment by hand and says so, but a comment is not an enforcer. OWNER: the chunk that next adds a GradingRun producer (the next one to touch this rule at all). INSTRUMENT: the two greps above, compared - any producer-returning function absent from the stamp-caller list is the defect. STEP: the fill's own commit updates the RULING 57 comment by hand to include run-header.ts's producer; the enforcing test itself is this residual's remaining obligation, owed by whichever chunk next touches this surface. WHAT BREAKS IF IGNORED: a future GradingRun producer can ship with no rubric provenance stamped and no test will fail - the instructor-facing 'Rubric used' region (RubricProvenance) would silently go blank or stale for that run, and nothing in this repo's gates would notice. ENFORCED 2026-09-29 AT 987e5491: RULING 57 is now a two-instrument guard, not a comment. Instrument A (rubric-provenance-producers.structure.test.ts) live-derives the producer set and set-compares against a frozen classified EXPECTED (9 producers - the design corrected the row's 6), catching a new unstamped producer; Instrument B (rubric-stamp.wiring.test.ts) drives each via its EXPORTED entry point and asserts the returned pair equals stampRubricProvenance(sent text), catching a producer that stops stamping. All sabotages proven per-direction; the naive body-scan the row sketched was shown to throw 3 false positives and discarded. No provenance bug found. R3 stale comment fixed. KNOWN RESIDUAL R1 (disclosed, not closed): Instrument A's regex misses arrow-function and GradingRun\|null producers - none exist today, so the guard is complete for the current tree, not all future ones; an arrow producer would evade Instrument A (proven live). Full suite green at 23465. State -> verification. | - |
| Grading runs: bounds, failures, not-attempted rows, class-trends signal | RES-FILL-7 | unscoped | RULING 132's rejected alternative - never normalise the displayed column order mid-run, recover determinism only in the CSV export - is the specified fallback if the one terminal column reorder at run completion turns out to disorient a reader watching the table | - | - | - | docs/a39-incremental-fill-architecture.md section 13 RES-FILL-7 and section 6.2 (RULING 132), plus owner-walk item 2 in section 12: a run with a blank/unparseable rubric and at least five submissions whose feedback uses different area wording, scoring row 1 while the run finishes and watching the header at the moment the progress region disappears. | docs/a39-incremental-fill-architecture.md section 13, RES-FILL-7 (round 2 of two; uncommitted in the working tree at filing time - a transient push failure per the filing brief, content read directly from disk per docs/DEV_LOOP.md step 0); filed as a row 2026-09-28. | WHAT IT IS: RULING 132 settled that column order stays in ARRIVAL order during an incremental run and is normalised to DENSE order exactly once, at completion - a CSV exported mid-run therefore differs from the same run's CSV exported after completion. The alternative RULING 132 rejected - never normalise the on-screen table at all, and recover determinism only inside buildCsvContent's own header construction - is fully specified and buildable, not merely sketched, and is the named fallback if the accepted approach turns out to disorient a reader. WHY DEFERRED: RULING 132 is a settled decision for THIS round, not reopened by this residual - the residual exists because the settled choice has an observable cost (a column reorder happening once, live, while an instructor may be mid-edit) that only a real run can confirm is acceptable or not. OWNER: repo owner first (it is a reader-experience judgement, not a code question), then whoever owns incrementalRunPlan.ts if the fallback is needed. INSTRUMENT: F20 (already built, pure) covers both the prefix-stability and terminal-determinism properties mechanically; what F20 cannot cover is the human judgement of whether the single reorder is disorienting, which is owner-walk item 2 in section 12 - one real run, scoring a row while it finishes, watching the header at the reorder moment. STEP: at that owner walk. If the reorder loses the reader's place or a score lands in the wrong column because the header moved under the cursor, move the normalisation out of canonicalColumns and into buildCsvContent's own header construction, re-pointing F20 clause (a) to the CSV path instead of the displayed one - both functions are already pure, so the instrument exists either way. WHAT BREAKS IF IGNORED: if the owner walk finds the reorder disorienting and this residual is never picked up, an instructor's in-progress score entry can land in the wrong column at the exact moment a run completes, with no code change to prevent it recurring on every future run. | - |
| assessment-shared: row restore and feedback composition | G2 | verification | A restored assessment list is seeded from a localStorage-reading useState initializer with NO mount effect, on an SSR'd surface - so per this repo's own documented rule it never shows its restored value on reload. RE-MEASURED 2026-09-14 and the original framing understates it TWICE: the seed is not snapshot-grading's, it is in the SHARED hook useAssessmentRowStore (src/app/components/assessment-shared/useAssessmentRowStore.ts:96-98), so it affects every grading surface built on it; and "recovers by re-render" contradicts what this repo states in two places about this exact pattern. | - | - | - | MEASURED 2026-09-14. THE SEED: useAssessmentRowStore.ts:96-98 is useState<R[]>(() => typeof window === "undefined" ? [] : deserializeAssessmentRows(window.localStorage.getItem(STORAGE_KEY_TABLE), codec)) - a localStorage read inside the INITIALIZER, and grep -c useEffect over that file returns 0, so there is no mount effect to correct it. THE RULE THIS BREAKS IS THE REPO'S OWN, stated in two independent places: SnapshotGradingPanel.tsx:167-172 says verbatim that "A localStorage-seeded useState initializer never shows its restored value on an SSR'd surface - it needs a mount effect", citing LectureScriptPanel.tsx:9-17/46-60, and the same panel FIXES it correctly for instructor instructions at :174-186 using the async-IIFE-plus-cancelled-flag idiom. So the panel knows the rule, applies it to one piece of state, and consumes a shared hook that does not. BLAST RADIUS: grep -rln useAssessmentRowStore over src/ excluding tests returns 7 files, including grading-recording/useGradingRows.ts as well as snapshot-grading - so this is not one surface. A SECOND instance of the same pattern sits at snapshot-grading/useSnapshotShots.ts:100, useState<SnapshotRole>(() => readArmedRole()), which reads ARMED_ROLE_KEY with no mount effect either. UNVERIFIABLE HERE and stated as such: no component is rendered by any test in this repo, so "the list does not appear on reload" is a READING claim resting on the repo's own twice-stated rule, not on an observation. An owner reload would settle it in seconds and should be the first step. | 82f5bde | MOVED TO VERIFICATION 2026-09-14, deliberately, after being measured. It had been given owns and verify while its own evidence said the first step is an OWNER OBSERVATION - that combination would have let an agent start building before the cheap check ran, so the fields are cleared and the row sits where it belongs. THE OWNER STEP, which takes seconds: open a grading surface that has completed assessments, reload the page, and say whether the list still appears. EITHER ANSWER IS USEFUL AND THEY LEAD OPPOSITE WAYS. If the list does NOT appear, this is a live user-visible defect in a SHARED hook with seven importers, and the fix is a known in-repo pattern (below) - re-scope it actionable and build it. If the list DOES appear, then this repo's own twice-stated rule is WRONG, and that is the more valuable finding: SnapshotGradingPanel.tsx:167-172 and LectureScriptPanel.tsx:9-17/46-60 both assert it as settled, and one of them added a mount effect on its strength. Correcting a false rule two files rely on beats fixing a symptom that does not exist. THE FIX, if it is needed: move the localStorage read out of the initializer into a mount effect using the async-IIFE + cancelled-flag idiom (SnapshotGradingPanel.tsx:174-186 is the working example in the same directory; eslint react-hooks/set-state-in-effect requires setState be reached only after an await). Do it IN THE SHARED HOOK, not per consumer - the finding is that one seed has seven importers - and fix the armedRole seed at useSnapshotShots.ts:100 in the same wave, since splitting them would leave a near-duplicate row. Original scoping note follows. SCOPED 2026-09-14. The fix shape is already demonstrated in-repo twice, so this is a pattern application rather than a design problem: move the localStorage read out of the initializer into a mount effect using the async-IIFE + cancelled-flag idiom (SnapshotGradingPanel.tsx:174-186 is the working example in the same directory; eslint react-hooks/set-state-in-effect requires setState to be reached only after an await). DO IT IN THE SHARED HOOK, not per consumer - the point of the finding is that the defect is one seed with seven importers. Also fix the armedRole seed at useSnapshotShots.ts:100 in the same wave, since it is the same pattern in the same feature and splitting them would leave the queue with a near-duplicate row. CHEAPEST FIRST STEP, before any code: the owner reloads a page with completed assessments and says whether the list appears. If it DOES appear, this row is wrong and the repo's twice-stated rule needs correcting instead - which would be a more valuable finding than the fix. | - |
| Discussion replies graded as independent posts | A8 | verification | The discussion grader treats a student's REPLIES as independent posts. Owner, 2026-09-15, with a screenshot of the submission view: one student's three contributions to a Student Introductions topic are listed as three peer items - their actual introduction, plus two replies addressed to other students ("@Seth Vander Vorst", "@Luke Demmel") - under a heading that calls all of them the assignment's submissions. A reply to a classmate is not a second introduction, and grading it as one both misreads the work and inflates whatever a rubric counts. | - | - | - | Owner screenshot plus a direct read of src/lib/canvas/discussions.ts:106-152, 2026-09-15 | owner request, 2026-09-15 | OWNER DECISION 2026-09-15, asked and answered: REPLIES ARE GRADED SEPARATELY, AGAINST DIFFERENT CRITERIA. Not excluded, not merely labelled. That is the reading most discussion rubrics already use - an initial post scored on substance against the prompt, and replies scored on engagement with a classmate - and it makes the current behaviour a real scoring error rather than a presentation one: a reply is being measured against criteria written for an introduction it was never trying to be. WHAT THE DECISION IMPLIES, for whoever scopes this. (1) THE RUBRIC GAINS A SECOND AXIS, and that is the design question: does the instructor supply two rubrics, or one rubric with a reply section, or does the app split a single rubric by convention? Do not default it - it decides the whole surface. (2) THE SCORE IS NO LONGER ONE NUMBER per student per discussion, which touches whatever posts to Canvas: check whether the grade path can carry two component scores or whether they must be combined before posting, and if combined, by what rule and whether the instructor sets it. (3) contributionCount (discussions.ts:152) sums posts and replies into ONE NUMBER and must become two, or the separation cannot be counted. (4) A STUDENT WITH NO REPLIES and a student with no initial post are now different failures and must read differently. (5) The UI in the owner's screenshot lists every contribution as a peer item with no indication which is the initial post - under this decision that is not cosmetic, it is the surface where the two scores have to live. MEASURED BEFORE FILING, and the finding narrows the search usefully: THE DATA LAYER ALREADY KNOWS. DiscussionPost carries isReply (discussions.ts:13-14) and parentUserId (:15-16); extractDiscussionActivity splits a student's work into initialPosts and replies (DiscussionActivity, :19-22), computing isReply from thread depth at :61 and pushing to the right bucket at :69. So the distinction is captured correctly at the source and something downstream flattens it. TWO CANDIDATES, both visible at discussions.ts:141-152. (1) The text handed to grading is built by concatenating [...initialPosts, ...replies] with each segment prefixed "Post: " or "Reply: " and joined by a --- separator - so the label IS present, and the question is whether the grading prompt is told what those labels MEAN or whether the model just sees three chunks of prose. (2) contributionCount is initialPosts.length + replies.length, ONE NUMBER with the distinction summed away - so anything counting contributions cannot tell a student who wrote one post and two replies from one who wrote three posts. THE SCOPING PASS MUST ALSO CHECK THE UI, which is what the owner actually saw: the screenshot is a submission-list surface that renders each contribution as a peer item with its own timestamp, and nothing in it says which one is the initial post. That may be a separate defect in the same family, and if it is, say so rather than fixing only the model-facing half. WORTH ASKING EARLY, because it changes the fix: does the owner want replies EXCLUDED from grading, graded SEPARATELY against different criteria (most discussion rubrics score the initial post and the replies on different lines), or merely LABELLED so the model and the instructor can tell them apart? Do not default this. SECOND OWNER DECISION 2026-09-15, closing implication (1), the one the scoping pass was told not to default: ONE RUBRIC WITH A REPLY SECTION - "one rubric with a reply section". Not two instructor-supplied rubrics, and not an app-side split of a single rubric by convention. So the rubric input gains an explicit reply section the instructor authors, the initial post is scored against the main body and the replies against that section, and the app never has to guess which criteria belong to which. Implications (2) through (5) are unchanged and still open. DISPOSAL 2026-09-15, under iteration-caps.md cap 2 and the REPEAT-class rule. A8's scoping had two revisions and two fresh checks; round 2 returned five blockers of which THREE were REPEATs of round 1's own classes (two false-absence, two pass-condition-too-narrow). Per the routing table a REPEAT goes to disposal NOW, and the orchestrator failed to do that for a round. No further revision of this artifact is legal. Recorded here so the scoping does not restart from zero. (a) RELOCATE - the UI half. Two candidate surfaces were found by render-shape search after an identifier-shaped search wrongly concluded none existed. Candidate 1, recording/DiscussionReplyTable.tsx, DOES render the distinction (a Reply badge at DiscussionReplyRow.tsx:629 and "Replying to X" at :645-646) and DOES render a per-item timestamp (formatCapturedTime at :601 under a "Captured" header) - so the artifact's stated reason for excluding it was FALSE, though the exclusion itself probably holds on other grounds: it is the multi-student reply-DRAFTING tool, one row per post across students, and no heading calls anything submissions. Candidate 2, the screen-capture submissions list, is live and lives entirely in A9's directory. OWNER ASKED AND ANSWERED "not sure" on which screen was photographed, so this cannot be settled by more argument: RECEIVER is whoever next opens those two surfaces in the running app, OBLIGATION is to say which one the owner saw. Until then the UI half does not ship. (b) REDUCE - one question for the owner, and it gates A8-2: ON THE EMBEDDED PATH, WHAT CRITERIA DO REPLIES GET once they stop borrowing the initial post's? OWNER DECISIONS 2026-09-20, all three, and they resolve the block. (1) SCREENSHOT: "canvas split out the initial posts from the replies, but this app should recognize the replies as being replies and not mark them as original posts (like it did with the defect i reported)". So CANVAS DELIVERED THE DATA ALREADY SEPARATED and THIS APP re-merged it and labelled replies as original posts. Both scoping passes that blamed Canvas's own UI were wrong in the same way twice; the defect is this app's recognition of replies, not Canvas's presentation. (2) REPLY SCORING when the rubric has no reply section: EXCLUDE the replies from the initial-post criteria and DISCLOSE the gap in the feedback. Do not invent an app-authored reply standard, and do not keep scoring them against the main criteria. (3) ROUTING: DO NOT force graded discussions to the deterministic embedded engine. The provider choice stays authoritative; FIX THE AI PATH. WHAT THAT MEANS FOR THE SCOPE, given the check's B2: a renderer plus one prompt sentence is NOT sufficient, because the owner chose exclusion, and exclusion is a code decision - the deterministic half. The clause competes with ~30 bullets that treat the submission as one blob (prompts.ts:83-86 awards full points absent an explicit violation, and the grade-generously rules make reply-padding help the student). A re-scope must carry a GUARANTEED-class component: what the code holds regardless of what the model returns. STILL OPEN AFTER THESE ANSWERS, from the check: the fourth route at grading.ts:619/:636 (gradeOneSubmissionAction bypasses gradeCanvasUrl, and submission-detail.ts:120 gives it a body with no labels and no discussion field), and the screen-capture path, which by the owner's answer is in scope because it is where replies get marked as original posts. WHICH SURFACE IS THE SCREENSHOT, settled by a CONSTRUCTION argument rather than a grep - this is what the two earlier wrong conclusions lacked: routes a/c/d/e each produce exactly ONE ROW PER STUDENT per assignment, so none can list three peer items; (b)'s row count is per-extracted-text, because ExtractedSubmission is {name,text} (grading-submission-merge.ts:54-57) with no kind field, isSameSubmission (:301-324) needs a name match AND text similarity so three different texts by one author stay three rows, and nothing groups by name (:395-428). RULING A - EXCLUSION IS DEFERRED; RECOGNITION SHIPS FIRST. The scoped A8-A would make grading WORSE than the defect it fixes, and this is measured, not feared: prompts.ts:87-89 says an absent required behaviour is an EXPLICIT rubric violation and instructs the model to deduct for it and name it; grading.ts:806-813 REQUIRES non-empty assignmentInstructions on that path and passes them verbatim, and for a graded discussion those instructions ARE the discussion prompt ("reply to two classmates"). Remove the reply text and the demand for it remains, so the model is instructed to deduct for work the student actually did - where today that same text at least earns generosity credit under :84-85. Compounding it, the reply-section AUTHORING SURFACE DOES NOT EXIST yet, so the has-a-reply-section predicate answers no for EVERY rubric and the exclusion would fire universally rather than rarely. The owner's own words asked for RECOGNITION - "this app should recognize the replies as being replies and not mark them as original posts" - not for removal. So: build recognition and labelling now; exclusion waits until a rubric can actually carry a reply section, and until the instructions shown to the model are narrowed in the same change. Exclusion without narrowing the instructions is the prior round's disposed class recurring - content removed from a scoring path that costs the student points. A bound must therefore sit BELOW the invocation ceiling for the action to return anything at all - which is why knowing the ceiling and bounding the call are two halves of one fix, not alternatives. WAVE B SHIPPED 2026-09-27 AT 50617d9: an abort is TERMINAL on both transports. Both classification sites caught every transport rejection without discrimination and retried it, so a deadline-triggered abort would have been retried five times with backoff, swallowing the signal the deadline exists to send. One guard before the existing branch in each site; every genuine transient failure unchanged, and the roughly 40-second worst-case backoff arithmetic verified untouched by the pre-existing retry test. THE IMPLEMENTER CORRECTED MY FRAMING AND I AM RECORDING IT AS A CORRECTION. I said the bug was LIVE and that the timeout therefore bounds nothing. The second half is true; the FIRST IS NOT. No signal option is passed to either fetch call in that file - verified independently, the only hits are prose - and the race helper says in its own doc comment that losing the race does not cancel the work. So nothing in this repo can produce a real abort on those calls today. The defect was LATENT, and it would have activated SILENTLY the moment a wrapping wave threaded a real signal, because the wrapper would have looked correct while the retry loop outlived it. THAT FINDING ADDS A STATED LIMIT TO MY RULING 75, which stands rather than reopening. RULING 75 chose per-caller wrapping over a threaded signal parameter. Wave B establishes that RACING A PROMISE IS NOT CANCELLING IT: an abandoned call keeps running and keeps consuming quota, and in a file with eight call sites the next call begins while the previous is still in flight. So per-caller wrapping bounds THE CALLER PATIENCE, NOT THE WORK. That is sufficient for this row own harm - the caller stops waiting and returns a real error before the platform kill, and the process is reclaimed at the ceiling regardless - and it is NOT sufficient against abandoned in-flight work or quota burn. Recorded as a named limit of the chosen shape rather than discovered later by whoever wonders why a bounded call still cost money. Any wave that wants the work itself cancelled must thread a real signal INTO fetch, and that is a different item from this one. ALSO SHIPPED: RULING 78 filed the image transport as G5 rather than leaving it a residual for a third scope. STEP: the next chunk that edits either file, and no later than the next time a banned specifier is ADDED to either list, because adding to a list with a known hole is what makes the hole permanent. THE HOLE WAS MEASURED RATHER THAN INHERITED, in node against constructed strings: the pattern requires the literal token from, so a side-effect import, a dynamic import and a require all pass it, while export-star is a RED HERRING the old regex actually catches because it contains the word. So three forms were missed, not four, and this row's own list of three was exactly right. THE FIX REUSES A23'S scanRuntimeEdges (src/lib/module-graph/runtime-import-graph.ts), which enumerates edges by AST node kind rather than by text pattern, instead of inventing a third spelling of one guard - the A17 pass measured five different labels for one act elsewhere in this app on the same day, so a third spelling was the live risk. Direct edges only, never the transitive walk, because both files guard their own source; that choice is stated in a comment in each file rather than left to be rediscovered. BOTH GUARDS WERE WATCHED FAILING with the banned dependency injected in a form the old pattern missed, then restored from a cp backup and diffed byte-identical - which is the whole point of the row, since a guard that cannot fail is the defect and the regex is only how it got that way. ONE DETAIL WORTH KEEPING for whoever writes the next guard of this shape: a literal require CRASHED module collection instead of producing a clean assertion failure, so the dynamic-import form is what demonstrates the guard. The guard covers the require form either way - the AST scanner enumerates it - but it cannot be DEMONSTRATED this way, and an implementer who tries will read the crash as a broken test. THE CONSTRAINT THAT MATTERS: this exact regex is independently duplicated in at least 64 named functions across 85 files rather than shared through one helper - GradingRecordingPanel.wiring.test.ts:38 documents that this duplication is deliberate house style (no cross-test-file imports) - plus one shared production module (modalAdoptionScan.ts) imported by two more test files, so any fix, even applied to only one copy, changes what THAT copy's dependents see, and a repo-wide fix changes it for all 85.
