| The loop's own docs, gates and process debt | L13 | verification | A CRLF-blind stripComments copy silently stops defending its own assertions, and the repo has no check that the 53 duplicated copies agree. CORRECTED 2026-09-16 - THE FIRST FILING OF THIS ROW WAS WRONG and the correction is the useful part. It claimed all 53 copies were blind, from a SYNTAX filter (does the source mention a carriage-return escape). A BEHAVIOURAL probe - extract each stripComments body, eval it, feed it a CRLF fixture whose only comment is a line comment, ask whether the comment survived - says otherwise: of 53 files defining the helper, 45 strip correctly, 8 were unparseable by the probe and read CR-safe by hand, and exactly ONE is genuinely blind: src/app/components/snapshot-grading/snapshot-role-setrole-callsites.structure.test.ts, which does split on a bare line feed then replaces with a pattern lacking the multiline flag. THE RULE THE FIRST FILING MISSED: in JavaScript a multiline dollar matches BEFORE a carriage return (CR is a line terminator), so the dominant shape - 28 of 53 files - is CR-safe despite never mentioning CR. Only a form that splits on a bare line feed AND drops the multiline flag is blind. | - | - | - | Behavioural probe, 2026-09-16, run from the repo root: enumerate grep -rl "function stripComments" --include=*.test.ts src (53 files), brace-match each body out of its file, eval it, and apply it to a fixture built with String.fromCharCode(13) so no tool can materialize the escape. Result: 45 CR-tolerant, 0 blind among the parseable, 8 unparseable (inspected by hand: six use the multiline flag or an explicit non-CR character class and are safe; one is the SQL-specific stripCommentsAndDollarQuotes; one is the snapshot-grading chunk's own copy, fixed in that chunk). The single blind file was then confirmed directly. The two-direction demonstration that produced this row stands for the copy it was run on: with the directory forced to CRLF a sabotage that should fail stayed GREEN, and separately a comment carrying an unmatched open paren survived stripping and tripped a bracket-counting throw, turning a green test hard red on a clean checkout. core.autocrlf=true, no .gitattributes, and git ls-files --eol src \| grep -c "w/crlf" returns 453, so CRLF working files are the normal case here. | N15c remediation verification 2026-09-16; corrected by the A15 round-3 check the same day | WHY THIS IS STILL WORTH A ROW AFTER THE CORRECTION SHRANK IT: the defect is one file, but the exposure is that nothing detects divergence among 53 copies that the repo REQUIRES to be duplicated (cross-test-file imports re-run the other file's describe blocks, so importing is not available). One copy drifted; nothing would have caught a second. So the row is a decision, not a patch: either give these helpers a single non-test home that test files may import, or add a mechanical check that every copy agrees on a shared CRLF fixture. RELATED TO L9, NOT THE SAME: L9 is "most source-scanning tests have no comment defence at all"; this is "a defence that exists can stop working invisibly". A fix for L9 that mints more hand-rolled copies makes this row worse - sequence them. METHOD NOTE, recorded because it cost a wrong filing: a syntax filter over a helper's SOURCE does not measure that helper's BEHAVIOUR, and this repo's own rule that a measurement names its command is not enough when the command measures the wrong thing. SECOND CORRECTION 2026-09-20, and it corrects THIS ROW'S OWN RECOMMENDATION as well as every orchestrator brief this session. THERE ARE TWO FAILURE MODES, NOT ONE, AND THE FORM THIS ROW RECOMMENDED IS BLIND TO THE SECOND. Measured with a node one-liner against a CRLF fixture and a trailing-comment fixture. MODE 1, the original finding: split on a bare line-feed, then strip with a line-comment pattern carrying no multiline flag. CRLF-BLIND. One file has it. MODE 2, and this is the one the row got wrong: the anchored multiline form (caret, then optional spaces or tabs, then the comment pattern, with the m flag) is CR-SAFE BUT TRAILING-COMMENT-BLIND, because the leading anchor only matches a comment that begins its own line. THE SAFE FORM, and it is what the tree already uses at snapshot-grading.structure.test.ts:14-20 and useSnapshotGrade.wiring.test.ts:44-51: split on a CR-tolerant line-feed pattern, then strip an UNANCHORED line-comment pattern per line. Safe on both modes - the split consumes the carriage return, and the unanchored pattern catches a comment anywhere on the line. WHY MODE 2 MATTERS AS MUCH AS MODE 1: useSnapshotGrade.wiring.test.ts:38-43 records an EXECUTED DEFEAT that forced the in-tree form - replacing live code with a void statement plus the same code as a TRAILING comment left every assertion GREEN under a line-start-only strip. That is the L9 defect exactly, and the anchored form does not stop it. CONSEQUENCE FOR THIS ROW'S OWN FIX: converting 53 copies to the anchored form would close mode 1 and OPEN mode 2 across the whole repo. Convert toward the in-tree form instead, and keep the CR canary that useSnapshotGrade.wiring.test.ts:58-70 builds with String.fromCharCode(13). METHOD NOTE, the second this row has earned: the first filing measured SYNTAX rather than BEHAVIOUR and was wrong by a factor of 53; this correction is about probing ONE failure mode and generalising to "safe". A probe that only tries the input you already suspect confirms whatever you already believe - vary the input along every axis the helper claims to cover. CONCRETE FIX SHIPPED 2026-09-28 AT 830a456b: snapshot-role-setrole-callsites.structure.test.ts was STILL genuinely blind (it split on a bare line feed and stripped with a non-multiline line-comment pattern) despite the row earlier believing the snapshot chunk had fixed it; reproduced and fixed to split on a carriage-return-tolerant pattern, CRLF describe proven red, suite green at 23389. The larger item - a committed behavioural probe that all ~53 duplicated stripComments copies agree on a multi-axis fixture set covering both failure modes - is IN PROGRESS as its own build; row stays open until it lands. PROBE SHIPPED 2026-09-28 AT 986b0447 - both halves of L13 are now done (concrete fix 830a456b, probe 986b0447), so the row moves to verification. The committed probe (src/tools/strip-comments-agreement.structure.test.ts) TS-parses every stripComments copy in src/**/*.test.ts (0 unparseable), classifies each against multi-axis fixtures, and FREEZES the classification so any new/fixed/drifted copy turns it red. MEASURED REALITY, a real finding: of 68 JS-// copies, 26 safe, 39 trailing-comment-blind (mode 2), 2 CRLF-blind (mode 1); 0 string-aware; 2 do not strip block comments; 1 SQL-dialect copy dialect-excluded (caught by a spot-check the first classification mislabeled). WHAT REMAINS (NOT this row): converting the 41 blind copies toward the safe form is L13's own convert-toward-the-in-tree-form item, sequenced with L9; the probe DETECTS drift, it does not fix the copies. A shared non-test home partially exists already (modalAdoptionScan.ts), relevant to the eventual de-duplication decision. State -> verification: an owner may still decide whether to fund the conversion or keep the frozen-classification detector as the standing guard. 2-COPY CONVERSION SHIPPED 2026-09-28 AT 7ba8f6df: the two genuinely CRLF-blind copies (submission-kind-callsites and its GradingRecordingPanel.wiring duplicate) converted to the CR-safe split(/backslash-r?backslash-n/), and the probe now classifies both as safe with MODE1_BLIND_FILES empty. The 39 trailing-comment (mode-2) copies remain the larger convert-vs-keep-the-detector owner call. CONVERT-TOWARD-SAFE-FORM COMPLETE 2026-09-29 AT 96015a49: all 39 trailing-comment-blind (mode-2) copies converted to the safe unanchored+CR-safe form; with the 2 mode-1 copies (7ba8f6df) that is all 41 blind copies now safe, and the probe enforces it (MODE1 and MODE2 both empty, 67 safe). The conversion surfaced exactly ONE over-specified test (moduleCard.selection.wiring - it pinned incidental leftover whitespace, not the discriminating fact), fixed to assert the fact plus the corrected literal, not reverted to blind. Full suite green at 23465; no production code changed. L13 is now fully addressed: the probe (detector) and the conversion (fix) are both done; nothing in this row remains open except the standing question of whether the helpers eventually get a single non-test home (the probe's EXCLUSIONS shows modalAdoptionSourceScan already is one for some callers) - an owner-optional de-duplication, not a defect. | - |
| The loop's own docs, gates and process debt | L14 | verification | A multi-path `vitest run` SILENTLY DROPS a path that matches nothing and still exits 0, so every verify command built as a list of files reports green while covering less than it claims. MEASURED 2026-09-20 twice, independently: `npx vitest run src/lib/no-emojis.test.ts src/does-not-exist.test.ts` prints "Test Files  1 passed (1)" / "Tests  18 passed (18)" and EXIT=0. Trailing arguments are FILTERS, not paths, and a filter matching nothing is not an error. Compounding it, the backlog's own closure-runner cannot catch the shortfall: src/tools/backlog/closure-runner.ts:31 TESTS_SUMMARY_RE matches the "Tests" line and never the "Test Files" line, and it requires only that at least one assertion passed - so a verify whose paths are half typos closes its row green. | - | - | - | Found by the A10 scoping revision and re-run independently by the orchestrator from PowerShell, same output and same exit code both times. Scope of the exposure TODAY: none live - `grep -c "^  verify: " docs/backlog.yml` is 30 and every one of them is null, so no open row rests on a multi-path command. The exposure is FORWARD: the A8 re-scope already proposes a THIRTEEN-path verify, and a single-path verify IS runner-decidable because a missing or renamed sole path exits 1 with "No test files found" (covered by an executing test at closure-runner.test.ts:34-41). Also worth recording alongside: runVerify has ZERO production callers (grep -rn "runVerify" src returns 8 lines, 4 in closure-runner.ts and 4 in its own test; its doc comment at :71-76 says so), so a verify string is documentation of a decidable check rather than an executing gate until something calls it. | A10 scoping revision, 2026-09-20; re-measured independently the same day | SCOPED 2026-09-21, docs/l14-scope.md revision 1 (commit 1fdc622; round 0 was 7bda6ca); state left unscoped until the orchestrator rules. This paragraph REPLACES the residual text filed at 0b96cab - it is the only current version. RECOMMENDED FIX: a paths-only wrapper, npm run test:paths <p1> <p2> ..., in a new src/tools/vitest-paths/. It requires every argument to exist on disk as a file or a directory, runs ONE vitest process (node on node_modules/vitest/vitest.mjs, no shell) with --reporter=default --reporter=json --outputFile.json=<a temp path under os.tmpdir()>, and lets vitest's substring rule choose only what RUNS. COVERAGE is credited per argument ONLY by executed files whose resolved path is EQUAL to a file argument or lies INSIDE a directory argument - never by substring, because five real test-less paths (docs, supabase, src/lib/workflows/presets, src/lib/workflow-triggers, src/lib/resource-links; find <p> -name "*.test.ts" returns 0 for each) each contain-match other test files and would otherwise pass. An argument must be credited at least one file with at least one passed assertion; a non-zero vitest exit is propagated, a signal-killed child (exit null) is exit 1, a missing report fails closed. It ships in ONE wave with its surface: this-repo.md section 1, traps-tests.md, ALL EIGHT .claude/agents definitions (loop-ac, loop-architect and loop-top wrote three of the five live instruments), and gate-commands.structure.test.ts, which pins the string test:paths in all ten routing files plus package.json, and uses a detector covering EVERY spelling that reaches vitest run (npx vitest run, npx vitest a b, npx vitest --run, npm test, npm t, npm run test with or without --, any npm script whose value starts with vitest, YAML block scalars, fenced commands with paths on following lines, and string literals inside spawn/exec calls). It holds raw multi-path commands at ZERO in agent definitions, loop cards, AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md, package.json scripts, workflows and every src file importing child_process; counts path arguments on EVERY family INCLUDING the wrapper in each non-null backlog verify; and freezes the exact SET of existing hits (file plus normalised command) in docs/**/*.md, red on a new hit and on a stale entry. The single-path verify ruling below is KEPT; that check is its first enforcer. RESIDUALS, each with owner / instrument / step. R1: implementer briefs are prompt text outside the tree, so nothing can scan them for a raw multi-path gate. Owner: orchestrator, plus the checker of every wave. Instrument: the implementer report quoting the exact command it ran (loop-implementer.md will say: if a brief spells a raw multi-path test command, run the wrapper instead and say so), read by the Verify seat. Step: Verify of every wave after the wrapper lands. R2: open items carry live raw multi-path INSTRUMENTS a future wave will copy - docs/a19-scope.md:945 (A19, 2 paths), docs/a22-scope.md:1443 (A22, gate step 2, a directory plus a file), docs/a23-criteria.md:513 and :623 and docs/a23-test-notes.md:1297 (A23, AC-7, 2 paths each). All their paths exist today, so none is partial yet. Owner: each item next plan or test seat, converting to the wrapper - and because the docs hit set is EXACT, that seat write set must include src/tools/vitest-paths/gate-commands.structure.test.ts so the entry is removed in the same commit. Instrument: the frozen docs hit set. Step: each item next revision round. R3: closure-runner.ts:31 TESTS_SUMMARY_RE stays blind to file counts; acceptable ONLY while the verify check rejects two or more paths on EVERY command family, the wrapper included (round 0 missed npm test a b and npm run test:paths a b). Owner: the verify check in gate-commands.structure.test.ts. Instrument: that check, whose canary is a fixture row with a two-path WRAPPER verify, and sabotages that give a row a two-path verify once as vitest run and once as the wrapper. Step: every npm test. R4: runVerify still has zero production callers (grep -rn runVerify src: only closure-runner.ts and its own test), so a verify string documents a decidable check rather than executing one. Owner: orchestrator, to decide whether a backlog-automation item gives it a caller; needs its own row. Instrument: that grep. Step: the next backlog-automation item. R5: npm swallows flags given without a preceding -- in BOTH shells - measured in Bash, npm test <file> -t zzNoSuch echoed "vitest run <file> zzNoSuch" (the -t was lost and its value became a path filter, silently dropped) and npm test <file> --reporter=verbose echoed no reporter at all; the round-0 checker also measured --json, --outputFile and --testNamePattern= being swallowed. In PowerShell, npm resolves to npm.ps1 and PowerShell strips a bare -- when calling a .ps1 script, so even npm test -- <file> -t name loses the -t; quoting '--' or calling npm.cmd preserves it (checker measurement). CONSEQUENCE: through npm run test:paths the wrapper refuse-any-flag rule almost never fires. What protects the run is that a displaced flag VALUE arrives as a positional and fails the existence check, and a flag npm swallows whole only makes a run BROADER; the refusal still fires on the direct node form. The raw npm test path is unchanged. Owner: the L14 implementer, as a docs/loop/traps-tests.md entry saying exactly this - it must NOT claim the wrapper refuses flags. Instrument: none mechanical - a documented hazard. Step: the L14 wave. R6: the docs hit set is EXACT in both directions, so converting any old doc forces a one-line edit of the structure test in the same commit (the price of closing the count ratchet slack round 0 had). Owner: accepted cost; stated in R2. Instrument: the frozen docs hit set. Step: none - accepted cost. R7: the detector counts only PATH-SHAPED arguments (containing a slash, or ending .test.ts) and stops at the first bare word, which is what keeps prose after a command and unquoted multi-word -t names from counting; so a raw run of two bare substrings (npx vitest run foo bar) is not caught statically. The wrapper refuses it at runtime because each argument must exist on disk. Owner: accepted limit. Instrument: the detector unit test table (40 cases). Step: none - accepted limit. R8: docs/REGRESSION.md has no entry for gate tooling (grep -ac "closure-runner\|vitest-paths\|test:paths" docs/REGRESSION.md returns 0). Owner: baseline seat, recording the reproduction matrix below before hand-off. Instrument: grep -ac "test:paths" docs/REGRESSION.md greater than 0. Step: before the L14 implementer hand-off. R9: L15 walker count is stale - its row says 32, grep -rlE "readdirSync\|walkTsxFiles" --include=*.test.ts src \| wc -l returns 38 on 2026-09-21 - and the L14 wave adds one docs-walking structure test and one test that spawns real vitest, both in L15 class. Revision 1 enforces the L14 side: every it() in the end-to-end test and the structure test must carry an explicit timeout (30_000 in the end-to-end test) and the structure test must read its corpus once, outside any test body, checked as source text; the end-to-end tests nest vitest only over light files (src/tools/backlog/ids.test.ts, areas.test.ts). Owner: orchestrator, into the L15 row. Instrument: that grep. Step: L15 scoping. R10, THE TRAP, so no agent repeats it: vitest --json takes an OPTIONAL argument, so whatever positional argument follows it - a test file path - is silently consumed as the JSON OUTPUT PATH and OVERWRITTEN. Measured 2026-09-21: npx vitest list --filesOnly --json src/lib/no-emojis.test.ts src/does-not-exist.test.ts replaced src/lib/no-emojis.test.ts with the two bytes [] (302 lines deleted), and every gate touching that file then failed with "No test suite found in file". Restored by git checkout -- because it was unmodified at session start (had it carried uncommitted work, that restore would have destroyed it - copy first). RULE: never put a bare --json, --outputFile or any optional-value flag directly before a path; always use the joined --flag=value form (for example --outputFile.json=<absolute path outside the repo>), and never let a report path point inside the repo. Owner: the L14 implementer - the wrapper builds its own argv, with a unit test that fails if ANY element starting with - lacks =, or if the report path lies inside the repo root; plus a docs/loop/traps-tests.md entry stating the rule. The round-0 checker built the wrapper in a sandbox and confirmed it cannot recur through it. Instrument: that unit test, and git status --short after any measurement run. Step: the L14 wave. Incident disposition: orchestrator checked on 2026-09-21 - the file is identical to HEAD with 18/18 passing, A16 wave 3 committed at 08:51 after the restore and re-ran its gates, so nothing shipped on a false result. R11: the residual text first filed here at 0b96cab described round-0 R2, R3, R5, R6 and R7 (a count ratchet, a verify check blind to the wrapper, a claim that the wrapper refuses flags). DISCHARGED 2026-09-21 by this replacement, which states them as revision 1 does. REPRODUCTION (vitest 4.1.9, exact argv from Bash): a missing path, a missing path next to a directory, a quoted glob, a typo, an existing .tsx, and an existing file outside src/ (docs/DEV_LOOP.md) are ALL dropped silently with exit 0 whenever one other argument matches, and the same holds for every spelling that reaches vitest run (npm test a b, npm test -- a b, npm run test -- a b, npx vitest a b, npx vitest --run a b); any single argument that matches nothing exits 1. Cause: filterFiles in node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/cli-api.24X8XwN1.js:10860-10871 unions all filters with .some() and never checks each one; no CLI or config option makes a filter strict (vitest run --help, 65 options, only --strictTags matches strict). In the JSON reporter numTotalTestSuites is NOT a file count (34 suites against 14 files in one run) - use testResults.length. PRIOR TEXT FOLLOWS. THE FIX IS A CHOICE and should be decided before the next verify is written, because two open items (A8, A10) are about to need one. (a) RULE that a verify is always SINGLE-PATH - simplest, runner-decidable, and it forces the oracle for a chunk into one file, which is arguably the right pressure anyway; the cost is that a chunk whose guarantees genuinely span several landed test files cannot name them all, and must lean on the wave gate instead. (b) TEACH THE RUNNER to read the "Test Files N passed (N)" line and compare N against the number of paths in the command - mechanical, but it has to parse the command. (c) REQUIRE `--reporter=json` and assert the collected file set - most precise, most work. RELATED: this is the same family as L13 - an instrument that reads clean while measuring nothing - and the same family as the repo's standing rule that a measurement names its command, extended to say the command must be shown to MEASURE WHAT IT CLAIMS. Whoever takes this should also settle whether runVerify ever gets a caller, since a decidable verify nothing runs is still not a gate. RULED 2026-09-20: SINGLE-PATH IS NOW REPO POLICY for a row's verify, option (a). A checker flagged that this row left the fix as an open CHOICE while three artifacts had already adopted single-path as if settled - so the precedent was being set by drift rather than by decision, which is how an unruled choice becomes an unexamined default. The reason (a) wins over (b) and (c): a single path is DECIDABLE BY THE EXISTING RUNNER - a missing sole path exits 1 with "No test files found", covered by an executing test at closure-runner.test.ts:34-41 - whereas both other options need the runner taught to parse something it does not read today. The stated cost stands and must be stated in each row: a chunk whose guarantees genuinely span several landed test files cannot name them all in verify, and leans on the wave gate instead. That is acceptable precisely BECAUSE the closure is verify AND wave gate, never verify alone. INSTRUMENT: grep -rn runVerify src - today it hits only src/tools/backlog/closure-runner.ts and closure-runner.test.ts. STEP: the next backlog-automation item. A decidable verify that nothing runs is still not a gate, so the decision is a real one either way. R8, NO REGRESSION ENTRY FOR THE GATE TOOLING. | The loop's own docs, gates and process debt | L15 | verification | A whole-tree walker runs under vitest's 5-SECOND DEFAULT timeout, so any gate in this repo can go falsely RED under concurrent load. MEASURED 2026-09-20 by a checker mid-run: `npm test` returned "Test Files 1 failed \| 1070 passed (1071)" with src/source-bytes.structure.test.ts > "carries no UTF-8 BOM" reporting "Test timed out in 5000ms" at an elapsed 9588ms. Re-run in isolation the same file passes in 538ms, and two full-suite runs either side of it returned a clean 1071/21297 - so it is LOAD-DEPENDENT, not a defect in the test. vitest.config.ts sets NO testTimeout (grep for testTimeout returns nothing), so the 5000ms default applies to a test that walks every file in src/. | - | - | - | Found by the A8-R scoping check while the machine was running several agents; reproduced as a NEGATIVE by the orchestrator - `npx vitest run src/source-bytes.structure.test.ts` alone is 538ms, and `npm test` immediately after was 1071/21297 green. That asymmetry IS the finding: the failure only appears when the box is loaded, which is exactly when an agent is most likely to be running a gate. Other whole-tree walkers share the exposure - `grep -rlE "readdirSync\|walkTsxFiles" --include=*.test.ts src \| wc -l` returns 39 files (re-measured 2026-09-22; this row said 32, and L14's R9 said 38 on 2026-09-21, so the set is growing). NOTE THE -E: the spelling this row used to carry, grep -rln without -E, returns 0 on this tree, because basic grep treats the pipe as a literal character - the row's own stated instrument measured nothing, and at least file-size-ceiling.structure.test.ts, no-emojis.test.ts and page-module-css-orphan-classes.test.ts walk all of src/ under the same default. | A8-R scoping check, 2026-09-20; reproduced as a negative the same day | SHIPPED 2026-09-22 AT 2a2c147, recorded here because the new shipped-but-uncited guard flagged this row on the very next turn - its second true positive, and the first against the orchestrator own push. THE FIX: 38 of the 39 walker files get one vi.setConfig({ testTimeout: 30_000 }) line; the thirty-ninth already carried an explicit timeout on every it(). MEASURED BEFORE CHOOSING THE NUMBER, which is what makes it trustworthy: no single assertion exceeds about 400ms alone (worst 312ms), so the multi-second file totals are transform and import overhead and the real hazard is a STARVED assertion under concurrent runs, not a slow test. 30s matches the value already vetted in this repo for slow tests and still fails a hung test in 30s rather than minutes. NOTHING ANY WALKER ASSERTS WAS TOUCHED, proven by sabotage: a real emoji violation was introduced and no-emojis still caught it, naming the file and the codepoint. npm test ran TWICE with identical counts (22325), which is the actual pass condition - the same run must not give two answers. FIX (c) WAS CONSIDERED AND REJECTED WITH ITS REASON STATED: several files walk the same directory two or three times, measured at hundreds of ms against multi-second totals dominated by import overhead, so de-duplicating would restructure test scope for little gain. That remains open as the only unaddressed half of this row. WHAT IS LEFT: nothing an agent can do here - the row closes when the owner is satisfied the gates are no longer crying wolf. OBSERVED LIVE 2026-09-21: closure-runner.test.ts > runVerify against real vitest subprocesses > treats a filter matching zero tests in a real file ... as a FAILED verify went RED once, taking 38876ms, while another agent was running vitest concurrently in the same checkout - and passed 7/7 when re-run alone seconds later. A test that spawns real vitest subprocesses is load-sensitive, so it fails under exactly the concurrency this loop runs at. THE ORCHESTRATOR ALSO COMMITTED PAST THAT RED: the gate was piped through grep inside an && chain, so grep succeeding on the word FAIL let the commit proceed. The commit was docs-only and could not have caused the failure, but a gate whose failure does not stop the chain is not a gate. WHY THIS MATTERS MORE THAN A FLAKE: this repo's whole method rests on a red gate meaning something. A load-dependent false RED trains the reader to re-run and move on, which is precisely how a REAL red gets waved through - and it lands on the structural tests, the ones that catch the defects no other instrument sees. It is also the mirror of L14: there an instrument read clean while measuring nothing; here an instrument reads RED while nothing is wrong. Both make the gate's output uninformative, in opposite directions. THE FIX IS A CHOICE, and it should be decided before the next long wave: (a) set an explicit testTimeout in vitest.config.ts generous enough for a whole-tree walk - simplest, one line, but it slows the detection of a genuinely hung test everywhere; (b) give only the walkers a per-test timeout via the third argument to it(), so the default stays tight for unit tests - more precise, needs every walker found, and the walker set is itself a floor (39 files by the grep above, re-measured 2026-09-22); (c) make the walkers cheaper - most re-read the whole tree per assertion rather than reading once into a shared fixture. Recommend (b) plus (c) for the three worst offenders. WHOEVER TAKES THIS should also decide whether a gate that can flake belongs in a row's verify at all, or whether the wave gate is the only honest place for a whole-tree walker. SECOND INSTANCE 2026-09-20, ON A DIFFERENT FILE - this is a CLASS, not one file, and the row should be re-read that way. The A11 implementer's full-suite run failed on src/lib/recording-files.kinds.test.ts with the same signature: a 5000ms timeout under concurrent load, passing in 400ms (5 tests) when re-run in isolation, and the full re-run then came back clean at 1072 files / 21331 tests. So the exposure is not specific to source-bytes.structure.test.ts and is not specific to whole-tree WALKERS either - recording-files.kinds.test.ts is a migration test, which widens the suspected cause from "walks all of src/" to "any test whose cost is load-sensitive under vitest's unconfigured 5s default". PRACTICAL CONSEQUENCE ALREADY VISIBLE: two separate agents have now had to distinguish a real red from a flake by re-running in isolation, and both did so correctly - but that is exactly the habit that lets a REAL red through, which is the whole reason this row exists. Whichever fix is chosen, an explicit testTimeout is now the minimum, because the per-walker option (b) cannot cover a file that is not a walker. CORRECTION TO THE PARAGRAPH ABOVE, 2026-09-20, and it reverses that paragraph's conclusion. I wrote that recording-files.kinds.test.ts "is a migration test, not a whole-tree walker", that the mechanism therefore widened beyond walkers, and that fix option (b) could not cover it. ALL THREE ARE WRONG. Measured: recording-files.kinds.test.ts:19 calls fs.readdirSync over supabase/migrations and :23 readFileSync-s EVERY .sql in it - 108 files, 519K (ls supabase/migrations/*.sql \| wc -l; du -sh) - and newestKindMigration() is called FOUR TIMES, once per test, so the directory is fully re-read four times per run. It is already a member of this row's own instrument set: grep -rlE "readdirSync\|walkTsxFiles" --include=*.test.ts src returns 39 files today and this is one of them. SO: L15 is still a CLASS by file count, but the MECHANISM DID NOT WIDEN - it is walkers, as originally filed - option (b) DOES cover this file, and this file is a textbook case for option (c), because it re-reads the whole directory per assertion instead of once into a shared fixture. Isolated three times: 1 file / 5 tests, 3.42s / 1.17s / 1.17s. METHOD NOTE, the third this session: I generalised from a file's NAME ("migration test") instead of opening it. The row's own first filing made the same error in the other direction, measuring syntax instead of behaviour. THIRD AND FOURTH INSTANCES, AND THEY CHANGE THE ROW FROM A FLAKE TO A BULK FAILURE MODE. Reported by the orchestrator on 2026-09-21 and again on 2026-09-22 and recorded here as reported - this reconciliation did NOT reproduce them: whole-tree walkers under vitest's 5-second default timed out IN BULK under concurrent load, 25 failures in a single run, and the same files passed in seconds when run alone. Every earlier instance in this row was ONE file at a time, which is what made "re-run it in isolation" a workable habit. Twenty-five at once is not a flake a reader triages; it is a run whose output carries no information at all, and it arrives exactly when several agents are working, which is this loop's normal operating state. That makes fix option (a) - an explicit testTimeout in vitest.config.ts - the MINIMUM rather than one of three alternatives, and it should be decided before the next long wave rather than after it. OWNER: the orchestrator, as a one-line config decision. INSTRUMENT: grep -n testTimeout vitest.config.ts, which still exits 1 today. STEP: before the next multi-agent wave. | - |

The file input's onChange (:338) fires handleFileSelect (:161-233), which uploads via saveCartridgeDrop (:210) using whatever rubricText the component held at that instant; the rubric textarea (:405-418, id=cartridge-rubric at :411) sits BELOW the file input in render order (:330-344 the file field, :405-418 the rubric field), so an instructor who reads the form top to bottom, or who edits the rubric after already picking the file, has that edit silently excluded from the upload that already fired - no error, no confirmation, no reopen. Compounding it: four of the five form fields persist across reloads via a ta-cartridge-* localStorage key with its own effect (courseLabel :38-41/:66-70, assignmentLabel :42-45, pointsPossible :46-49, lms :50-54) and the rubric is the only field that is plain useState('') with no persisting key (:55), actively cleared after every successful upload (:220 setRubricText('')). This is NOT the RubricInputModal.tsx non-persistence policy applied here: that policy (:29-34) is written for a different component - the recording-grader's rubric modal, reachable only on paths F and G - and is scoped by its own acceptance criteria (docs/grading-via-recording-acceptance-criteria.md section 2, "The rubric modal", R2) to that one upload-then-extract flow; CartridgeDropPanel.tsx never imports or renders RubricInputModal and states no policy of its own for treating its rubric field differently from its four siblings. | - | - | - | RE-MEASURED 2026-09-23 directly against the tree (not inherited from docs/a39-census.md's citations); every line below opened via the Read tool on the real committed files. src/app/components/CartridgeDropPanel.tsx: file input :334-341 with onChange={handleFileSelect} at :338; handleFileSelect spans :161-233 and calls saveCartridgeDrop at :210, with setRubricText('') only at :220, AFTER the upload call returns - so the payload sent to saveCartridgeDrop carries whatever rubricText held at invocation, and an edit made afterward cannot reach it. Rubric field :405-418 (id=cartridge-rubric at :411) sits after the file field :330-344 in one continuous JSX block, confirming render order. The four persisting fields and their own effects: courseLabel useState+localStorage.getItem at :38-41, its useEffect at :66-70; assignmentLabel :42-45; pointsPossible :46-49; lms :50-54 - the comment directly above this block, "Form state - persisted" at :37, labels exactly these four and stops before rubricText. rubricText itself is plain useState('') at :55, with no matching localStorage key anywhere in the file (grep -n "ta-cartridge-rubric" src/app/components/CartridgeDropPanel.tsx returns no match and exits 1; grep -n "ta-cartridge" on the same file confirms the other four keys exist and none is rubric-named, so the absence grep is a real absence, not a broken pattern), and it is cleared at :220. STILL WITH THE OWNER: the disclosure half (DECISION 16 decided its CONTENT - presence plus origin with the new column - and its test notes are in round 2), and B4, whether the cross-assignment rubric fallback should exist at all. THE DISCLOSURE HALF SHIPPED 2026-09-27 AT 8708009, completing A40 as decided. The drop row now discloses PRESENCE PLUS ORIGIN - which course and assignment, or which uploaded archive, the persisted rubric ACTUALLY came from - stored in a new nullable column rather than recomputed at render, which is the whole reason DECISION 16 chose the column: a render-time comparison silently stops being true on reload. The origin is the scope the rubric ACTUALLY came from, never the requested one. That is the point of the item: the cross-assignment fallback can supply last week rubric and the row now names last week assignment. Proven by an 11-case oracle against eight mutants, including the case RULING 112 added - rubric text empty while a restored rubric is still retained, reachable because the post-upload reset clears the text but must NOT clear the retention ref, which is the untouched sentinel. A decider that checks retention before the empty-text guard scores 9 OF 9 ON THE OLD TABLE and is killed only by the two new cases. Zero added interactions per RULING 100: one hint line inside the existing course/assignment cell, reusing an existing CSS class - no new column, no new control. THE MIGRATION was checked because migrations auto-apply to production unattended on push: a single additive nullable column with add-column-if-not-exists, no default, no destructive statement. THREE THINGS THE IMPLEMENTER GOT RIGHT BY NOT TRUSTING THE SPEC. The export name the notes reached for COLLIDES with an existing one, confirmed by grep before use. A naive construction of RULING 112 mutant is a NO-OP that kills nothing, so it built the variant that actually reproduces the predicted result instead of reporting a dead end as a pass. AND IT FOUND A REAL GAP IN THE NOTES: the zero-interaction guard scans an ENUMERATED list of element names, so adding a details/summary disclosure passes all three of its assertions - it left the instrument as specified and filed the gap rather than improvising a broader tag scan. That gap is open. Non-discriminating assertions stay LABELLED as such rather than counted as coverage - one case, two clauses, and an identity that cannot disagree with itself. STILL WITH THE OWNER: B4, whether the cross-assignment rubric fallback should exist at all, which DECISION 3 own text calls a correctness defect wearing a convenience feature clothes. The origin field now makes that fallback VISIBLE wherever it fires, which is what DECISION 16 bought, but it does not decide whether it should fire. THE DISCLOSURE REMEDIATION LANDED 2026-09-27 AT 19cf77b, and the defect it fixed was MINE. The row rendered two states where it needed THREE: a null origin meant both no rubric AND a rubric whose origin was not recorded, and the copy asserted the first. EVERY PRE-MIGRATION ROW IS NULL, because the migration correctly adds a nullable column without backfilling - so historical uploads that DID carry a rubric told the instructor they did not. A false sentence about real data, shipped by the feature whose entire purchase was a sentence true in every reachable state, which makes it a repeat of my own RULING 113 from three hours earlier. The remedy was a third BRANCH using rubric_text, already on the row, not better wording. THREE MORE DEFECTS, ALL THE SAME SHAPE - an instrument claiming more than it measured. The disclosure was SILENTLY DELETABLE: one line of JSX and its import, and all eleven panel-reading clauses stayed green, because the CSS class it uses appears five times so the class-set assertion could not see one go. The migration-ordering guard asserted on the DIRECTORY last file rather than on the new migration, so a file renamed 53 places earlier passed - on the only ordering check for something that applies to production unattended. And the zero-interaction guard claimed in its own framing sentence to catch any new token of any kind while scanning a FOUR-NAME LIST, so a details/summary disclosure added the click RULING 100 forbids and passed all three assertions. All four were found by RUNNING the instrument, never by reading it, and the class is filed as A40-D7. Also corrected in the same commit: a placeholder one screen away still promised the workflow would generate a rubric if the box was left blank - the exact claim RULING 113 proved false when auto-grading is off, which it is by default. FOLDED FROM THE TOOLS-GRADING-SUBTAB AC CHECK (backlog seat, 2026-09-28), its R4: this row own owner-verification instrument text - load Tools > LMS > Grading, scroll to Submissions, pick a file with the rubric box still empty, then type a rubric - names a destination (Tools > LMS > Grading) that the not-yet-filed Tools > Grading sub-tab consolidation (docs/tools-grading-subtab-ac.md) would delete under its AC8. NO ACTION NOW: that consolidation has no backlog row yet and is still in its AC/architecture round. If and when its wave 1 ships, this row instrument text must be updated to the new destination in the same push, or it silently sends the owner to a deleted screen. Not yet measured against the tree - scope must survey what PRES-1 (src/lib/presentations/, src/app/components/presentations/), A43 (src/lib/decks/, office-template-fill.ts, the A43-S/T1/T2 ships) and A43-C (docs/a43-c-scope.md, deck-operations C1+C3) already deliver of these 13 stages before sizing any wave, and name each stage as shipped / partial / new with the file that proves it. | Owner, 2026-09-29, verbatim pasted method: source-aligned -> intuitive -> progressively technical -> visually explanatory -> interactive -> repeatedly challenged -> polished; standard: a student should be able to reconstruct the lesson from the slides alone. | EXTENDS PRES-1 (Slide Deck Creation, shipped), A43 (uploaded-template structural guarantee, shipped T1/T2) and A43-C (conversational content-ops, C1+C3 in flight). The owner's method has 13 stages; several map to shipped pieces and the row is EXTENSION not greenfield - scope must trace each. THE LEVERAGE (docs/loop/leverage.md), which is the whole point: a chat re-types the standing rules every session, drifts off the mental model by slide 20, and cannot guarantee/remember/version anything. The app can. (1) STANDING RULES AS A VERSIONED DECK STANDARD: the owner's stage 5/7/9 rules (five-second dominant claim, Title Case, professional title slide with only the title, consistent cards/arrows/code formatting, projection contrast, separate question/answer slides so a poll cannot spoil itself) become reusable enforced config applied by the generator AND checked by the review passes with a violations receipt - not re-prompted. (2) PINNED FRAME AS FIRST-CLASS STATE: stage 3 one mental model (example Find->Connect->Request->Build) and stage 4 one running example, stored as objects threaded into every slide prompt and validated for consistency so drift is a caught error. (3) ADVERSARIAL PASSES AS NAMED AUTOMATED GATES: stages 11/12/13 (info-flow checklist: main point obvious, where to look first, detail too early, diagram implies something false, vocabulary without a model, standalone-reconstructable; visual checklist: arrow direction, request/response shown as one message, HTML appearing to make requests, query-string vs fragment; polish linter: dedupe labels, fix overlaps, standardize spacing, normalize activity badges, consistent phase/nav) become validators producing diffs/receipts, reusing PRES-1 per-artifact critique + A43-C ops. (4) STRUCTURAL TEMPLATE CONFORMANCE (A43). (5) REUSE ACROSS A TERM: same standard+template+frame applied to next week with no re-instruction, with provenance (which standard/template version produced a deck). PIPELINE STAGES, each emitting an EDITABLE intermediate and independently REGENERABLE-with-context (the re-enter-at-stage-6 ability a chat lacks): source intake [shipped] -> lesson spec (objectives/activities/terms/sequence) [partial] -> Frame (mental model + running example) [NEW] -> outline (intuition->analogy->term->mechanism->example->application) [shipped] -> activities (3 candidates, pick 1, weave throughout) [partial] -> slide plan (per-slide dominant claim + guaranteed prediction/answer PAIRING) [NEW] -> visual specs (structured explanatory diagrams) [partial, graphic model exists] -> render into uploaded template [shipped A43] -> review (the two checklists -> findings -> regenerate) [partial] -> deterministic polish linter [NEW]. GRANULAR CONTROL: every intermediate editable and honored downstream (edit the Frame -> only affected slides regenerate); A43-C content-ops give per-slide control that cannot break template/layout; review findings itemized and opt-in (not a black-box improved deck); prediction/answer becomes a structural invariant so the app cannot spoil its own poll. GENUINELY NEW vs shipped: the Deck Standard config+enforcement, the pinned Frame, the slide-plan stage with prediction/answer pairing, the named adversarial checklists as gates, the polish linter. NOTHING RENDERS UNDER VITEST here so appearance/interaction/download stay owner-verification; the standard-enforcement checks, the Frame-consistency validation, the checklist gates and the polish linter are pure/mocked-callLlm testable. Scope must decide sequencing against PRES-1/A43/A43-C already in flight and must not rebuild what ships. WHAT THE OWNER ASKED FOR HAS TWO HALVES AND THEY ARE NOT THE SAME SIZE. (1) PLACEMENT - trends renders alongside each grading tool instead of only in Drafted Grades. (2) TRIGGER - "when i run a tool over a series of assignments, THAT RUN should generate trends", i.e. trends become an OUTPUT OF A RUN rather than a thing the instructor navigates to afterwards. Half (2) is the substantive one: it needs the run to carry enough identity to group its results into a series, and the scope must establish whether each grading tool's run already has that or whether it is being invented. A CONSTRAINT FROM THIS REPO'S OWN HISTORY, and it is the one to watch: recording-split.structure.test.ts holds HARDCODED COUNTS for that strip - exactly 12 sub-tab entries, exactly 11 role=tabpanel occurrences, and panelTargets.size === 11 - and the card records that adding a sub-tab breaks three of those and passes the fourth FALSELY. If the answer is a panel WITHIN each existing grading tool, they may not move at all - which is an argument for the second shape. SCOPED 2026-09-20 to docs/a16-scope.md, and THE OWNER ANSWERED TWO FORKS THE SAME DAY. OWNER ANSWER 1 - "A SERIES" MEANS ONE RUN OVER MANY STUDENTS' SUBMISSIONS FOR THE SAME ASSIGNMENT, not trends spanning several assignments. That is the smaller path and it is available today: every grading tool already does exactly that, so trends can attach to a run without inventing a multi-assignment run object. This matters because the scoping measured that NO INTERACTIVE GRADING TOOL RUNS OVER A SERIES OF ASSIGNMENTS AT ALL - GradingRunEntry is ONE assignment (types.ts:320-338), and the only GradingRunEntry[] producers are the workflow grade step (steps.grading-run.ts:425,496) and GradingDraftPayload.runs (grading-drafts.ts:33), whose consumer is the Drafted Grades site trends ALREADY occupies. Cross-assignment accumulation is therefore a separate, larger item and is NOT in A16. OWNER ANSWER 2 - FIX THE RECORDING TOOL'S DATA GAP INSIDE A16. Mounting today's panel there would render "Trends (0)" FOREVER. Snapshot grading is unaffected (snapshot-row.ts:122-124). The only production caller of gradeCapturedSubmissionsAction is GradingRecordingPanel.tsx:593, inside handleGradeAll, which builds its submissions list from gradingRows.rawRows - the whole table - on every press, wired to one button. A31 established the same shape on the five engine entry points: a re-run grades the same prefix, so the bound overflow has no remedy today. THE PRECEDENT ALREADY EXISTS ON A SIBLING SURFACE: Repo Grades has a per-cell Grade button, and A30 shipped its outcome classification through the same pure function the bulk path uses - so the shape to copy is known and the classifier to reuse is named. | Owner, 2026-09-23, answering the fork A34 raised | WHY THIS IS THE FIRST FEATURE OF ITS FAMILY RATHER THAN A COPY EDIT: two shipped fixes this week could only DELETE a promised action, because the action did not exist. A per-row Grade turns both of those sentences back into something true - the bound overflow gets a remedy, a single failed row can be retried without paying for the whole table again, and a rubric edit can be tested on one submission. THE OWNER CHOSE SINGLE ROW OVER A SUBSET, and the reasoning is recorded so a later round does not relitigate it: single row covers every case named above at the cost of one button per row, while a subset adds selection state, a count and a grade-N-selected flow, and only pays off when the instructor wants a specific ten of forty. Subset is a legitimate later row, not this one. WHAT THE SCOPE MUST SETTLE, and must not default silently. (1) THE BOUND: does a single-row grade count against the same submission limit, and what happens when the table is already past it? The limit is read per run from the environment, so a one-row run is trivially under it - say whether that is intended or an accident to guard. (2) THE RUBRIC: the bulk path resolves one rubric for the whole run and reuses it; a single-row grade must not silently resolve a DIFFERENT rubric than the row beside it was graded against, which would make two rows on one screen incomparable. (3) COST DISCLOSURE: one row is one model call, and the instructor should be able to tell what a press will spend. (4) THE COPY THAT WAS DELETED: A31 and A34 now say what happened and offer nothing, because nothing was available. If this ships, those sentences may offer the action - but only where it is genuinely reachable, and the rule from A31 ruling 1 binds: a sentence may assert only what holds on every caller and every reachable state. (5) CONCURRENCY: A26 shipped a lock because two clicks during a rubric fetch double-graded every repo. A per-row button multiplies the click surface, so say whether the same lock covers it or whether per-row presses may interleave. NOTHING RENDERS UNDER VITEST HERE, so the button appearing and its disabled state are reading claims - route them to owner verification rather than implying coverage. | Grading runs: bounds, failures, not-attempted rows, class-trends signal | N13b | verification | Make the class-trends draft worth sending: count and name a SUBSET of students who missed the same area, not just an all-or-nothing whole-class verdict. Owner, 2026-09-15. Split out of N13 because it is a feature, while N13a is a bounding change. | src/lib/grade/class-trends.ts, src/lib/grade/class-trends-draft.ts, src/app/components/drafted-grades/ClassTrendsStudentListPanel.tsx | - | - | Direct read of src/lib/grade/class-trends.ts:124-196, src/lib/gemini.ts:25,124-125 and src/lib/grade/engine.ts:126, 2026-09-15 | N13, owner decision 2, 2026-09-15 | OWNER, VERBATIM: "make the draft more worthwhile. even in a class of 9 where 4 students miss the same area of the homework in a way that doesn't meet the current criteria for drafting an announcement, it's still worth sending out an announcement or contacting those 4 students individually". WHY THE CURRENT DESIGN CANNOT SAY THAT, measured: classifyDirection (src/lib/grade/class-trends.ts:178-196) reads "consistently" literally - EVERY percent value in an area must be >= HIGH_PERCENT_THRESHOLD or <= LOW_PERCENT_THRESHOLD - so 4 of 9 is neither high nor low and renders no clause at all. On any realistically mixed class the modal output is the opening sentence and nothing else, which is what made the original N13 decision 2 a question about whether the feature was worth sending at all. The owner's answer is that it is, once it can see a subset. THREE PARTS. (a) A SUBSET/CLUSTER classification beside the existing direction, with a DERIVED rather than arbitrary threshold - and an explicit answer for small classes, where a fraction is loud but a count is not (3 of 4? 2 of 3?). (b) PER-STUDENT ATTRIBUTION, which layer A does not have: AreaTrend (class-trends.ts:124-150) carries percentValues as an ANONYMOUS number[], so even once a subset is counted the feature cannot NAME the students the owner wants to contact. Identity exists upstream (GradeResult carries student, a Canvas run carries userId); the design must say exactly where attribution enters. (c) TWO OUTPUTS, because the owner named two actions - an announcement to the class and a per-student list. A CLASS ANNOUNCEMENT NAMING STUDENTS IS A PRIVACY DEFECT: the announcement must describe the area without naming anyone while the per-student list stays instructor-facing, and that distinction needs an oracle that FAILS if a student name can reach the class-addressed text. HARD CONSTRAINT INHERITED FROM N11: prose generation stays WITHDRAWN. REGRESSION 423 records that layer C deliberately does not call a model, because every claim it makes was already typed by layers A and B, and reintroducing generation reopens the provenance problem the two-renderer construction closes by type. The fix is MORE COUNTED SIGNAL IN LAYER A. Anyone who concludes generation is unavoidable must argue it explicitly, never smuggle it in. Both outputs are surfaces, so per this repo's own rule the surface is a layer - do not ship a library with no way to reach it. ClassTrendsPanel.tsx and classTrends.wiring.test.ts are the existing surface. OWNER SET THE THRESHOLD 2026-09-15, so it is no longer something to derive. Verbatim: "honestly, i want any trend where three or more students missed points on the same sections called out". THREE OR MORE STUDENTS, AN ABSOLUTE COUNT, not a fraction of the class - so 3 of 4 and 3 of 30 both qualify, and the small-class question this row used to pose is answered by not asking it. AND THE PART THAT CHANGES MORE THAN THE NUMBER: "MISSED POINTS" IS ANY DEDUCTION, not the existing low-score rule. The shipped design only ever notices a student at or below LOW_PERCENT_THRESHOLD (60), so a student who scored 8/10 on an area is invisible to it today. Under the owner's rule that student HAS missed points and counts toward the three. That is a much broader signal than classifyDirection's and it is the real reason the current feature renders nothing useful: it was looking for failure, and the owner wants to see FRICTION. The scoping must therefore establish, and not default: what counts as a deduction when a score is unparseable or carries no scale (the module already refuses to invent a denominator - requirement 4, class-trends.ts:182-187 - and that refusal must not silently become a not-missed), and whether a trivial deduction (19.5/20) should count, since under a literal reading it does. Recommend, do not decide silently. The existing high/low direction is NOT replaced by this - it is a separate, stricter signal that can stay; the subset call is additive. ORCHESTRATOR RULING 2026-09-15, settling the one design decision the check (round 1, NOT READY, 3 blockers) said could not be relocated to an implementer because the implementer's safe-looking default is the SILENT one. THE GATE: class-trends-draft.ts:179-182 filters EVERY clause through areaFullyCovered, which requires area.resultsWithArea === report.totalResults. The design never said whether the new subset clause passes that filter, and both branches are defective as written. RULING: THE SUBSET CLAUSE IS NOT GATED ON FULL COVERAGE. The owner's rule is an absolute count - three or more students missed points on the same section - and that fact is TRUE whether or not every other submission was gradeable. Suppressing it because one submission failed to grade is precisely the silence the owner asked us to fix. THE DISCLOSURE THAT MAKES IT HONEST, and it is not optional, because the existing gate exists for a real reason (the header at :86-93 warns that a clause backed by 5 of 30 with nothing disclosing the gap is the hazard): the subset clause must state its OWN denominator - "4 of the 8 submissions that included this section" - rather than inheriting the opening line's totalResults. Tie every rendered clause's basis to the number it was actually computed from. A LIVE DEFECT THE CHECK FOUND ON THE WAY, which is a second and independent cause of the owner's "renders nothing" report and is NOT fixed by the subset signal alone: because the gate compares against totalResults, ONE FAILED SUBMISSION IN A RUN OF FIVE SWITCHES THE WHOLE DRAFT OFF, today, at shipped defaults. Executed, not reasoned: 5 graded with 4 low on Testing plus 1 grading-failure row gives coverage 4/5 and status=empty, while the same five with no failure row gives status=ok. N13a makes this ROUTINE rather than exceptional, because a deadline-stopped run produces failure rows by design. The same ruling covers it - clauses disclose their own basis instead of being suppressed - and the fix lands in class-trends-draft.ts, which is already in both owns lists. RES-W3-4, OWED HERE BY A16 WAVE 3 (docs/a16-wave3-scope.md): on Repo Grades, every trends cohort result carries student = the repo's FULL NAME (github-repos.ts:618-619, label or digest.fullName), not the bound roster student - invisible today because ClassTrendsPanel never reads .student and the insight route anonymises, but N13b's per-student attribution would name repos, not students, on that surface. Owner: the N13b scoping seat. Instrument: grep -n student: src/app/actions/github-repos.ts, plus an N13b criterion stating which name a Repo Grades attribution shows (the repo, or the binding's roster student - the binding lives in RepoGradeRow.binding, repoGradesRows.ts). Step: N13b's scoping pass, which already follows A16. | One upload per student: boundary, auto-grade, rubric ingest | A44 | verification | Read per-student FOLDERS in a submissions zip as a first-class identity signal. Split out of A41 by RULING 69 on 2026-09-27, because A41 ships a REFUSAL and this is a parser change - a different kind of work with a different risk. THE MEASURED REASON THIS ROW EXISTS: A41 refusal is conservative by construction, and the A41 test notes quantified the cost. A folder zip where each student submits ONE file is never refused, 0 of 20,000 generated sets. But a folder zip where a student submits TWO generically-named files - Homework Final and Homework Draft inside the same student folder - is refused 14.5% of the time WITH NOTHING WRONG, 2901 of 19,975, because at that point the data carries no identity signal the fallback can use. CORRECTED 2026-09-27 BY THE A44 CHECK (docs/a44-check.md B1) - THE ERROR IS MINE. I wrote that reading the folder name is the signal that reduces that 14.5%. IT CANNOT. A conservative refusal is BY DEFINITION a collision with no cross-student harm, so every colliding file belongs to ONE student, so in a per-student-folder zip they all sit in the SAME folder, so the folder component of a compound key is CONSTANT across them - the folder cannot discriminate a folder from itself. The argument needs no measurement, and the measurement agrees: against A41 own published generator the rate RISES from 14.6% to 25.1%, and afterwards 100% of remaining refusals on that shape are false ones. It reproduces on both of A41 published conservative cases. WHAT THIS ROW ACTUALLY BUYS, and it matters more than the number I put on it: the real harm goes to ZERO on the folder shape, 12177 of 12177 blended sets. A wrong grade nobody sees, closed completely. So the row is worth building and MY STATED REASON FOR IT WAS WRONG. RULING 85 therefore widens A44 to own A41 REFUSAL DECISION as well as the parser, because making the refusal folder-aware is the only thing that makes the rate fall - and it is cheapest now, since A41 is still doc-only with no landed predicate to renegotiate. That also closes a gap the check found under EVERY split: a wave writing A41 predicate is required and was unplanned in both rows. A41 frozen fixture F6 is re-frozen from REFUSE to ALLOW as a deliberate recorded change. THE RISK THAT MAKES THIS ITS OWN ROW RATHER THAN AN A41 WAVE: changing identity resolution changes the DISPLAY STRING, and stored instructor edits are keyed on the display string in three places in the grading-results helpers - so a naive change silently ORPHANS saved edits. It is not trivially revertible, unlike the refusal. It also must not break the pinned behaviour where one student two resubmissions correctly merge into ONE row, which is required, not a bug. | - | - | - | the A41 test notes published generator, re-run with folder shape and ground truth: the conservative refusal rate for a two-generic-file student folder must fall from the measured 14.5% (2901 of 19,975), and zero saved instructor edits may be orphaned - proven by an assertion that executes the display-string keying in the grading-results helpers, not by reading the source | A41 RULING 69, and the A41 test notes fork answered (2) | Filed by me 2026-09-27 under RULING 69, and it is a promise kept rather than a nicety: I ruled A41 ships a refusal and said the folder reader would be filed separately, so this row is that filing. Do not treat it as optional follow-up - without it the 14.5% conservative-refusal rate is permanent AND the reason to investigate it is gone, which is the shape the A41 test author warned about when recommending this be filed rather than merely recorded. FACTS ALREADY ESTABLISHED, so a scope does not re-derive them: a folder inside the top-level zip is NOT a zip crossing, and the base-name helper strips the directory BEFORE the convention regex runs, which is why the folder name is invisible today - the extraction file own comment says so. The collapse reproduces byte-identical on three students each submitting essay.docx in their own folder: one row, student essay, mergedFileCount 3, three students text concatenated into one model call and one grade. A SCOPE MUST ALSO HANDLE THE BOUNDARY A41 FOUND: the refusal does NOT close every cross-student collapse, only the fallback-reached ones, and at least one genuine cross-student collapse is currently PINNED AS CURRENT BEHAVIOUR by an existing fixture that A41 own row excludes from scope - so decide deliberately whether this row closes that too, and do not let a green suite decide it by accident. FINALLY, the one instrument that must not be trusted here: the guard asserting the student set is unique CANNOT fail, because the map is keyed on the lowercased display string, so uniqueness is true by construction. It is however LOAD-BEARING on key-to-display injectivity, which stored edits depend on, so it must be repaired rather than retired. WAVE 1 SHIPPED 2026-09-27 AT 580e505: the identity key folds the container-relative path and the display disambiguates terminally. Three students each submitting essay.docx in their own folder no longer collapse into one graded row. WAVE 2, the refusal, MUST NOT RUN CONCURRENTLY and is not started. THE ENCODING IS TOTAL AT ALL SIX RETURN SITES AND THAT WAS PROVEN BY SABOTAGE, not by reading the diff: leaving the four non-folding sites raw goes RED on nine tests including a forgery sweep at 36,077 instances. A partial encoding is FORGEABLE - a flat file whose name contains the prefix pattern produces a key identical to another student compound key - and there are THREE such forgeries, the dangerous one through the convention branch with the display list BYTE-IDENTICAL, invisible to the refusal on every branch. THE TERMINAL PASS SEARCHES FOR THE FIRST UNCLAIMED LABEL rather than counting, because a counted suffix can collide with another row RAW label when a leading space defeats an anchored regex. That mutation survives 105 of 107 tests and is caught by exactly the one fixture built for it. It walks in IDENTITY-KEY order, not insertion order: both give distinct labels so the invariant passes either way, but the two orders assign labels DIFFERENTLY and under RULING 93 the display IS the storage label. TWO FIGURES MATCHED THE TEST NOTES PREDICTIONS EXACTLY - 156 on one shape for a dropped terminal pass, and the counting mutation surviving everything but one fixture. It also hit the false-positive trap the test notes warned about, on the same shape, and REBUILT THE INSTRUMENT to compare suffixes rather than whole labels instead of accepting a convenient red. IT REBUILT THE BEFORE-PARTITION FROM LIVE CODE rather than reimplementing it: calling the real unmodified parser with the path stripped to its basename makes the fold no-op, reproducing pre-change behaviour with ZERO duplicate algorithm - the divergence hazard this repo has been bitten by. A FINDING FROM THE SABOTAGE CYCLE ITSELF: the backup taken BEFORE any mutation already had CRLF, one per line, while HEAD is pure LF - so an EDIT-TOOL CALL introduced it, not the cp restore, which is the opposite of what this repo had recorded. Verified by me at CR=0 and NUL=0. TWO PRE-EXISTING FAILURES ON MAIN, VERIFIED NOT THIS WAVE: the client-boundary transitive test fails with 48 modules reaching canvas-core through the grade barrel from a browser bundle, and the build fails at COMPILE rather than at the documented env-dependent prerender tail. I checked the attribution myself - this wave diff changes NO import or export line and does not touch the barrel the violation names - so it cannot have created that edge. Diagnosis dispatched separately, because until the build is green it cannot confirm ANY wave is clean, and that gate is the only instrument here for server-export shapes, client-bundle boundaries and chunking. Wave 1 own gates: 284 tests across eight paths at exit 0, tsc clean, lint exit 0 with no new warning in the write set. One file to watch: the new invariants test is at 901 of 1000. WAVE 2 SHIPPED 2026-09-27 AT e53979c - the collision refusal, so A44 IS COMPLETE across both waves. A zip whose parse would blend distinct students into one graded row now REFUSES with a counted, named reason, placed inside the extraction entry and in the engine BEFORE the convention inference, so a refused zip spends ZERO of its own model calls - and not in the two homes the plan ruled out by measurement. THREE EXPORTS, NOT TWO: the conditional resolved in the direction nobody could predict, because the copy DOES name a folder, so the directory helper has a real caller. The decoder recovers a group arity from the identity key it already holds rather than re-deriving the folder from the raw path, and the new discriminator is set at all six return sites - the field that provably cannot come from the key, since a convention match and a directory-less fallback both decode to arity one. The copy is a default-less exhaustive switch over a discriminated union, so a future refusal variant with no case fails TSC rather than vitest. THE SECOND SABOTAGE SHOWS THE EXACT HARM rather than an abstraction: discarding the refusal instead of throwing produces a graded run containing TWO STUDENTS BLENDED INTO ONE ROW WITH ONE GRADE, named by the wiring test. TWO OPEN OBLIGATIONS, both stated by the implementer rather than found later. FIRST AND MORE IMPORTANT: RULING 89 EXACT UNSOUND INTEGER IS NOT PINNED. The generator that landed under RULING 97 is the implementer own re-derivation of the published spec, not verified byte-for-byte against the test notes frozen draw order, so the suite asserts PROPERTIES - zero unsound-and-allowed on the sound shapes, never refuses on the allow-required shapes - instead of the literal. Properties do NOT deliver what that pin was for, which was that the number can only go DOWN and any change raising it fails loudly. Filed as its own row rather than left here, because residuals in this project were silently dropped twice in one day. SECOND: the wave added eight lines above a cited line in the engine, so a bare-basename citation in single-file-entry.ts has shifted and was not re-pinned - the one in-src re-pin the plan named as the implementer obligation. AND IT SETTLED THE CRLF STORY I HAD RECORDED WRONGLY TWICE. I blamed a cp restore, then corrected that to blame the Edit tool. NEITHER IS TRUE: core.autocrlf is true here and CLEAN UNTOUCHED FILES CARRY CR - types.ts 432, rubric.ts 443, package.json 52, while canvas-core.ts has zero - so working-tree CRLF is CHECKOUT behaviour varying by file. And the consequence is benign, which I verified: the HEAD blob is CR=0 with 432 LF, and the edited file diff is 11 added and 0 removed with no line-ending churn. THERE WAS NEVER A CORRUPTION and I sent several agents chasing one. What survives is narrower - grep -c for a carriage return is still broken, and the thing worth checking is the INDEX rather than the working tree. Verified by me: 228 tests across eleven paths at exit 0, tsc clean, Compiled successfully, and the app-wide client-boundary guard green, which this wave needed because it adds imports. THE LAST OPEN OBLIGATION IS CLOSED AT 166ce6c: RULING 89 integer is now a FROZEN LITERAL alongside the properties, not instead of them. AND THE ANSWER WAS NOT WHAT I EXPECTED - I assumed a re-derived generator would diverge from the published spec and need reconciling. Read field by field - PRNG, seeding, draw order, all seventeen shape formulas, the forgery-shape tracking, every constant - IT DOES NOT DIVERGE AT ALL. So the previous pass caution was warranted and its conclusion was wrong in the harmless direction: it declined to pin numbers it had not confirmed, and they were right. The apparent discrepancy in an earlier comparison was a DIFFERENT SAMPLE SIZE, not a different generator. The integers reconcile exactly at the generator own frozen size and seed: 2254 of 19936 and 287 of 19990. THE SABOTAGE PROVES THE PIN FAILS ON AN INCREASE, which is the entire point of RULING 89 and the one thing a property assertion cannot do: widening the amnesty takes the counts to 2780 and 349 and the pin goes red naming both received values, where a not-zero property passes at either number. And the received values are themselves a check on the sabotage - 2780 and 349 are exactly the ground-truth blend totals for those two shapes, so the mutation removed the discriminating condition ENTIRELY rather than nudging it, and the pin caught it at the first integer that moved. Split out of A44 by RULING 89 on 2026-09-27, because the folder-aware refusal has a MEASURED soundness hole that no rule reading only the file paths can close. The hole: on a zip that has per-student folders AND one shared folder, the run-level gate opens, the colliding group is folder-homogeneous, amnesty from refusal is granted, and a real cross-student blend is SILENTLY ALLOWED - 2159 sets, 79.1% of that shape harm. The one further attempt the iteration cap allows was made and it changed KIND rather than strength (amnesty requires two or more distinct folders each carrying a collision): that variant is SOUND, zero unsound, and takes the conservative false-refusal rate to 24.0%, worse than the 14.5% baseline A44 exists to improve. So one rule is unsound on one shape and the other fails the row own condition, NEITHER DOES BOTH, and cap 1 forbids a third attempt on that class. THE WAY OUT IS A DIFFERENT KIND OF EVIDENCE: the app already holds a roster, studentRepos usernames and a Canvas user list, and THE ZIP PATH READS NONE OF THEM. Verbatim: "each student will have their own zip". It was given as the answer to a re-run identity question on bound-stopped grading runs - the zip path has no durable per-student id, so a stopped run could not name what to re-run. One upload per student dissolves that: the upload is the identity. TWO CONSEQUENCES FOR THIS ITEM. (1) The per-upload-is-one-student mode is not only a convenience for the owner's grading workflow, it is what gives every downstream feature a re-run key on the zip path - so the checkbox this row describes carries more weight than its size suggests, and A12's not-attempted rows depend on it for that path. (a) REPEATED SINGLE UPLOADS that append rows: 2 interactions per student, which by the census's crossover rule NEVER beats the chat baseline and is strictly worse than the zip path sitting beside it. No instructor-typed name field and no roster-match utility exist in the tree, verified with canaries, so (a) also needs an identity source built. COST OF BEING WRONG: choosing (a) builds a flow that is measurably slower than what the app already has, plus an identity mechanism to make it safe. | Ask AI modal: persistence and chip copy | A3 | owner | Persist AskAiModal answers so a later session reads them back. The ideation pass ranked this FIRST; the check demoted it, because as scoped it is not the corpus class - it asserted the WRITE and left the read-back unguarded, and docs/loop/leverage.md:61 strikes generic persistence except where a record is "genuinely read back later, not for merely writing a row". Delete the read-back and the proposed test stays green. | - | - | - | L7 ideation check, 2026-09-15: four required changes, two of which contradict the proposal's own "no schema change" headline | owner request, 2026-09-15 | UNSCOPED because two of the four changes are design decisions rather than edits. (1) THE REUSE CONTAMINATES A SHIPPED FEATURE, and this is the serious one: course-intel.ts:113 calls listAllCourseIntelAnswers(supabase, user.id) with NO course filter and NO feature filter, and :132 calls clearAllCourseIntelAnswers on the same scope. So writing AskAiModal answers into course_intel_answers makes them appear in the Course Intel tab history list and puts them under that tab Clear history button - every gate green, another feature user-facing list quietly filling with unrelated rows. The honest fix is a source discriminator column or a separate table, which is the schema change the proposal said it avoided. (2) THE ROW SHAPE DOES NOT FIT: AppendCourseIntelAnswerCommon (history.ts:415-431) requires citedStudents, omissions, assembledAt and tier: AssemblyTier, and AssemblyTier is signals or signals+text (types.ts:385) - an AskAiModal answer is neither, so the build either writes a false tier or widens a union across three files. (3) The read-back must move into a server action to be assertable at all, since nothing renders here. (4) Persisting forces a userId and therefore an auth guard onto askAboutCourseAction, which is PINNED as deliberately unguarded at action-guard-coverage.test.ts:248 in a list of 28 - a correction to my own "about twenty", measured by awk over the block plus grep -c. That test is exact-set equality, so the pin comes out in the same commit; the check rules this the CHEAP part. Also unflagged by the ideation pass: llm-content.ts imports no Supabase at all today, so this puts a service-role DB dependency on the path of a user-facing modal that currently needs none, and an insert that throws must not cost the instructor the answer they just waited for. | TWO NEW OWNER ITEMS 2026-09-23, both BEFORE the migration lands, since migrations auto-apply on push here. (1) PRIVACY, and the design created it: fixing the blocker required storing the model's ANSWER, and an answer can quote roster names back, because the roster is sent verbatim (course-facts.ts:109-110). So this design digests the roster precisely in order to store NO NAME, and then stores an answer that may contain names anyway. There is no instrument for it in this checkout - no database, no model call - and the scope says so rather than inventing one. Options: store the answer as designed and accept it; store only a digest of the answer, which costs the panel its usefulness; or redact before storing, which needs a detector nothing here can test. MY RECOMMENDATION: store it, because the answer is the instructor's own material about their own course on their own account, and the roster it could quote is data they supplied - but this is yours, not mine, because it is the one place the feature writes student names to a durable store. (2) On a facts-shape version mismatch, refuse all comparison, or compare the labels present in both and report the change separately? RECOMMENDATION: compare what is comparable - refusing everything turns the feature dark exactly when an instructor most wants it, after something changed. Both are shaped so either answer ends the activity. ANSWERED 2026-09-23: the owner took the recommendation - REDESIGN AROUND PROVENANCE, dropping recall as the goal, with the retrieval interaction part of the deliverable rather than a follow-up. See docs/owner-decisions-2026-09-23.md DECISION 11. The existing scope was written against the old premise and must be re-scoped from this one. OPEN, AND docs/loop/leverage.md FORBIDS AN AGENT TO DEFAULT IT. Re-measured 2026-09-23: the 2026-09-15 check was right and is still right on today's tree, so this row's premise does not survive. Nothing persists an Ask AI answer - AskAiModal.tsx:44-45 holds it in useState, CoursesTab.tsx:448 unmounts the modal on close and destroys it, and the action at llm-content.ts:855-921 documents itself as deliberately free of the storage row shape. TWO THINGS KILL THE ROW AS WRITTEN. First, the value it claimed is gone: a39-research.md section 5 established that a chat CAN persist across sessions via Projects, so remembering an answer is not an advantage. What survives is narrower - PROVENANCE, which facts produced which answer - and that is a different feature from the one this row proposes. Second, the one existing precedent for reading an answer back (CourseIntelHistory.tsx) costs a tab switch plus an expand click plus an unscoped scan, which is MORE expensive than re-asking - the same failure shape measured for the rubric this week. A stored answer that costs more to find than to re-ask is worse than not storing it. THE FORK IS YOURS: (a) REDESIGN around provenance - store which facts produced an answer and show that, dropping recall as the goal; (b) ACCEPT the cost and build recall anyway, with a retrieval interaction cheaper than re-asking specified FIRST, not as a follow-up; or (c) REJECT the row and delete it. COST OF BEING WRONG: (a) is a different and probably smaller feature than what was filed and is the one I recommend; (b) is the expensive branch and it must beat re-asking on clicks or it ships a surface nobody uses; (c) is the only one-way door, though the row is recoverable from git. WHAT IS NOT BLOCKED: nothing else on that surface, and the A4 question about the same modal's chips is independent of this one. |
