Reading B - a midweek draft reflecting what has actually happened since the first announcement - is OUT OF SCOPE and must not be built or implied. The tool reads no gradebook, counts no submissions and has no memory of the earlier announcement, so any copy that SOUNDS like the midweek draft knows who is behind would be false confidence. The scope must state that limit explicitly and check no generated copy claims knowledge of student progress. The original fork is kept below because the rejected reading is what the guard exists to prevent. THE SCOPING QUESTION IS WHAT BEGINNING-OF-WEEK AND MIDWEEK ACTUALLY MEAN, and it must be settled before any code, because the two readings build different things. READING A - TWO TONES OVER THE SAME MATERIAL: one capture session produces a looking-ahead announcement (here is what is coming this week, what to start) and a checking-in one (you should be partway through, here is the common sticking point). Same captured pages, two prompts, two drafts. READING B - TWO TIMINGS: the midweek draft is meant to reflect what has happened SINCE the beginning-of-week one went out, which this tool cannot know - it has no gradebook read, no submission counts and no memory of the earlier announcement. READING A IS BUILDABLE TODAY AND READING B IS NOT, so the working assumption is A unless the owner says otherwise. If the owner means B, the honest version is still A plus an explicit disclosure that the midweek draft is written blind to actual student progress - a draft that SOUNDS like it knows who is behind while knowing nothing is the exact false-confidence failure this backlog already carries rows about. THE LIKELY SHAPE, to be confirmed by scoping rather than assumed: this is a per-slot ANNOUNCEMENT KIND that is ORTHOGONAL to the existing TemplateChoice - kind says what the announcement is FOR, choice says what FORMAT it copies - and conflating them would be wrong, because an instructor can reasonably want a midweek announcement in a saved exemplar format. Adding a fifth member to TemplateChoice is therefore the wrong move; a separate field is the right one. WATCH THE FROZEN INVARIANT at announcement-draft-slots.ts:226-235: the dropdown must always contain an option matching the slot state, and that rule exists because it was once violated. A second dimension multiplies the option set, so whatever enforces that invariant must be re-derived, not assumed to carry over. FOUR THINGS THE SCOPE MUST SETTLE: (1) the two readings above; (2) whether kind lives on the slot, the draft, or both - Drafted.builtFrom is REQUIRED precisely because an optional field let a caller omit it with every gate green, and the same argument applies here; (3) whether the two kinds share buildWalkthroughAnnouncementPrompt with a parameter or get their own module, with walkthrough-script-prompt.ts as the precedent for splitting - the default-arm-byte-identical technique this repo has used before is the safe way to add a parameter to a live prompt; (4) the panel's 21 lines of headroom, which almost certainly forces an extraction into the same chunk. NO COMPONENT IS RENDERED BY ANY TEST HERE, so a new control cannot be verified by the suite - the reachability of whatever gets built must be traced from the control to the code by hand. RECLASSIFIED TO verification because the only thing left is the item this row already named as the one that matters and the one this checkout cannot discharge: WHETHER A REAL BROWSER ALLOWS THE DOWNLOAD. A programmatic download fired from recorder.onstop is not adjacent to a user gesture, and the failure mode is NO FILE AND NO ERROR. Owner, in a real browser, on both surfaces: record, stop, and check that a file actually arrives. SCOPED to docs/a20-scope.md; round 2 at 886b32d, round-2 check in flight. ITS RESIDUALS ARE RECORDED HERE because DEV_LOOP step 0 says a residual that is not in the backlog does not exist, and the scoping seat could not reach this file (its write scope was its own artifact). Live in docs/a20-scope.md: residuals R1-R5, two batched owner questions in section 13, and the section 8 OWNER-VERIFICATION item. THE OWNER-VERIFICATION ITEM IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS AND IT CANNOT BE DISCHARGED IN THIS CHECKOUT: a browser may REFUSE a programmatic download that is not adjacent to a user gesture, and recorder.onstop is an event callback, not a click. The failure is NO FILE AND NO ERROR. Nothing here can observe it - vitest is node-env and renders no component - so a real browser on both surfaces is the only instrument, and a green suite must never be read as covering it. THREE ORCHESTRATOR RULINGS ARE ALREADY BAKED IN and must not be re-argued: the auto-download uses the house triggerFileDownload helper (both in-scope files already import it, and one carries a header rule saying never to hand-roll the alternative); ONE extension rule serves BOTH the auto-download and the manual link, because letting them diverge would put two names on one recording with one of them unopenable; and the post-stop UI MUST DIFFER between auto-download-requested and not, since the pre-existing link renders identically in all three outcomes and therefore cannot disclose a silent failure. THAT LAST ONE CORRECTS THIS ROW: the note below originally nominated the existing review link as the visible fallback, and that was the defect, not the fix. OWNER SETTLED THIS 2026-09-20: JUST THE TWO THAT ALREADY RECORD - discussion replies and message replies. GROUP 2 IS OUT OF SCOPE AND STAYS OUT. Do not add recording to the grading-from-a-recording panel, the legibility probe, the module deck capture or the walkthrough announcement tool, and DO NOT touch ModuleDeckCapturePanel.tsx ACCEPTANCE CRITERION AC13 point 3 (its header at :30 and :126, a recording blob never exists) - that AC stands. A scope or an implementer that widens saveVideo on any group-2 surface has exceeded this row. The group-2 analysis is kept below because it is the reason the boundary exists, not a pending option. THE ASK IS SMALL ON TWO SURFACES AND A DIFFERENT FEATURE ON THE OTHERS, and the scope must not blur them. GROUP 1 - DISCUSSION REPLIES AND MESSAGE REPLIES: these already record on request and already hold the blob. Auto-download here is genuinely just a second persisted control (Download automatically when recording stops) plus a call into the existing anchor-and-click helper at the point where recorder.onstop already fires. This is the part that matches the owner words right after recording exactly. GROUP 2 - GRADING FROM A RECORDING, THE LEGIBILITY PROBE, THE MODULE DECK CAPTURE AND THE WALKTHROUGH ANNOUNCEMENT: these pass saveVideo: false unconditionally, so there is NO VIDEO TO DOWNLOAD. Giving them auto-download means first giving them the ability to record at all, which is a materially larger change with a privacy dimension - these surfaces read gradebooks, student submissions and LMS pages, and the module-deck one has a WRITTEN ACCEPTANCE CRITERION (ModuleDeckCapturePanel.tsx:30 and :126, AC13 point 3) that a recording blob never exists. DO NOT QUIETLY REVERSE A DOCUMENTED AC TO SATISFY A FEATURE REQUEST - if the owner wants recording on those surfaces, that is an explicit decision with its own row, and the AC and its comment must be rewritten to say why, the way A16-2 handled reversing a pinned assertion. RECOMMENDED SHAPE: ship group 1 now as the literal reading of the ask, and put group 2 to the owner as one batched non-gating question naming the privacy trade and the documented AC. THINGS THAT WILL BITE. (1) EVERY NEW CHECKBOX MUST PERSIST ACROSS RELOADS in this repo, and a localStorage-seeded useState initializer NEVER SHOWS on reload without a mount effect - React only warns on the hydration mismatch. Both existing saveVideo controls are the pattern to copy. (2) A BROWSER MAY BLOCK A PROGRAMMATIC DOWNLOAD that is not user-gesture-adjacent, and recorder.onstop is an event callback, not a click. The failure mode is silent - no file, no error - and NOTHING IN THIS SUITE CAN SEE IT, because vitest is node-env and no component is rendered by any test. Whatever ships needs a visible fallback (the existing review link) and an owner verification row, not a green suite. (3) THE OBJECT URL LIFETIME: the current code revokes only on unmount-before-stop. An auto-download adds a second consumer of the same URL, so decide ownership explicitly rather than revoking under the anchor. (4) FILENAME: useTakes.ts:116 already computes a safe name and extension - reuse it rather than minting a second convention, and note the recorder mime type is negotiated (pickRecorderMimeType), so the extension is not always webm. The new test RUNS the hook and asserts an observed marker sequence rather than reading source text, and it was watched failing with the download moved above the state setters while all nine pre-existing source-text tests in the same file stayed green - which measures how little they were covering. It carries a second assertion guarding the INSTRUMENT, because the setter is identified by a magic slot index into the hook's useState order: insert a useState above recordingUrl and the marker moves to the wrong setter, and the failure would arrive disguised as an ordering violation that never happened. That guard was proven by inserting one and watching it name the real cause. WHAT REMAINS OPEN, and why this row stays in verification: a browser's refusal of the automatic download is UNDETECTABLE in JS as written - triggerFileDownload returns void and nothing reads a result - and nothing renders under vitest here, so whether any real browser honours the download, and whether the two new checkboxes rehydrate after a reload, are owner-only checks. docs/a20-owner-check.md carries both, now including the sharing-bar stop path, and REGRESSION 435 carries residuals BL435-2/3/4 routing them. Survey before building - the scope owes a vetted list of what it reuses versus what it adds, and the answer should be mostly reuse. THE REAL DESIGN QUESTION IS WHERE THIS LIVES. A prompt-driven announcement has NO CAPTURE STEP, so it does not belong behind a screen-share flow, and the three existing generators are each welded to their own material source. Weigh that before proposing a new tab. THE LEVERAGE QUESTION IS SHARP HERE AND MUST BE ANSWERED HONESTLY, because generate text from a prompt is the single thing a plain chat with an LLM does best. docs/loop/leverage.md requires naming what this app does that a chat cannot, and the credible answers are all about the SURROUNDINGS rather than the generation: the saved exemplar library means the draft matches THIS instructor's established format; the course context and knowledge base are already resolvable; the result can be posted to the right Canvas course; and the same anti-injection framing, link guard (walkthrough-announcement-link-guard.ts) and completeness-phrase refusal that protect the other generators apply. IF THE SCOPE CANNOT NAME A LEVERAGE CLASS BEYOND drafting text, SAY SO PLAINLY rather than inventing one - an honest no claim is a legitimate result and the card says to record the fired trigger. FOUR THINGS THE SCOPE MUST SETTLE: (1) what the prompt IS - a topic line, a full brief, or bullet points - and whether course/module context is auto-attached or purely typed; (2) whether the output joins the existing draft-slot machinery (getting regenerate, template choice, copy and post for free) or stands alone, with the click cost of each priced; (3) what happens with NO template - the none kind exists but what it produces must be specified, not left to the model; (4) whether the prompt persists across reloads, which in this repo is a REQUIREMENT for every new textbox, and note the mount-effect rule is NARROWER than this row first stated: measured at LectureScriptPanel.tsx:46-54, it applies to a BOOLEAN rendered as a checked control, not to a text input. A21's prompt box is a text input and uses the guarded-initializer idiom already in its own file at announcements-panel.tsx:24-26. An implementer who adds a mount effect here is building what the scope forbids. GUARDS THAT MUST CARRY OVER: instructor-typed text reaching a prompt is an INJECTION SURFACE - the walkthrough path already frames untrusted material with a header it never reformats, and a typed prompt is strictly more attacker-shaped than a captured page. The link guard must apply here too. NOT the forbidden-completeness-phrase refusal - see the correction above; it is on the grading path only, A21 inherits it from nowhere, and it is a phrase denylist, which this backlog measured at 0 of 9 attacks caught. | Announcement composition: prompts, templates and what reaches the model | A29 | verification | Owner asked 2026-09-21, verbatim: for courses that have a live lms connection, i need a feature that sends out a bulk email to the students via the email they have in canvas. | - | - | - | Measured 2026-09-21 before recording, so this row does not assert an absence that is not there. HALF OF IT EXISTS: src/lib/canvas/inbox.ts:384 exports createConversation(courseUrl, recipientUserId, body, subject), which POSTs to Canvas /api/v1/conversations with a course context_code - it sends a Canvas Inbox message to ONE recipient per call. Nothing in the app sends to many at once. THE OTHER HALF DOES NOT EXIST: no file under src/lib/canvas or src/lib/course-intel reads a student email address (grep for .email and email: returned nothing), and package.json carries NO mail-provider dependency (grep for resend, nodemailer, @sendgrid/mail, postmark, mailgun and @aws-sdk/client-ses returned exit 1). | Owner, 2026-09-21, mid-turn | OWNER ANSWERED OC9 2026-09-23, VERBATIM: no courses should exceed 100. THAT IS THE ANSWER THE WHOLE LEDGER EXISTS FOR, AND IT REMOVES MOST OF THE BUILD. Canvas documents that a single POST to recipients[]=course_<id> with group_conversation at its default of false creates INDIVIDUAL PRIVATE CONVERSATIONS with each recipient - one request, no roster exposed, no reply-all - and the over-100 sentence that forced bulk_message and group_conversation to true is the ONLY reason the design fanned out per student at all. Under 100 it does not apply. SO THE PER-STUDENT PUMP, THE DURABLE ATTEMPT LEDGER, THE CLAIM-ONE-ROW CAS, THE STALE SWEEP AND THE RESUME MACHINERY ARE ALL UNNECESSARY - and five of the six blockers the last check found live in exactly that machinery. WHAT SURVIVES AND STILL MATTERS: the live-roster read with its enrollment-state filter and its silent-pagination disclosure; the confirm step showing course, count and the message; the honest limit that Canvas accepted it rather than that an email arrived; and the single-recipient allow-pattern, which still binds because course_<id> is a DIFFERENT recipient form, not a list of ids - the builder must accept exactly one recipient token and that token may be the course form. THE PRICE OF THE SIMPLER SHAPE, stated rather than buried: ONE request means ONE outcome for the whole class, so there is no per-student receipt - the app can report that Canvas accepted the message, and nothing about which students it reached. That is the same honest limit the notification-preference fact already forces. RE-SCOPED ON THIS ANSWER; the earlier architecture stands as the record of what the over-100 case would need if a course ever crosses it. OWNER ANSWERED THE FORK 2026-09-21, VERBATIM: for bulk email: message on canvas and also use canvas built in emailer. THAT IS OPTION (A), and it supersedes the (B-prime) working default. Read together, the two halves are one mechanism: a Canvas conversation IS how Canvas emails - Canvas delivers each Inbox message to the student by email through its own notification system, so sending the message through Canvas uses Canvas built-in emailer. There is no separate Canvas send-an-email endpoint to call in addition. ONE HONEST LIMIT THE UI MUST STATE: whether the email copy actually goes out is decided by EACH STUDENT OWN Canvas notification preferences, which the app cannot see or override - so the app can report that Canvas accepted the message, never that an email arrived. CONSEQUENCES: the Sent Items privacy question is moot (it applied only to the Outlook option); no student address is ever read or handled by the app, which removes the address-source criterion entirely; and the batch- versus per-recipient receipt question changes, because a Canvas conversation send may report per recipient where a BCC could not. The Canvas facts still unverified here decide the build shape and belong to the architecture pass: whether POST /conversations accepts course_<id>_students or a bulk_message / async mode, whether a multi-recipient send creates ONE group conversation (every student sees every other) or one per student - a privacy question in its own right - and the rate limits on fanning out. RULINGS 2026-09-21 in docs/a29-rulings.md, after the revision-1 check returned 4 blockers (2 repeat): the double-send guard is keyed on the COURSE having an unresolved attempt, never on the body, because clearing the body at confirm guarantees a retyped retry differs; the attempt record is DURABLE, written before the first outbound request, since a function killed at 60s keeps no in-memory state; the confirmation is SERVER-ISSUED and consumed once; the client receives only the summariser output so no display can compute its own count; and the capability wall measures the REQUESTS a send makes, not import closure - measured red on day zero, since today tree already reaches both model clients from the course reader. OWNER QUESTION ADDED: the existing Outlook send keeps every BCC address permanently in the instructor own Sent Items. STALE TEXT IN THIS ROW, CORRECTED: the old R3 (a component-only double-send guard) is superseded by the server-issued confirm, and R4 means only the delivery residual. CRITERIA REVISION 1, 2026-09-21: DEFAULT FORK IS NOW (B-PRIME), real email from the instructor connected Outlook - closest to the owner words, and mostly built. Put to the owner; not settled. CORRECTION TO WHAT I TOLD THE OWNER: I said a whole-class email is one click away today. IT IS NOT, FROM A FREE-TEXT COMPOSE. The Send by email path works only on an announcement draft that carries a course tile, and those are created only by the recording take path, the weekly kickoff step and a workflow step - so a direct compose-and-send for a course is something A29 genuinely adds. ALSO MEASURED: that existing whole-class confirm CAN GO STALE - its arm signature covers the draft id, action, body and title but NOT THE ROSTER, so an armed N students in COURSE survives a change to the tile roster. Pre-existing and outside A29 wording; recorded in the criteria section 10. RESIDUAL R4, NEW: under EVERY option, actual delivery to a student inbox is outside what the app can observe - Graph 202 means accepted, Canvas notification email is Canvas business - owner, a test student own inbox, at the owner verification pass. CORRECTED 2026-09-21 BY THE A29 CRITERIA PASS, AND THE ERROR IS MINE: I said the app has no way to send real email. IT DOES. sendMessageDraftByEmailAction (src/app/actions/messaging-outlook.ts:128) sends through the instructor OWN connected Outlook via Microsoft Graph (src/lib/microsoft-graph.ts:92), and for an announcement draft it ALREADY BCCs the course tile whole roster (messaging-outlook.ts:167-178). My grep for a mail-provider package was correct and my conclusion was not - it measured a DEPENDENCY, not a CAPABILITY. And my grep for code reading student emails was scoped to two directories; elsewhere, message drafts carry recipientEmail (message-drafts.ts:31) and the nudge path passes on emails from the gradebook CSV. The narrower claim survives: nothing reads the address CANVAS holds, and the roster readers never request include[]=email. SO THE OWNER HAS THREE OPTIONS, NOT TWO: (A) Canvas Inbox fan-out; (B) real email to the Canvas profile address via a new provider; and (B-prime) real email from the INSTRUCTOR CONNECTED OUTLOOK, which already exists and already reaches whole rosters, costing none of (B) - no provider, no sender domain, no bounce plumbing, and it arrives from the instructor rather than from Canvas. This is the same wrong-directory error the A21 row records, repeated by me the same day. CRITERIA: docs/a29-ac.md, C1-C17 plus L1. THREE FACTS THE CRITERIA MEASURED: the feature gate is canLms (courses-table-helpers.ts:603), and a failed roster read must send NOTHING and must keep not-connected separate from connected-but-unreachable; the roster readers stop SILENTLY at 20 pages of 100, about 2000 students (CANVAS_PAGINATION_PAGE_CAP); and A21 drafter produces MARKDOWN while every existing Inbox drafter asks for plain text, so reusing it as-is would put literal Markdown into students messages. RESIDUALS - CORRECTED, I MISRECORDED THESE ON FIRST FILING and the criteria check caught it: R1-R3 are in section 8, not 7, and they are OWNER BROWSER CHECKS, not API facts. R1: the owner verification items OV1-OV3 - owner, a real browser plus a Canvas sandbox, verify before the push. R2: under (A), Canvas notification delivery by email is entirely outside the app observation - owner, a test student own email inbox, at OV1. R3: the double-send guard, if the architect places it only in the component - owner, a real browser double-clicking confirm, at verify. SEPARATELY, the Canvas and Graph API facts that nothing here can measure (network blocked, no key) belong to a research pass before the build: whether Canvas native POST /conversations already bulk-sends to course_<id>_students, which enrollment states the roster query returns by default, whether a multi-recipient send creates a group conversation, and the Exchange Online sending limits that decide whether a per-student Outlook send can finish inside the 60s Vercel cap. R4: two existing roster readers use DIFFERENT enrollment-state filters (listings.ts:248 and :286 against :318) - owner of whichever build touches roster reading, instrument a fixture with each enrollment state, step that build. OWNER DECISIONS E1-E4 are listed in the criteria. A PRODUCT FORK THE SCOPE MUST NOT SETTLE BY DEFAULT, because the two readings are very different builds. (A) SEND THROUGH CANVAS INBOX: fan createConversation out across the roster. Canvas then delivers each message to the student by email according to THAT STUDENT notification preferences, it arrives from Canvas, and replies land in the instructor Canvas Inbox where the app already reads them. No email provider, no sender domain, no address handling, and it stays inside the LMS record. (B) SEND REAL EMAIL to the address on each Canvas profile: needs the app to READ student emails (Canvas exposes them only under certain permissions, so it may not be available for every institution), plus an email provider the app does not have, a verified sender domain, bounce handling, and it takes student addresses outside the LMS - a privacy decision in its own right. The owner wording, via the email they have in canvas, reads as (B); reading (A) reaches the same inbox for most students at a fraction of the cost. ORCHESTRATOR DEFAULT PENDING THE OWNER: FIRST (A), NOW SUPERSEDED BY (B-PRIME) - see below. THINGS EITHER READING MUST DESIGN FOR: a confirm step before a bulk send, since a mistaken send to a whole class cannot be recalled; rate limits on the Canvas API when fanning out; a per-recipient success and failure report rather than one aggregate; and the LEVERAGE question, since drafting a message is chat-equivalent - the credible advantage is the roster, the course context and the delivery, not the text. RELATED: A21 (announcement from a prompt) already drafts course-addressed text; reuse it rather than building a second composer. ALSO RELATED: an announcement already reaches every student - the scope should say plainly when a bulk message is the right tool and when an announcement is. BUILD STARTED 2026-10-04 on reading (A) Canvas Inbox fan-out via the single course_<id> token, the cheapest reading that reaches the owner words; recorded as MY reading acted on, NOT an owner ruling - the (A) vs (B-prime) product fork Q-1 is still with the owner and binds only the W3 surface host, not the W1/W2 lib and action layers which are byte-identical either way. | What it costs an instructor to get from rubric to graded, measured against a chat window | A39 | verification | OWNER REPORT, 2026-09-23: grading an assignment is FASTER IN AN LLM CHAT than in this app. The owner's chat path is paste the rubric, paste the assignment description, then paste each submission. The app is supposed to beat that and does not, which is the question docs/loop/leverage.md requires every feature to answer - answered wrong at the level of the whole product rather than one feature. This row owns the measurement and the remedy: count every interaction the app demands from rubric to graded, count the same for the chat, and close the gap. | - | - | - | NOT YET MEASURED - that is the first deliverable, and it must be a COUNT, not an impression. The instrument is an interaction census of both paths for one assignment of N submissions: for the chat, the owner's own three-paste loop, which is 2 + N pastes and no navigation; for the app, every click, field, upload, wait and page transition from a cold start to a graded table, taken from the real code rather than from a design document. The census must separate PER-ASSIGNMENT setup cost from PER-SUBMISSION cost, because they scale differently and the chat's advantage is almost certainly in setup: a paste has no file dialog, no format requirement and no upstream state. DIRECTION OF FAILURE: any step the app requires that the chat does not, which does not buy something the chat cannot do, is a defect - and the app's answer to a step it cannot remove is to REMEMBER it, since a rubric pasted once should never be pasted again, which is a thing a chat genuinely cannot do. | Owner, 2026-09-23, unprompted, while the backlog was working on grading disclosure rows: "currently to grade assignments using an llm chat, i paste/drop the rubric in, do the same for the assignment description, and then do the same for each submission - it's much faster to use an llm chat today - plan a way to make this app faster to use than llms and then implement". | WHY THIS ROW OUTRANKS THE FEATURE ROWS AROUND IT: docs/loop/leverage.md asks what this app does that a chat cannot, and it is asked per feature. The owner has just answered it for the PRODUCT, and the answer is that the chat wins on the axis the instructor actually feels. A backlog full of correctness fixes on a surface that is slower than the alternative is a backlog optimising something nobody reaches. WHAT THE SCOPE MUST NOT DO: it must not assume the remedy is a faster upload. The chat's three pastes are not fast because pasting is fast - they are fast because the chat asks for NOTHING ELSE: no course selection, no assignment selection, no format, no naming convention, no connection, no waiting on a list to load. Every one of those is a step this app may be adding before the first submission is even seen. Measure them before designing anything. THE THREE CANDIDATE SHAPES, recorded so the scope argues against something concrete rather than inventing freely. (1) PERSISTENCE: the rubric and the assignment description are pasted EVERY TIME in a chat, once per app, and never again - if the app already stores them, the defect is that reaching the stored one costs more than re-pasting. (2) ONE SURFACE: if the three inputs live on three screens, the app has turned a linear paste loop into navigation, and no amount of per-field polish recovers that. (3) NO UPSTREAM GATE: any requirement to connect an LMS, pick a course, or produce a correctly-named zip BEFORE the first grade is a cost the chat does not charge, and it is paid before the instructor has seen any value at all. WHAT WOULD MAKE THE APP GENUINELY FASTER RATHER THAN EQUALLY FAST, which is the bar - CORRECTED 2026-09-23 BY docs/a39-research.md (ecdc5f8), WHICH FALSIFIED TWO OF THIS ROW'S OWN PREMISES WITH SOURCES, so the original wording is recorded here rather than quietly replaced. IT SAID: the chat cannot remember a rubric, cannot grade while the instructor is away, cannot hand the same rubric to a second assignment, and cannot write the grade back. TWO OF THOSE ARE FALSE. A chat CAN remember a rubric - Projects carry persistent per-project instructions and uploaded knowledge, so an instructor pastes it once, ever. A chat CAN write grades back - community MCP servers document Canvas grade-writing tools against a personal access token. WHAT ACTUALLY SURVIVES, and what any leverage claim must now rest on: PROVENANCE, because a project's instructions can be edited with no record of what was in force when a grade was produced, so a chat cannot say which rubric version produced a given grade; UNATTENDED RUNS; CONSISTENCY across a class in one invocation; and REMOVED SETUP rather than unavailable capability, which is the honest form of the LMS write-back claim. THE LOAD-BEARING CONSEQUENCE, which outranks both corrections: the chat's advantage is in FIXED cost and this app's is in MARGINAL cost, so the curves cross - and a design must therefore win at N = 1, because an instructor who finds the app slower on their first assignment never reaches the N where batching pays. NOTHING RENDERS UNDER VITEST HERE (docs/loop/this-repo.md section 6), so no interaction count can be verified by running the suite. The census is a READING claim traced from control to code, and the owner is the only one who can confirm the count matches what the screen asks for - route that confirmation rather than implying coverage. OWNER: a scoping seat, then an architect pass, then implementation. This row is deliberately not scoped in the same breath as being filed, because the measurement is the part that decides the shape and it has not been taken. A LIVE DEFECT FROM THIS FAMILY SHIPPED 2026-09-23 AT a9d9771, found in passing by the rubric-picture scoping pass (docs/n15-rubric-picture-scope.md), which concluded that row is a WAVE of A39 rather than an independent item. THE DEFECT: the zip grading path had NO wire-budget check at all, while the rubric text and the zip share one request body. Measured rather than assumed - grep -nE "upload-budget\|checkWireBudget" src/app/actions/grading.ts exited 1 against a canary of grep -c "formData.get" returning 8, proving the instrument reached the file. THE CEILING IT ENFORCES IS NOT THE CONFIGURED ONE: serverActions.bodySizeLimit is set to 10mb and that is DEAD CEILING, because the platform caps the body at about 4.5MB and the setting cannot raise it (src/lib/upload-budget.ts:5-9), so the operative refusal threshold is 2752512 bytes - 2.625MB on disk - re-derived independently this pass as the wire budget divided by the base64 inflation factor. THE FIX REUSES checkFileWireBudget rather than computing a second threshold, because two size limits that disagree is a bug nobody sees until a real upload lands between them, and it means the MB figure in the refusal is always the number the code enforces rather than one typed into a sentence. BOTH HALVES WERE WATCHED FAILING: removing the check, and forcing it to always refuse. The second is the one that matters - a size guard that refuses work which would have succeeded is a denial of service on the app's own users, and no other assertion would have caught it. WAVE 1 SHIPPED 2026-09-23 AT c031050 - the first production code from this row, and deliberately the N=1 case rather than the impressive one, because the design target is to win at ONE submission: an instructor who finds the app slower on their first assignment never reaches the class size where batching pays. A single dropped file now routes to the per-entry path instead of requiring an archive. THE TRAP THE WAVE WAS WRITTEN TO AVOID, and the reason classification is by EXTENSION and never by sniffing bytes: a .docx IS a zip, so a magic-number check classifies every Word document as an archive and tries to unpack it. The sabotage produced exactly that - a Corrupted zip? error against a real document - which is why the leaf was watched failing against that specific hazard shape rather than against a generic mutant. TWO SMALLER THINGS LANDED WITH IT: the refusal names what was actually uploaded rather than always saying zip, and the over-budget path is asserted to refuse WITHOUT calling the grader, so an oversized file cannot spend a model call before being rejected. THE TYPE GATE EARNED ITS PLACE AGAIN: tsc caught a bad tuple cast in the new test that the suite had accepted, the fourth time in one day that tsc caught what vitest could not - vitest here bundles a transform that erases types without reading them, so a green suite says nothing about types. SCOPE HELD AND IS STATED RATHER THAN IMPLIED: the non-Gemini provider branches are untouched and their single-file limitation is recorded as PRE-EXISTING rather than quietly absorbed, and the form still has exactly one action call site, so the pin guarding against a second submit path is unchanged. WAVE 3a-i SHIPPED 2026-09-23 AT 87fb303, under the owner branch (a) decision - tsx components with NO oracle, a cost recorded rather than dressed up: the moved markup has no executing instrument, leaving the ceiling number, the structure assertions and an owner check. SnapshotGradingPanel.tsx went 989 to 881 against a 940 gate, measured on both counters. RULING 33 WAS RIGHT AND CONSERVATIVE. Two grouped components rather than five small ones, because each extraction boundary costs its own call site: the arithmetic predicted 932-937 and the measured result is 881, while the five-small shape would have landed at 950-957 and missed. Anyone tempted to split these further should re-read that ruling first. THE EXTRACTION CROSSED MORE ANCHORS THAN THE PLAN NAMED - two anticipated, four not. Three structure tests pinned content to the panel own source that moved: the choose-files control added earlier the same day, two autofire onClick literals, and a disclosure paragraph plus a checkbox. Each was repointed per RULING 34 - an anchor an extraction crosses becomes part of its write set - preserving each check intent and reusing the file own combined-directory-source idiom rather than inventing a pattern, with new props named after the handlers they carry so the call-name pins still hold. LINT EARNED ITS PLACE as a mandatory gate here: the first run caught a real regression this wave introduced, an unused import in a new leaf, and the recorded React Compiler preserve-manual-memoization hazard on this file did not fire. ONE HONEST NOTE, carried rather than hidden: the full suite failed once with six timeouts in whole-tree scanning tests, none of them touching this directory, and passed when those four files were re-run in isolation - concurrent load under many parallel agents, not a regression. A24 WAS GATED ON THIS COMMIT (docs/a24-a39-sequencing.md RULING 32) and is unblocked by it, with its own target to be RE-DERIVED against this tree rather than adjusted from the old one. WAVE 2 SHIPPED 2026-09-27 AT 8a977b1 - rubric memory plus version provenance, the largest removable interaction cost the census measured. A run now reports the rubric AND fingerprint it actually used, read from the run and never from the store, so editing a stored rubric cannot change what a past run says graded it. The claim holds STRUCTURALLY rather than by review: the provenance reader takes a Pick of the run type, so it cannot see the storage shape even if sabotaged into trying. TWO TRAPS AVOIDED BY CONSTRUCTION, both worth keeping. An EMPTY SCOPE RESTORES NOTHING, because restoring a rubric before the assignment identity is known produces wrong grades that look right. And a miss falls back to the newest entry across scopes but is ALWAYS labelled with the scope it came from, never the one requested - so a borrowed rubric cannot masquerade as this assignment's. Anyone simplifying either behaviour is removing a guard, not dead code. THE FINDING WORTH MORE THAN THE FEATURE: W2-5's gate DOES NOT MEASURE ITS OWN CLAIM. Sabotaging engine.ts to import the fingerprint from the heavy module again left the test GREEN, because that canary already carries a pre-existing violation reaching server code via src/lib/grade/rubric.ts's own import of rubric-bank.ts - verified independently at rubric.ts:2 - so its assertion was already true and stays true either way. It cannot distinguish a widened closure from an unwidened one. That idiom compares keyof against a HAND-MAINTAINED array of field names, and bumping the array satisfies the type check ON ITS OWN, whether or not the field ever reached the serializer. A person can add a field to the type, add its name to the list, never open the serializer, and the type gate goes green while the field is dropped on the wire. Copying it would have produced something that looks like what I asked for and does not close the gap at the type gate - the same convention-dependent safety net DECISION 4 already got wrong once. What shipped derives the key set FROM the serializer instead: the explicit Record return annotation is dropped so TypeScript infers the literal shape the return object actually writes, and that inferred type is exported. | What it costs an instructor to get from rubric to graded, measured against a chat window | A46 | verification | CONCURRENT AND INCREMENTAL GRADING - the owner named this 2026-09-27 as an advantage the app can have over a chat and currently does not: upload a submission, have it START GRADING while the next one is still being uploaded, and so on down the line. THE APP CURRENTLY DOES THE OPPOSITE, measured: gradeStudentEntries (src/lib/grade/engine.ts) is a SEQUENTIAL for loop, one model call per student, with a DELIBERATE await sleep between iterations - DEFAULT_INTER_REQUEST_DELAY_MS = 1200 at src/lib/gemini.ts:67. And nothing is shown until every student finishes: GradingTab.tsx:513 renders results only when run.results.length is above zero, and run is the action single return value. So for 20 submissions the instructor waits at least 1.2 times 19 seconds of PURE SLEEP plus 20 model latencies, with zero feedback the whole time. A chat is strictly serial too, but it shows each result as it arrives - so on the axis the instructor actually feels, the chat currently wins the wait even though A39 census proves the app wins the CLICK COUNT at two submissions or fewer. THIS IS WHERE A39 LOSS ACTUALLY LIVES: its own section 2.1 is titled the number the interaction count cannot see. | - | - | - | the model-seam call pattern under a mocked seam: today a sequential loop with DEFAULT_INTER_REQUEST_DELAY_MS = 1200 between calls and one emission at the end; the row is discharged when the emitted-per-completion count is greater than one for N greater than one, asserted by observing the seam calls and the emissions rather than by timing, with A44 identity invariants still green so no reordering can misattribute a score | Owner, 2026-09-27, unprompted, on A39: concurrent grading and determinism as advantages over a chat | WHY THIS IS A LEVERAGE ROW AND NOT A PERFORMANCE ROW. docs/loop/leverage.md asks what this app does that a chat cannot, and CONCURRENCY IS NOT IN THAT CARD - grep for concurrent, parallel or pipeline returns ZERO. A chat is blocked on the human: it cannot begin work on submission two while the human is still producing submission one, because the human IS the transport. An app can, and that is a categorical advantage rather than a speed tweak. The owner supplied the class; the card should carry it. WHAT A SCOPE MUST ESTABLISH BEFORE DESIGNING. First, WHY the 1200ms sleep exists - it is almost certainly rate-limit protection, so concurrency has a real ceiling and the scope must find it rather than removing a guard it does not understand. Second, the platform bound: this loop runs inside a Server Action with NO declared maxDuration - the census measured that grep as exiting 1 against a firing canary - so a longer or wider run dies on the platform default, which is exactly what G4 and G5 exist to bound. A concurrency design that ignores the ceiling makes the failure mode worse rather than better. Third, whether INCREMENTAL DISPLAY can be separated from CONCURRENCY: showing each result as it completes may deliver most of the felt improvement with none of the rate-limit risk, and if so it is a smaller first wave. THE INSTRUMENT PROBLEM IS REAL HERE: nothing renders under vitest in this repo, so no claim about what the instructor SEES during a run can be executed - and a wall-clock timing assertion is not available either, because the network is blocked and there are no keys. So the scope must say what CAN be executed - the order and count of model-seam calls, the shape of what is emitted per completion - and label the rest reading claims. DO NOT LET THIS COMPROMISE ATTRIBUTION. A44 and A45 exist because the right score must reach the right student; a concurrent design that reorders completions must keep the identity key attached to each result rather than relying on arrival order. The A44 identity work shipped 2026-09-27 at 580e505 and e53979c and its invariants are executable - use them as a constraint, not as an afterthought. SHIPPED IN PART 2026-09-27 AT ceab414, AND THE HEADLINE CLAIM DID NOT HOLD. Concurrent grading is real - a three-at-a-time pool, results emitted per student as they arrive rather than all-or-nothing, with sourceIndex-keyed attribution that turns red when keyed on arrival order instead. That is the owner two asks, and both have failing tests behind them. BUT THE REFUSAL-BEFORE-SPEND CLAIM IS BOTH UNPROVEN AND FALSE END TO END, and I reported it to the owner as proven before the check caught it. UNPROVEN: grading-incremental.test.ts asserts mockCallLlm not.toHaveBeenCalled() at three places and NEVER asserts the positive - and the module under test has NO model-call site at all (grep for callLlm/gradeEntries/gradeSubmissions/generateRubric in it returns one hit, a comment), so the assertion is true by construction. I told the owner an anti-vacuity companion existed showing the mock CAN be called. It does not exist. This is a REPEAT of the a-check-whose-assertion-cannot-fail class ruled on earlier the same day, so its second attempt must change KIND: the claim moves to the CONSUMER, not to a harder assertion in the same place. FALSE END TO END: the hook answers mode whole-run - which the action returns for EVERY thrown error, the collision refusal included - by running the full gradeAction, which pays generateRubric plus the full-credit checklist and the sample answer in the SAME Promise.all as the throwing call, already dispatched and not cancelled by the rejection. So a refusal currently COSTS money on the way out. That is a design finding and is NOT fixed by fixing the vacuous test. The consumer is also the only place the spend is observable, so one instrument closes both. STILL OPEN AND NOT ADDRESSED BY THIS WAVE: residual R2, whether Canvas submissions can collide the way zip filenames can - extractCanvasEntries has no refusal today and none was added, so the Canvas ticket-building inherits that gap unchanged. Not a regression, restated because it is still open. THE REFUSAL FIX LANDED 2026-09-27 AT 8018858, closing both halves of what the build check found. THE SPEND: prepareGradingRunAction mapped EVERY thrown error to mode whole-run, the collision refusal included, and the consumer answered that mode by running the full gradeAction - which pays generateRubric, the full-credit checklist and the sample answer in the SAME Promise.all as the again-throwing call, already dispatched and not cancelled by the rejection. So the refusal COST money on its way out. There is now a third variant, mode refused, which surfaces the reason and never reaches the paying seam; an ordinary unexpected error still falls back exactly as before. THE VACUOUS PROOF: the old assertions checked that the model mock was not called in a module with NO model-call site, so they were true by construction - and I reported that to the owner as proven. Being a REPEAT of the assertion-that-cannot-fail class, its second attempt changed KIND rather than strength: the claim MOVED to the consumer, the only place the spend is observable, with the existing block that proves the paying seam IS reachable serving as its positive control rather than a duplicated one. Reverting the fix turns it red with the seam called once. DEBT RECORDED RATHER THAN HIDDEN: the refusal is distinguished by a STRING PREFIX on the error message, because the throw site is a plain Error in a module outside that write set. It is correct today - collisionRefusal.ts:128 is the emitter - but the prefix is NOT a unique sentinel repo-wide: CommandProposalModal.tsx:90 builds the same string for display. It cannot reach that catch on any current path, so this is a LATENT coupling, not a live bug. A typed error or an exported sentinel is the real remedy and needs the throw site in scope. NOTE the route itself is gated off by RULING 116, which is exactly WHY this had to be correct before the flag flips rather than after. RESIDUAL R2 SETTLED AND FIXED 2026-09-28: scope at fd3c027, fix at 2f06261. THE ANSWER WAS WORSE THAN THE QUESTION. Two students sharing a display name collapsed into ONE slot of a name-keyed edit map (seedEdits, keyed on result.student), and the Canvas post payload then read userId PER ROW while taking grade and comment FROM THAT COLLAPSED MAP - so both students were PUT with the second one score. LIVE ON MAIN via gradeCanvasUrl, NOT behind INCREMENTAL_ROUTE_ENABLED. So the owner second guarantee - the right score goes to the right student - held on the zip path and FAILED on the Canvas path. Verified link by link before accepting it. WHY NINE SUB-WAVES RESTATED R2 WITHOUT RESOLVING IT: R2 POINTED AT THE WRONG CONTROL. It asked whether extractCanvasEntries needs the zip path collision REFUSAL. It must not - a refusal is right when the app CANNOT tell two students apart, and Canvas hands over an authoritative user id. The zip path own answer to distinct-identities-identical-display is not a refusal either; it is a terminal DISAMBIGUATION pass. The missing control was the disambiguation, not the refusal, and no amount of restating R2 as written would have found that. THE TRIGGER IS NOT EXOTIC. My brief listed enrolment exotica - test students, re-enrolment, groups, resubmissions - and FOUR OF THOSE FIVE DO NOT COLLIDE. What collides is two distinct students with the same sortable name, or two rows that both degrade to an unknown-student placeholder. The real mechanism is that the app identity is a NAME while Canvas identity is a NUMBER, and nothing in the grading path keys on the number. THE FIX reuses the zip path mechanism rather than writing a second dialect: assignUnclaimedLabel extracted as a pure function and wired at all THREE Canvas entry points, including the single-submission action as a deliberate no-op so a future multi-entry caller cannot forget it. The zip path 107-test oracle across 27 frozen fixtures is unchanged and green, which is how behaviour preservation was proven rather than argued. Positive control is REFERENCE IDENTITY - two differently-named entries come back identical by reference, because a disambiguator that renames when it need not is its own defect and only reference identity catches that. IT ALSO FIXED A TEST THAT COULD NOT FAIL: an existing assertion already claimed one slot per result, labelled exactly that, and its two fixture students had DIFFERENT NAMES - green by construction on the only case that mattered. That is the fixture-shape trap, live, inside the one test that would otherwise have caught a wrong grade reaching a student. WHAT IT DOES NOT PROTECT AGAINST, stated rather than hedged: if two Canvas submissions can share one userId - re-enrolment, multi-section, or a pagination-boundary double-serve - disambiguating the DISPLAY makes the collision LOOK handled while the double PUT still overwrites. Needs a live Canvas; stays a residual with an owner. Nothing renders under this vitest, so the payload builder and the React keys at render time remain an owner browser check - proven to the absorption site, not end to end, and it says so. The disambiguated label WORDING is deliberately undecided and is an owner call, since Canvas holds a real discriminator the zip path never had. CONFIRMED 2026-09-28: assignUnclaimedLabel is imported and called at src/lib/grade/extraction.ts:17,226 (grep -rn assignUnclaimedLabel src/lib/grade/extraction.ts), matching the Canvas-path disambiguation fix this row's note describes as shipped at 2f06261; the disambiguated-label wording and the live-Canvas-only shared-userId edge case remain open with the owner as this row's own note already states. | - |

| Slide decks generated from an uploaded source against an uploaded template | A43 | verification | OWNER ASKED 2026-09-27: generate a SLIDE DECK from an uploaded content source - a repo, chapter objectives, a homework, anything - with the flexibility of an LLM and the structure of a deterministic method. The requirement that makes it hard is the conjunction: the owner must be able to upload and ask for things conversationally WHILE the output is guaranteed to use a predefined template THEY uploaded. Not a template the app ships, and not a layout the model imitates - the owner's own file, obeyed. | - | - | - | MEASURED 2026-09-27 BEFORE FILING, because the tree already holds substantial slide machinery and that changes what this row is. git ls-files \| grep -i slide returns 20+ paths including src/app/components/slide-studio/ (SlideStudio.tsx, DeckModeSection.tsx, useDeckMode.ts, constants.ts, utils.ts), src/app/components/ppt-design/SlidesPanel.tsx, src/app/components/SlideGraphicPreview.tsx, src/app/actions/slide-graphics-repair.ts and a spec at docs/slide-spec.md. TEMPLATE MACHINERY ALSO EXISTS: git ls-files \| grep -iE "pptx\|template" returns src/app/actions/artifact-templates.ts, src/app/components/artifact-design/TemplateSelector.tsx and - the most relevant hit - src/app/components/content-tab/modules/useLmsGenerationDeckTemplates.ts, whose name asserts deck templates are already a concept here. NONE OF THIS IS A CLAIM THAT THE FEATURE EXISTS: the scoping pass must trace each surface end to end, because this repo has repeatedly found both capabilities with no surface reaching them and surfaces that call nothing. What is established is only that the row is probably EXTENSION or REACHABILITY work rather than greenfield, and a scope that starts from a blank page would rebuild what ships. | Owner, 2026-09-27, verbatim: "A way to make a slide deck given an uploaded content source (repo, ch obj's, homework, etc). The flexibility of an llm with the structure of a deterministic method. I need to be able to smoothly upload and ask for things while still ensuring the output is going to use a predefined template that i upload". | THE CONJUNCTION IS THE FEATURE, and a scope that treats it as two features will get it wrong. An LLM asked to produce slides against a template will drift: it renames placeholders, invents a layout, or emits content that does not fit the shapes it was given. So the design question is not how to prompt for slides - it is WHERE THE GUARANTEE LIVES. The candidate shapes, recorded so the scope argues against something concrete: (a) the model emits CONTENT ONLY as structured data and a deterministic writer places it into the uploaded template, so the template is never in the model's hands; (b) the model emits a deck and a validator rejects anything that violates the template, with a repair loop; (c) the model picks among template-declared layouts by name and cannot express anything else. Shape (a) is the only one where the guarantee is structural rather than checked, and this project has a rule about that - a constraint enforced by construction beats one enforced by review - but it also constrains what the owner can ask for, which is the trade the owner has to see. WHAT THE SCOPE MUST SETTLE, and must not default. (1) WHAT FORMAT IS THE UPLOADED TEMPLATE? A .pptx the owner exported, an HTML/CSS theme, or a JSON layout spec are three different features with three different libraries, and docs/slide-spec.md plus the existing deck-template module may already answer it - read them before asking. (2) WHAT DOES SMOOTHLY UPLOAD AND ASK MEAN against the A39 census, which measured that this app's problem is interaction cost and that a chat's advantage is asking for NOTHING ELSE? A deck flow that demands a course, a template pick and a format before the first slide has reproduced the defect A39 exists to fix. (3) WHERE DOES THE SOURCE COME FROM? A repo is already a first-class input on the grading side, and the owner lists it first - so reuse is likely, and a second uploader would be a second spelling of one act. (4) WHAT HAPPENS WHEN THE CONTENT DOES NOT FIT? Text longer than a shape, more sections than the template has layouts, an image where the template wants none. Silent truncation is the worst outcome and the most likely one. LEVERAGE, and this row has a real answer rather than a thin one: a chat can draft slide TEXT, and can even be handed a template to imitate, but it cannot GUARANTEE the output conforms to a file the instructor uploaded, cannot apply the same template across a term without being re-told, and cannot say which template version produced a given deck. That last one is the same provenance class A3 is being redesigned around. Argue it against the mechanism, and concede the parts that are convenience. NOTHING RENDERS UNDER VITEST HERE and tests are network-blocked, so no claim about a generated deck's appearance can be verified in this checkout - route those to owner verification with named steps rather than implying coverage. FIRST CODE LANDED 2026-09-27 AT 8301129, and it is a test rather than a feature, deliberately: src/lib/office-edit.ts is 755 lines, carries the entire byte-identity guarantee this row depends on, and had ZERO direct tests - every test file reaching it mocked it. Five assertions now cover the pptx read path, the byte-identity contract, numeric slide ordering, the documented inability to add a slide, and the zero-slide case, with every fixture built in memory because no pptx is tracked in the repo. THE MOST USEFUL RESULT WAS A SABOTAGE THAT FAILED TO FAIL. The first attempt to prove the byte-identity assertion could break stayed GREEN, because the fixture's untouched paragraph happened to carry its attributes in the same order the rebuild path emits - so a full rebuild was indistinguishable from leaving it alone, and the assertion was proving an accident rather than a guarantee. It had to be re-fixtured with a deliberately NON-CANONICAL attribute order before the guard absence became observable. That is the class this project is organised against, and it surfaced only because proving the sabotage was mandatory rather than optional. Anyone editing that test must keep the non-canonical fixture or it silently stops measuring anything. THE OTHER SABOTAGES DID GO RED as intended, including dropping the numeric flag from the slide sort, which orders ten before two. NEW INFORMATION ON RES-A43-4, which the scope had routed to owner verification: a template carrying no slide entries IS reachable through this read path, and it returns an EMPTY RESULT rather than throwing. Silent, not loud - the worse direction for a feature whose whole promise is conformance to an uploaded file. The residual stays with its owner; only the mechanism is now known. ALSO PINNED AS A LIMIT, not as a bug: the machinery can rewrite, clone or delete a paragraph and cannot add a slide. The test asserts that an unmatched append-shaped section is INERT, and says in its own comment that a future slide-clone feature is expected to make it fail - so the contract gets updated deliberately rather than discovered by surprise. A43-S SHIPPED 2026-09-27 AT 4080c2e - the wiring piece. The manual deck surface now takes a source: paste a repo reference or attach a file, and it reaches the generator's materials slot, which was declared and injected into the prompt all along while that one surface built its context without the key. It reuses the existing extractor and the existing repo ingest rather than writing a second of either. THE REACHABILITY ASSERTION WAS WATCHED FAILING ON EXACTLY THE CITED DEFECT: the pure module was already correct, so only the context wiring was red, and THREE CANARIES prove the detector discriminates a real binding from an empty one and from an absent one. A test that merely found the word in the file would have passed while the slot stayed empty - which is the shape this row keeps encountering. TWO CONSTRAINTS HELD rather than being traded away. No course identifier was introduced, verified by grep across all six touched files, so the surface still requires no course - unusually good for this app and easy to lose. And the Generate button no longer requires a typed subject, which was the concrete interaction-cost violation: the subject derives from the source, so attaching a file is enough. Measured after: 6 cold with a source and a blank subject, warm unchanged at 3, and UNCHANGED for anyone who uses no source at all - the control is fully optional. ONE DEFECT THE ORCHESTRATOR FIXED ON REVIEW, recorded because it is a cross-row hazard: the new actions used requireOwner, which is literally a return of requireUser (auth.ts:451-453). Row R2 exists to retire that alias, so two new call sites would have made R2 bigger while reading as an ownership check they do not perform. Both now call requireUser explicitly, with the reasoning recorded at the import rather than left for the next reader. ANY LATER A43 WAVE ADDING AN ACTION MUST DO THE SAME. The guard-coverage ratchet and the use-server export rule both stayed green. The implementer also declined to add an icon dependency this repo does not have, substituting plain buttons instead of introducing one silently. STILL OPEN ON THIS ROW: A43-T, the uploaded-template guarantee, which is the real feature; and A43-C, the conversational ask the owner kept in scope. WAVE T1 SHIPPED 2026-09-27 AT f497303 - the port. A template is now an owner-uploaded pptx filled by the mechanism already shipping for docx, and H1 IS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN DOCUMENTED: the module exports exactly one writer, it takes a partial REPLACEMENT list and never a section list, and it internally maps over every paragraph the parser returned - so the section count equals the paragraph count unconditionally and there is no exported function a caller could hand a partial list to. WATCHED FAILING THREE WAYS: removing the untouched-paragraph short-circuit in the shared writer turned the byte-identity assertion red; sabotaging the new module to emit sections only for what it filled - the exact predicted defect - turned four of ten tests red with paragraphs silently deleted; and a PERMANENT regression test calls the underlying primitive with a hand-built partial list to pin its failure mode independently of any temporary sabotage. Behaviour preservation is BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by a pin: the wave touches neither the shared writer nor the shipped docx action, both confirmed by empty diffs, so there was nothing to freeze. THE GAP IT SHIPPED WITH, live until wave T2 lands: when a deck needs more slides than the uploaded template has, the extra generated slides are SILENTLY NOT WRITTEN. The upload control is reachable, so a five-slide template for a ten-slide deck loses five slides with no indication. That is the fixed-length limit the row knew about, and the refusal is T2 - dispatched in the same turn, with closing the silence named as its purpose rather than a nicety. THE MIGRATION WAS CHECKED BEFORE PUSH because migrations auto-apply here: additive only - one new table, policies guarded with drop-if-exists for idempotent re-apply, and a cascade only from a new foreign key. No destructive operation against existing data. One deliberate deviation from the docx precedent, reported rather than taken silently: that path has a separate CRUD lib module, but this wave file list named only the action file, so CRUD lives inline rather than in an unlisted new file. WAVE T2 SHIPPED 2026-09-27 AT e4021de, closing the silence T1 shipped with - a deck needing more slides than the uploaded template holds is now REFUSED with a counted reason instead of the extra slides being written nowhere. THE WATCHED FAILURE IS THE HONEST KIND: the assertions were written before the planning function existed and failed with not-a-function, which is the proof that the tree had no path even to ASK whether the deck fits - a two-slide template against a five-slide deck had nothing to detect it with. All six adjustment classes are typed and COUNTED rather than described, so the copy carries real numbers, and the zero case says so explicitly rather than rendering nothing, because no-news is indistinguishable from a broken report. Only the slide-count class is wired to a live caller; the module header says which five are unreachable BY DESIGN rather than leaving that to be discovered. TWO THINGS THE IMPLEMENTER FOUND AND REPORTED RATHER THAN ABSORBED. The wave file list omitted the action file where the silent truncation actually lived, so fixing only the named files would have shipped unreachable dead code - the assignment-must-include-the-wiring-file failure this repo has hit before, hit again by MY file list. And the panel rendered its error only in the pre-generation branch, so a refusal raised from Download or Save would have been swallowed by the UI even though the caller was setting it. The type gate caught a real error in the new test - a property read on a union member that lacks it - which vitest had accepted, which is the rolldown type-erasure hazard behaving exactly as this repo documents. ONE MEASUREMENT CORRECTED: the implementer reported a sibling test file at 1034 lines, over the hard ceiling. Re-measured independently at 992 by wc -l, and the ceiling gate passes at exit 0 - the reading was a transient mid-write state of the concurrent A32 remediation, not a breach, so no warning was issued on it. A43-C C1+C3 SHIPPED 533f66eb (conversational content-op layer + POST /api/decks/ask): closed DeckOperation vocabulary bound to PptxSlide[], coerce-returns-null-not-default, content ops MERGE preserving notes/graphic (leave-unless-present), slides.length preserved by construction (retarget/reorder refuse), add/delete-slide/layout unrepresentable-or-refused, route requireUser + withDeadline 60s wall + explicit refused-not-success; 43 tests, CO-PRESERVE/CO-COUNT sabotages RED/GREEN, verify SHIP 0 blockers. C2 (in-app ask box wiring) is the owed successor; endpoint inert-to-user until then. A43-C C2 SHIPPED e4dea341 (in-app ask box on the ppt-design deck wired to POST /api/decks/ask): ok applies to editedSlides so preview+Download+Save reflect it, refusal/error/timeout surface inline with deck untouched, draft persists ta-ppt-ask-draft, reducer branches HTTP+status so refused-at-200 is never success; verify SHIP 0 blockers. A43-C is now END-TO-END; render/keyboard/live round-trip are owner-verification. | ANSWERED 2026-09-27: the scope recommended routing the conversational ask OUT of this row, on the measured ground that no function or tool calling exists anywhere in the app. THE OWNER SAID KEEP IT, so A43 is the whole request. See docs/owner-decisions-2026-09-27.md DECISION 15, which records what that costs: the mechanism does not exist, so building it is now the largest single thing in this row - an operation set, a schema per operation, a validating dispatcher and a refusal path - and the row cannot close until it ships. THE MITIGATION IS SEQUENCING, NOT SCOPE: the template half ships first and completely, because it is the half with a structural guarantee, and the conversational layer is built against something that already conforms. THREE THINGS THAT DO NOT RELAX TO FIT IT: the guarantee stays structural, so a conversational request may change CONTENT and may never reach the writer, the template or the layout - the operation set is the enforcement point; the interaction budget stays, so no required step, mode switch or prerequisite is added to a surface measured at three warm interactions with no course prerequisite; and the inability to add a slide stays, so an ask to add one must refuse in a way that says why rather than silently producing nothing. STILL OPEN FOR THE REVISED SCOPE, deliberately not decided: whether this is a general tool-calling mechanism or a deck-specific operation set - reusable and much larger, against smaller and certain to be re-implemented. The scope must argue it with a recommendation rather than default to whichever is easier to write. |

The carve-out is KNOWN_UNREGISTERED_LOCAL_FILES - which has MOVED since this row was filed and now lives at src/app/components/grading-results/gradingResultsHelpersWiring.test.ts:81-84 with its consuming filter at :142, relocated when gradingResultsHelpers.test.ts was split at 999 lines against a 1000 ceiling, following this repo's existing DELIBERATELY_UNREGISTERED idiom so it is visible in the test file rather than hidden. The offending import is in src/app/components/grading-results/classTrendsEntry.ts (46 lines, added by A16-1 in commit 6ecc226). The guard it collides with is BANNED_IMPORT_PATTERNS, whose entries are anchored to the @/lib/grade alias. NOTE the same commit also added FilesCell.tsx without registering it in CLIENT_FILES; the A12/A13 implementer registered that one directly after verifying it carries no banned imports, so FilesCell is CLOSED and only classTrendsEntry remains. Re-measure with: grep -n "@/lib/grade" src/app/components/grading-results/classTrendsEntry.ts | A12/A13 implementer, 2026-09-20, flagged as a follow-up rather than fixed out of scope | TWO LEGITIMATE FIXES AND THE CHOICE IS A DESIGN CALL, not a cleanup. (1) CHANGE THE IMPORT to @/lib/grade/types, which is the narrow specifier A12/A13's Ruling R deliberately exempted. Cheapest, and it makes the carve-out unnecessary. Check first that every symbol the file needs actually lives in types.ts - Ruling R exempted types.ts precisely because it is the type surface, and the A12/A13 round also added a guard that types.ts itself stays free of value imports, so widening what flows through it is not free. (2) TEACH THE GUARD to distinguish a TYPE-ONLY import from a value import, and accept type-only barrel imports explicitly. More honest if other files will hit this, but note the A12/A13 round ALREADY REPLACED a regex guard here with a walled-set check after the regex was shown to miss export-from, export-star, require and dynamic import - so do not reintroduce a regex-shaped answer. WHY THIS IS WORTH A ROW RATHER THAN A DRIVE-BY: a named carve-out is a guard with a hole in it, and this repo's recorded failure mode is exactly an instrument that reads clean while measuring less than it claims. The carve-out is currently CORRECT and VISIBLE; it becomes a defect the moment a second file is added to it without argument. Whoever takes this should also decide whether the carve-out list should exist at all once the one entry is gone - an empty escape hatch invites use. SHIPPED 2026-09-20 at 7375a21, which narrowed the specifier and deleted KNOWN_UNREGISTERED_LOCAL_FILES outright - which also answers this row's closing question about whether the carve-out list should exist at all: it does not any more. RES-A22-1 was discharged in substance by 4dad288, which replaced the raw-source pattern guard with an AST walk. RES-A22-4, FROM docs/a22-scope.md SECTION 11 AND RECORDED NOWHERE ELSE: seventeen other files type-only-import from a barrel the same way classTrendsEntry.ts did, so the same collision is latent wherever the next guard is added. The scope doc assigned it to "a note on whichever row adds the next sweep" and no such row was ever named, which is the deletion. OWNER: whoever adds the next import guard in this directory. STEP: the next chunk that adds or widens an import guard under src/app/components/grading-results/.
