| Grading runs: bounds, failures, not-attempted rows, class-trends signal | N13a | verification | The grading CAP became an honest bound of 40 plus a wall-clock deadline (shipped 7c409a6) - still owed: remove the class-trends-draft FLOOR (class-trends-draft.ts:18, enforced at :168 - re-measured 2026-09-22 by grep -n on the file; the :154 this row carried is pre-2c92fec). The comment half of this row is DISCHARGED: 2c92fec (2026-09-20, "the class-trends coverage warning describes a condition that has already arrived") rewrote it, and it now reads correctly at :88-104. Only the floor removal is still owed. | - | - | - | git show 7c409a6 --stat, 2026-09-15, confirms the cap/deadline half shipped (engine.ts deadlineMs at :187/:207-210, GradeResult -> GradedResult \| UngradedResult union in types.ts). Direct read, 2026-09-15: gemini.ts:32 (DEFAULT_MAX_SUBMISSIONS = 40), gemini.ts:129-133 (getGeminiMaxSubmissions), class-trends-draft.ts:18 (DEFAULT_CLASS_TRENDS_DRAFT_FLOOR = 5, unremoved), :88-104 (the comment, REWRITTEN by 2c92fec on 2026-09-20 and no longer stale), :168 (floor still enforced, re-measured 2026-09-22), class-trends.ts:124-196 (AreaTrend/classifyDirection, unchanged and still accurate). | N13, owner decision 1, 2026-09-15 | THE FLOOR is NEXT_PUBLIC_CLASS_TRENDS_DRAFT_FLOOR - layer C refuses to draft below 5 graded submissions. Straight removal, no replacement. RECORD THAT IT WAS DELIBERATE: the floor existed because the draft is addressed to STUDENTS rather than to the instructor who did the grading, so a message written off two submissions goes out to a whole class. The recommendation on file was keep-the-floor-raise-the-cap; the owner overrode it knowingly and that is the owner's call to make. THE CAP is DEFAULT_MAX_SUBMISSIONS (src/lib/gemini.ts:25), read via GRADE_MAX_SUBMISSIONS (:124-125) and applied by .slice(0, maxSubmissions) at src/lib/grade/engine.ts:126. DO NOT SIMPLY DELETE IT, and this is the whole reason this row exists separately: the cap is not only a cost knob, it is also what keeps a grading run inside the platform function limit. This deploys to Vercel Hobby, where maxDuration is capped at 60s. Delete the constant with no replacement bound and a large class times out MID-RUN - strictly worse for the instructor than a capped run that completes, because a timeout loses the work already done. The scoping must measure the real constraint (the route's maxDuration, the per-submission latency assumptions, any existing batching) and propose what replaces it: a much higher explicit ceiling, batching, a resumable run, or something better. Obeying the instruction literally here would ship the failure, so the deliverable is the bound, not the deletion. OWNER SET THE BOUND 2026-09-15, so it is no longer a question to escalate. Verbatim, in two messages: "we need to raise that to at least 20", then "raise it to 40 - we'll rarely go that high". THE CAP BECOMES 40, and the second half of that sentence is the part that changes the design rather than just the number: 40 is intended as a RARELY-TOUCHED SANITY CEILING, not as the working limit. THE CONSEQUENCE THE OWNER HAS ACCEPTED, stated so nobody re-litigates it: the count bound is no longer what keeps a run inside the platform invocation ceiling. That protection has been deliberately traded away, so src/lib/visualizer/selection-coverage.ts:62-100's written rule - that the count bound IS the actual defense against a platform kill - cannot simply be ported to this loop. Whatever ships must either argue why the grading loop differs or supply a different survival mechanism. THE ARITHMETIC THAT REFRAMES THE ITEM, modelled and NOT measured, flagged as such because the real per-call latency is not measurable in this checkout: engine.ts:207-209 applies getGeminiInterRequestDelayMs between entries (DEFAULT_INTER_REQUEST_DELAY_MS = 1200, gemini.ts:60) and this repo's own written model in selection-coverage.ts puts an LLM call at roughly 5s, so a student costs on the order of 6.2s and a run of TEN is already near a 60s grant. If that holds, the cap of 5 has been MASKING a pre-existing timeout, and raising it does not create the problem so much as expose it - which also means the owner's own working case, a class of 9 to 12, may already be at the ceiling today. That reframing, not the constant, is the item. SO THE DELIVERABLE IS NOW SURVIVAL, not sizing: incremental persistence so finished students are durable before an invocation dies, a Route Handler that declares its own ceiling, or chunked/resumable execution across invocations - with the not-attempted disclosure as the REPORTING half rather than the protection half. IMMEDIATE LEVER, owner-only and requiring no deploy: GRADE_MAX_SUBMISSIONS is read at runtime from the environment (gemini.ts:122-127), so setting it to 40 in the Vercel dashboard raises the bound in production now; DEFAULT_MAX_SUBMISSIONS = 5 in code is only the fallback, and parsePositiveInt silently falls back to it on a malformed or non-positive value, so a typo in the dashboard reverts to five without complaint. Raising the constant in code is still owed, because the fallback should not contradict the owner's decision. NOTE THE COLLISION this creates, already documented at class-trends-draft.ts:87-88: the class-trends draft floor and the cap are both 5 today and that file's comments reason about them being equal. At 40 they are not, and those comments go stale in the same change that deletes the floor. DISPOSAL ROUND 2026-09-15. The round-2 artifact came back NOT READY (4 blockers, 7 majors), and under iteration-caps.md this artifact is now AT ITS DISPOSAL ROUND on two independent grounds: cap 2, because round 2 was its second revision, and cap 1, because owns-list-caller-misclassification has now failed TWICE and the second attempt strengthened the same mechanism - a longer, better-cited owns list - instead of changing kind. No further revision of the owns list by a design seat is legal. THE FOUR DISPOSALS. (a) RELOCATE, the owns list: it moves to a step that DERIVES it with its own instrument rather than authoring it again. RECEIVER: the implementer wave that opens the build. OBLIGATION: run the enumeration itself over gradeAction, getGeminiMaxSubmissions, composeClassTrendsDraft and below-floor, treat every list in these artifacts as a FLOOR and never as the set, and open every call site rather than describing it. The instrument is grep plus opening the file; the step is the wave gate, which already diffs git status --short against the assignment. WHAT THE SECOND FAILURE ACTUALLY MISSED, recorded so the derivation starts ahead rather than level: GradingTab.tsx is NOT a caller of the grading path (it imports only fetchCanvasMetaAction and a type), while the real callers are src/app/page.tsx:63 via useActionState, and three workflow registry steps - steps.grading-run.ts:473,533, steps.grading-draft-flow.ts:262 and steps.grading-cartridge.ts:100 - none of which appeared in either round. src/lib/grade/engine.test.ts and src/lib/grade/class-trends.test.ts are also owed. (b) REDUCE, two questions to the owner, batched and not gating. (c) RESIDUAL, the owner-blocked measurements, escalated rather than parked with a future implementer: the REAL platform grant for the current Server-Action path, and ONE timed grading run against a live key. Until both exist every per-student latency figure in these artifacts is modelled, the chunk size is a starting point and must be labelled one, and at this repo's own 25 percent margin standard that starting point is SEVEN, not eight. (d) DELETE: nothing. ORCHESTRATOR RULINGS, mine, because the check's stopping point was rulings and re-dispatching the author on them is forbidden. RULING 1, SCOPE: N13a COVERS THE UNATTENDED CALLERS, and this is the finding that would have shipped a silent regression. getGeminiMaxSubmissions is read ONCE, at engine.ts:122, on the shared path - so raising the default to 40 raises it for EVERY caller, including three workflow registry steps that run server-side inside a cron tick whose route declares maxDuration = 60 (src/app/api/cron/run-schedules/route.ts:56). Built as round 2 specified - client-driven chunking - the browser path would be safe and the unattended path would attempt up to 40 students inside a 60s tick and lose the entire run, with lint, tsc, next build and all 94 tests green, because nothing here renders a component or runs a tick. A mechanism that only exists in the browser cannot bound a knob that is read on the server. RULING 2, REINSTATE THE WALL-CLOCK DEADLINE. Round 2 withdrew it on the ground that the owner's decision "superseded" it; that is a non-sequitur - the owner settled a COUNT, which says nothing about whether the loop should refuse to START entry N+1 past a deadline. The withdrawal removed the only proposed mechanism that works inside a single server-side invocation, which is exactly what ruling 1 requires. It is also already shipped in this tree and was never cited: cron/run-schedules/route.ts:123-126 computes runDeadlineMs = now + 50_000 and stops starting new institutions past it so the rest resume next tick, threaded as deadlineMs at :372 and :515. That is the precedent to port. RULING 3, THE NOT-ATTEMPTED ROW IS A SEAM, not a detail, and it goes to the TOP TIER rather than to a design seat: A12 and N13b are both blocked on its exact shape, three downstream consumers read it (class-trends.ts, gradeReposAction's reporting arrays, grading.ts's Canvas post), and round 2 both demoted it to "the reporting half" and left it unspecified while asserting elsewhere that it folds into unscoredCount. Those two cannot both hold. THE CONSTRAINT THAT SHAPE MUST SATISFY, discovered by the check and load-bearing: a not-attempted row must be appended to results BEFORE engine.ts:226's canonical-column reconciliation. Appended after, it reproduces the exact 4-of-5 coverage defect recorded against N13b - N13a's own feature resurrecting the defect this pass spent a page retiring. AND IT MUST NOT BE POSTABLE TO CANVAS: see A13, filed separately, where a FAILURE row already reaches the student today. ONE RECORDED DEFECT IS RETIRED, with a caveat. The check settled by reading what round 2 could only call likely: engine.ts:226-247 gives every result an entry for every canonical area, blank-filling at :245, and class-trends.ts:274 counts every entry present, so a failure row leaves resultsWithArea equal to totalResults and the clause DOES render. The "executed, not reasoned" 4-of-5 example in N13b's note does NOT reproduce against the real engine and was almost certainly executed on a hand-built fixture whose failure row never passed through reconciliation - this repo's own recorded failure mode. N13b's ruling that clauses disclose their own denominator still stands on its own merits; only the worked example is withdrawn. The confirming test, when written, must build the row THROUGH gradeStudentEntries, or it will re-confirm the fixture instead of the code. ALSO CORRECTED: round 2 misquoted its own margin anchor - selection-coverage.ts:68 says roughly 15s and 25 percent, not 13s and 22 - and labelled the Server-Action grant "confirmed" when selection-coverage.ts:51-58 says in terms that it was never independently verified. SEAM DISPOSAL AND SIX RULINGS 2026-09-15. The not-attempted row seam (scratchpad/not-attempted-seam.md) reached its disposal round: round 2 was its second revision, and its round-2 check returned 6 blockers over FOUR classes, EVERY ONE A REPEAT. Under iteration-caps.md cap 1 a repeat goes to disposal immediately and a third attempt would be strengthening the same mechanism, which is forbidden; under cap 2 round 3 produces only dispositions. THE AUTHOR IS NOT RE-DISPATCHED. The check also established that with these six rulings applied THE SEAM IS SHIPPABLE: its type shape compiled clean over ten requirements the author never probed, its door table was independently re-derived and matches at all 8 call sites, its consumer enumeration was accurate at every line opened, and its reconciliation against the landed checkRowPostability (6 of 8 doors covered, the 2 CSV doors left as the seam's own residual) is correct. RULING 1, THE FAILURE MESSAGE: ungraded.message CARRIES the "This submission could not be graded: " prefix. The artifact defined it two incompatible ways - section 2's field doc said the verbatim thrown message, section 5 said the prefix is its opening - and an implementer pasting section 2's code block would have picked the one that silently changes today's string and falsifies the artifact's own byte-identity claim. Carrying the prefix preserves engine.test.ts:108 and today's displayed text. CONSEQUENCE THAT MUST SHIP WITH IT, closing the artifact's major 6: GRADING_FAILURE_PREFIX currently lives at src/app/components/grading-recording/grading-rows.ts:214, and src/lib/grade/engine.ts MUST NOT import from a component directory - that is the layer inversion the seam itself forbids three paragraphs earlier. MOVE the constant into src/lib/grade/ and have grading-rows.ts import it from there. Do not duplicate the literal; two copies of a user-visible prefix is exactly the drift this repo keeps re-discovering. RULING 2, THE CSV DOORS: the refusal at the gradebook-CSV doors is isUngraded(row) AND checkRowPostability(...).postable === false - NOT isUngraded alone. The artifact mandated an unconditional isUngraded refusal, which contradicts the shipped A13 guard and the owner's own decision: steps.grading-singles.ts:592 admits a row only when a reviewer typed a real numeric grade, which is precisely the RESCUED row that A13 clause (c) deliberately lets through. Unconditional refusal would post a rescued row to Canvas while silently vanishing the same row from the gradebook CSV built from the same approved set - a divergence no gate here can see. RULING 3, THE REVIEW INSTRUCTION IS BY PROPERTY, NOT BY SPELLING: NO CONSTRUCTION MAY PRODUCE AN OBJECT CARRYING BOTH userId AND ungraded. The artifact told reviewers to look for `as GradeResult`, and the check compiled two CHEAPER escapes that carry no cast and no assertion for a grep to find - Object.assign onto a typed row, and assignment through a loosely-typed alias - both of which are the natural shape in parseGradeResult and coerceGradeResult, which build field by field from untrusted input. A spelling-based review instruction cannot catch them. The sanctioned repairs are a branch or a conditional spread. RULING 4: add fillGradebookCsv to the wiring oracle's builder-name seed. The door TABLE is complete, but the durable instrument was seeded with only postCanvasGradesAction, buildCanvasGradebookCsv and buildMoodleGradebookCsv, and fillGradebookCsv (steps.grading-singles.ts:660, writing scores into a real gradebook file at gradebook-csv.ts:463) would let a future module reach a gradebook through a door the oracle cannot see. RULING 5, AND THIS IS THE SILENT-GREEN ONE: the factory carries codeExecution, gradedRepo and gradedRef UNCHANGED, and the oracle asserts all three. The artifact's field table omitted them while its prose claimed byte-identity with today's failure row, so built literally a failed repo grade would lose the repo and commit that types.ts:154-159 exists to record - the evidence for defending a grade to a student - and lose the sandbox run. Nothing here would catch it: the allowlist coverage test uses a hand-built sentinel rather than the engine, engine.test.ts asserts none of the three, and no component renders. RULING 6, THE SENTINEL GUARANTEE: every field on every sentinel EXCEPT that member's own excluded key (a two-element exclusion set) - NOT the artifact's proposed SENTINELS.some(...), which is strictly WEAKER than the check it replaces while being described as stronger. Under .some, a future optional field given a value on the graded sentinel and omitted from the ungraded one passes, and the ungraded sentinel's three round-trips then preserve it trivially - verbatim the defect that check's own comment says it exists to catch. ALSO CARRIED FORWARD, from the check and not disputed: engine.ts:162-171 does NOT compile under the union and the fix is narrowing gradeSubmission to Promise<GradedResult>; "unpostable by construction" is honestly "closes the one hole present today"; two producers were missing from the table (embedded-grader/index.ts:196-211 and discussion.ts:471-486, hidden from a grep requiring "resubmitNotice:"); submissionTruncated has NO production rule on a not-attempted row and cannot be "carried from the entry" because StudentSubmissionEntry has no such field and truncateSubmission never runs for an unattempted one; report.ungraded needs a stated production rule; and the O1/O4/O6 oracles must pin a rubric that actually parses criteria or they pass vacuously. N13b IS SETTLED IN THE SEAM'S FAVOUR: the empty-canonical path is real and reachable, a not-attempted row with an empty rubricAreas raises totalResults without raising resultsWithArea, so the class-trends exclusion is REQUIRED rather than optional - the author was right to decline to agree with the orchestrator's paraphrase, and this is recorded as an instance of a seat correctly refusing a handed-down claim. REMEASURED 2026-09-15 - THE FLOOR HALF OF THIS ROW'S OWN TITLE IS STILL OPEN. class-trends-draft.ts was not touched by 7c409a6 (absent from its file list) - DEFAULT_CLASS_TRENDS_DRAFT_FLOOR = 5 still stands at :18 and is still enforced, now at :168 (re-measured 2026-09-22; the :154 this row carried was correct before 2c92fec moved it). THE HEADER-COMMENT HALF IS DONE: 2c92fec rewrote that paragraph on 2026-09-20 and it now sits at :88-104, states the cap correctly as DEFAULT_MAX_SUBMISSIONS = 40 at gemini.ts:32, and says outright that the two knobs no longer coincide. So nobody removing the floor still owes a comment fix here - only the floor itself. The row's instrument line numbers for gemini.ts and engine.ts are themselves now stale post-implementation and are corrected above - re-cite them rather than reusing the pre-7c409a6 numbers. The full ruling history already on file above (the six seam rulings, the owner quotes setting the bound at 40, the sequencing rulings) is KEPT as an accurate record of what was decided and built, confirmed against 7c409a6. CLOSED 2026-09-28 AT d5179a92 - the floor is removed. This was the only part still owed; the cap and the not-attempted seam (the bulk of this note's history) shipped earlier at 7c409a6 and the seam rulings. Blast radius grep-derived (6 files: the constant + getter + below-floor status member + the floor param threaded through composeClassTrendsDraft, its state machine, and its panel branch); no reader beyond them. composeClassTrendsDraft is now 3 params; a 1-submission run drafts (status ok). Tests rewritten not deleted, proven red by reintroducing the floor; suite green at 23385, tsc clean. STALE DOCS from the removal also corrected in the closure commit: REGRESSION.md entry 439 supersedes the floor claims in 423d/423e, and owner-private-secrets.md dropped the two retired env vars (tunables 35->33). Historical planning docs (a16-*, the triage) still name the constant as a record of what existed then and were left as history. State -> verification: nothing renders here, so the below-floor-branch removal and the drafts-below-5 behavior are confirmed by the rewritten tests and are otherwise an owner-walk observation. | - |
| Grading runs: bounds, failures, not-attempted rows, class-trends signal | RES-FILL-12 | verification | RULING 134 deliberately SUPERSEDES W4-1's byte-identity invariant for the no-parseable-criteria fallback case - reconcile.ts:14-17 and reconcile.test.ts's own header still assert the invariant unconditionally and must be rewritten to say it is superseded, not broken | - | - | - | docs/a39-incremental-fill-architecture.md section 13 RES-FILL-12 and section 6.5 (RULING 134), plus section 14.1's disposition of build-check finding B5: reconcile.ts:14-17's comment and reconcile.test.ts's header, both read against the post-change tree, plus the re-captured frozen literal's own provenance line naming the command that produced it (F23). | docs/a39-incremental-fill-architecture.md section 13, RES-FILL-12 (round 2 of two; uncommitted in the working tree at filing time - a transient push failure per the filing brief, content read directly from disk per docs/DEV_LOOP.md step 0); filed as a row 2026-09-28. | WHAT IT IS: this is a SUPERSESSION, not a defect - RULING 134 deliberately changes reconcileRun's empty-canonical-column fallback from an isolated per-run computation into a call to the single shared unionAreaNames implementation (closing build-check finding B5, a real cross-route divergence), which means the byte-identity invariant W4-1 originally froze for reconcile.ts:51-58's fallback case no longer holds for that case - it still holds for the parseable-criteria case, which this ruling does not touch. reconcile.ts:14-17's comment and reconcile.test.ts's own header currently assert the OLD, now-superseded invariant unconditionally, with no qualification. WHY DEFERRED: correcting the comment before the RULING 134 change lands would itself be premature - the invariant is still literally true in the current, unchanged code. OWNER: the implementer of the reconcile.ts:51-58 change (RULING 134's own implementation). INSTRUMENT: both comment blocks (reconcile.ts:14-17, reconcile.test.ts's header) read against the post-change tree, plus the re-captured frozen literal's own provenance line naming the exact command that produced the new frozen value (this design's F23 - the literal must be RE-CAPTURED BY RUNNING IT, not hand-edited to a guessed value). STEP: the same commit that changes reconcile.ts:51-58 rewrites both comment blocks to say the byte-identity invariant holds for the parseable-criteria case and is DELIBERATELY SUPERSEDED for the fallback case, naming RULING 134 by number so a future reader can trace why. WHAT BREAKS IF IGNORED: a frozen literal sitting beside a comment that still claims an invariant the code no longer has is how a legitimate, deliberate design change reads as a silently broken guard to the next person who finds it - the same silenced-guard failure mode named in RES-FILL-1 and RES-FILL-9, but here the underlying change is INTENTIONAL and correctly authorized by a ruling, which the comment must say plainly rather than leaving the reader to guess whether it was a mistake. CONFIRMED 2026-09-28: src/lib/grade/reconcile.ts:14-20's comment already states the byte-identity invariant holds for the PARSEABLE-CRITERIA case and is "DELIBERATELY SUPERSEDED" for the no-parseable-criteria fallback, naming RULING 134 by number - the comment this row flagged as needing rewriting has already been rewritten as instructed. | - |
| One upload per student: boundary, auto-grade, rubric ingest | RES-FILL-3 | verification | gradeSubmissions (engine.ts:365-423) hand-composes extractSubmissions + decideCollisionRefusal + inferFileNameConvention + groupSubmissionsByStudent, a composition extractStudentEntries will now also perform - freeze a student-attribution oracle before consolidating them | - | - | - | docs/a39-incremental-fill-architecture.md section 13 RES-FILL-3, citing engine.ts:365-423's hand-composed ingestion steps, and noting 2f06261 gave assignUnclaimedLabel three call sites - so more than two compositions of overlapping ingestion steps exist today, understating round 1's own framing. | docs/a39-incremental-fill-architecture.md section 13, RES-FILL-3 (round 2 of two; uncommitted in the working tree at filing time - a transient push failure per the filing brief, content read directly from disk per docs/DEV_LOOP.md step 0); filed as a row 2026-09-28. | WHAT IT IS: the whole-run engine (engine.ts:365-423) and the fill's own extractStudentEntries both independently compose the same four ingestion steps - extraction, collision refusal, filename-convention inference, and student grouping - which is exactly the kind of duplicated composition that drifts the two routes apart the way item #9 in section 4.1 already did (the incremental route deriving student names WITHOUT the LLM inference, closed by this design, but only for the two routes as they exist today). WHY DEFERRED: consolidating the engine's ingestion under the SAME commit that changes student-name derivation would leave no independent oracle to catch a consolidation defect - the fix must land, THEN a follow-up chunk can safely consolidate, oracle in hand. OWNER: the chunk that next owns src/lib/grade/engine.ts. INSTRUMENT: a frozen-literal oracle over gradeSubmissions's returned results[].student for a zip whose filenames need inference, captured BEFORE any consolidation of the two compositions. STEP: after the A39 fill ships, not inside it. WHAT BREAKS IF IGNORED: result.student is the React key and the persisted-edits key (gradingResultsEditsKey) - a future consolidation that silently changes which student a row's name resolves to would relabel a grade, post a CSV, or write a Canvas comment under the wrong student's name, with no test catching it because the only oracle proposed here does not yet exist. SCOPE + CHECK SHIP: no HARMFUL drift today (both paths call groupSubmissionsByStudent identically) so this is a guard + small consolidation, NOT a no-op; the recon also surfaced a wrong-student merge filed as GRADE-INFER-MERGE. W1 (the frozen attribution ORACLE) test notes passed the check (1 blocker applied: F10 AND F15 both labelled KNOWN-DEFECT, F12 distinct; the M5/F15 prediction is order-dependent so not banked, F15 kill is M4/P4). Only a verified call path makes a site owner-only. THE SCOPE REDIRECTED THE WORK and that redirection is the useful part: the 250ms figure is SELF-ADMITTED as assumed (the source says so, and git log -S returns only the commit that created the file), but THE RUNTIME NEVER READS IT. It sizes two constants once. So the honest first move was not to re-measure or re-guess the number - it was to read two signals that were already computed on every tick and thrown away, truncatedByBudget and scannedUserPrefixes, which the workflow extraction ignored while pulling three other fields. WHY THAT SIGNAL MATTERS MORE THAN THE NUMBER: the two failure directions are opposite. Phase one breaches LOUDLY against an existing gate whose error message even names the constant. Phase two cap becomes LESS binding and fails SILENTLY - a sweep processing a fraction of what it claims while reporting success. The shipped change surfaces both values in the per-tick summary plus a NON-GATING warning when truncation happens. Sixteen lines added, one changed, zero exit statements: gating is N3 decision and was already made the other way, because the failure count can be non-zero for non-defect reasons until its own blocker resolves. PRESENCE WAS CONFIRMED PER PATH, not assumed: both fields are non-null on all three result paths including the zero-prefix guard and the budget-exceeded early return, and the two auth failures never reach the extraction because a status check exits first. A field absent only on an error path would have printed null and LOOKED like a measurement - the same defect class this row is about, one level up. WHAT REMAINS: the constant is still unmeasured, and there is NO timing instrument in the code at all - so even a production log cannot currently confirm or refute 250ms, which leaves this row worse off than its sibling, whose response body already carries a purpose-built instrument. L10 shipped the procedure into docs/loop/parallel-disjointness.md, and the implementer who wrote it said plainly in its own report that prose will not be enough - and it is right. The evidence is L10's own history: the correct definition was already in that card, was quoted at people twice, and was still missed five times in one day, the fifth by the orchestrator ONE COMMIT AFTER writing the rule down. A sixth card is not the fix. | - | - | - | L10 implementation report, 2026-09-15, plus the five instances recorded in L10 before it closed (A1, N15, A2, A5 fixtures, A5 index.tsx) | L10 | THE PROPOSAL, which is the implementer's and which I endorse: the WAVE GATE runs the grep the card describes and diffs it against the submitted owns list, rather than a human or an agent remembering to run it. The gate already executes git status --short against the assignment, so the machinery and the moment both exist; this adds a second, cheaper question to the same step. WHY THIS IS UNSCOPED AND NOT JUST DISPATCHED - three things have to be settled first and none of them is obvious. (1) WHAT TO GREP FOR. A change to a function has a symbol; a change to a type, a prop, a CSS class or a string constant has a different one, and some have none an implementer could name. A gate that only works for named exports will be silently useless for the other half of waves. (2) FALSE POSITIVES ARE THE WHOLE RISK. Every caller of a widely-used symbol would be flagged, and a gate that cries wolf on a forty-hit grep gets ignored within a week, which is worse than no gate because it looks like coverage. Something has to distinguish "reached by this change" from "merely mentions the symbol", and that judgement may not be mechanisable - in which case the honest answer is a gate that REPORTS the hits for a human to classify rather than one that passes or fails. (3) WHERE IT LIVES. This repo's gates are vitest files; a wave-gate check is not obviously one, since it is about a brief rather than about the tree. It may be a script the orchestrator runs, which makes it exactly as skippable as the card - and if so, say that out loud rather than shipping a gate that is a card with extra steps. RESOLVED WONT-DO (recon docs/l11-scope.md, 2026-10-04): a mechanical owns-list/caller gate is not worth it. Check A (every new export has a non-test caller) would have caught 0 of 4 readable L10 instances and has zero demonstrated yield (0/33 new value exports dead at HEAD over 80 commits); the dangerous shape (helper + unit test, no production caller) is indistinguishable from a legitimate test-exported helper (77 at HEAD) without a ~111-name allow-list = churn. Check B (grep each changed symbol, diff vs owns) is the card's existing grep (parallel-disjointness.md:38-44) and the A1/A2 add-an-option shape has no detectable symbol. Reachability from a UI control is NOT grep-able, so a verify pass runs regardless - a mechanical check would add per-wave spend to catch a miss already caught downstream (tsc at the wave gate, the verify-reachability pass, git-status-vs-assignment). The class is already covered at 5 doc placements (parallel-disjointness.md:38-52, seats.md:160-172, loop-plan.md:41-50 tightened today by retro AI4 36831e0d, traps-spec.md:53-64, DEV_LOOP.md:166). WONT-DO; kept as a verification record. Residual R1: reopen only if 2+ new caller-miss instances reach main (orchestrator measures at loop-retro). | - |

That reasoning is SOUND and must be preserved by any fix. The gap is not that the guard asks the wrong question; it is that the schema cannot EXPRESS the answer. Note the guard is self-limiting - escape 2 (stopHookActive) means it blocks at most once per turn - so this is friction and a misleading signal, not a trap. | Observed across three turns, 2026-09-20; guard source read the same day | THE FIX THAT RESPECTS THE GUARD'S OWN DESIGN is a fifth state, because a state is QUEUE-LOCAL and needs no session-scoped tracking - the precise property stop-guard.ts:10-17 argues for. Proposed: "building", set when a row is dispatched to an implementer and cleared at its push, with selectNext treating it like actionable for ORDERING but not for BLOCKING. That is a real chunk, not a one-liner: it touches types.ts's union and BACKLOG_STATES, isBacklogState, yaml-codec's validation, render.ts's section logic, next.ts's selectNext, stop-guard.ts, and each of their tests, plus the header doc in docs/BACKLOG.md that enumerates the states. AN ALTERNATIVE WORTH WEIGHING FIRST, because it may be cheaper and better: do nothing to the schema and accept that the guard is a floor rather than a signal - it fires, the orchestrator confirms work is running, and the turn continues. The cost of that is real though: a signal that is routinely correct to ignore is a signal that trains you to ignore it, which is the same failure mode as L15's flaky gate and L14's vacuous verify. This row is the third in that family and the family is the point - THIS REPO'S WHOLE METHOD RESTS ON A GATE MEANING SOMETHING, and three separate instruments now say something other than what they appear to. WHOEVER TAKES THIS should decide the family together rather than one row at a time: L14 (an instrument reading clean while measuring nothing), L15 (one reading red while nothing is wrong), L16 (one reading block while the work is already running). | The loop's own docs, gates and process debt | L17 | verification | The exhaustiveness guard on GradeResult and GradingRun is WEAKER THAN I HAVE BEEN CITING IT AS. Found 2026-09-27 while building the equivalent guard for the snapshot row types (shipped 007f615), when the implementer declined to copy this idiom and proved why. It compares Exclude<keyof T, ...> against a HAND-MAINTAINED as-const array of field names - so bumping the array satisfies the type check ON ITS OWN, whether or not the new field ever reached the serializer. A maintainer can add a field to the type, add its name to the list, and never open toWire; the type gate goes green and the field is silently dropped on the wire. The only thing that catches that is a separate runtime sentinel test, i.e. a convention someone has to remember - which is EXACTLY the convention-dependent safety net my own DECISION 4 record already described wrongly once. The snapshot row now derives its key set from ReturnType<typeof toWire> instead, so nothing is maintained by hand and nothing can drift; this row is to decide whether the two older types get the same treatment. NOT a silent fix: converting them means dropping an explicit Record return annotation on each serializer, which is a real type change to code with more callers than the snapshot codec had, and one of the two types is carried across a storage boundary. WHAT THIS ROW MUST NOT DO is add a second idiom for the same job and leave both - if the derived form is adopted, the maintained arrays go away in the same change, or the row is closed as WONT DO with the weakness recorded where the guard is read rather than here. | - | - | - | grep -n "Exclude<keyof" src/lib/grade-result-allowlist-coverage.test.ts (the maintained-array form) against grep -n "ReturnType<typeof toWire>" src/app/components/snapshot-grading/snapshot-row-serialization.ts (the derived form shipped at 007f615); the row is discharged when the two older types use one form or the weakness is recorded at the guard itself | the snapshot-guard wave, 007f615 | Filed by me on 2026-09-27 from the snapshot-guard wave. That is the distinction between a guard and a belief, and I had been quoting this one as a guard. SECOND FACT, from the same wave, which any scope here must handle: the array form CANNOT be used on a type whose shape includes a private unique-symbol key, because a string-literal array can never name it, so Exclude never reduces to never and the guard is a permanent false positive. The snapshot row hit exactly that and had to subtract the base shape structurally. Check whether either older type has the same property before scoping an array-based repair. THE REACHABILITY QUESTION FOR THIS ROW is not whether the guard compiles but whether it FAILS: any proposal must paste the verbatim type error from adding a field to the type and deliberately not to the serializer, per the standing rule that if the red output does not exist the instrument does not exist. | The loop's own docs, gates and process debt | L9 | verification | Source-scanning tests in this repo can be satisfied by COMMENTED-OUT code, and most of them do not defend against it. Found by the N14 wave 1 verification, on the one assertion least able to afford it: the wiring canary whose entire purpose is to stop the snapshot keyboard layer dying silently. The verifier commented out window.addEventListener while leaving removeEventListener in place and NOTHING caught it - tsc, lint, 311 tests and next build all stayed green, because the canary is a regex over raw source and a regex matches text inside a comment exactly as well as live code. RE-MEASURED 2026-09-22 from the repo root: grep -rl readFileSync --include=*.test.ts src \| wc -l returns 203 and grep -rl stripComments --include=*.test.ts src \| wc -l returns 69. So on the order of 134 source-scanning test files have no comment defence, though not all of them need one. The 176/57 this row was filed with on 2026-09-15 is superseded: the undefended gap has WIDENED by 15 files in a week, not narrowed. | - | - | - | grep -rl readFileSync --include=*.test.ts src \| wc -l (203 files) and grep -rl stripComments --include=*.test.ts src \| wc -l (69 files), both re-measured 2026-09-22 from the repo root. The equivalent PowerShell form is Get-ChildItem -Path src -Recurse -Include *.test.ts \| Select-String -Pattern readFileSync -List; the two forms agreed at 176/57 on 2026-09-15, and only the grep form was re-run today, so a disagreement between them has not been re-checked. | N14 wave 1 | THE WAVE 1 INSTANCE IS ALREADY FIXED - this row is about the other 134. UNSCOPED because a blanket sweep is the wrong shape and would be worse than nothing: stripComments is NOT always correct. Some assertions deliberately scan comments (this repo pins RULES and CITATIONS in comments, and docs/REGRESSION.md treats a stale comment as a defect, so a test asserting a comment exists is legitimate). Others assert a string is ABSENT, where stripping comments makes the test WEAKER rather than stronger. So the work is a triage, not a sweep, and the triage rule has to be authored before any file is touched. THE RULE I WOULD START FROM, for whoever scopes this: an assertion that something IS PRESENT in code - a call, a registration, a guard - must strip comments, because commented-out code satisfies it while doing nothing. An assertion that something is ABSENT must NOT strip them, because a commented-out forbidden call is still a signal worth failing on. Assertions about comments themselves are a third category and are left alone. WHERE TO LOOK FIRST: the 22 *.structure.test.ts files (find src -name "*.structure.test.ts" \| wc -l, 2026-09-22 - this row said five, and docs/loop/this-repo.md says twelve in one section and seventeen in another; none of the three is right today, so re-measure rather than quoting any of them), since those are the ones this repo uses as construction guards, and any test whose name contains canary or wiring. ONE LIVE INSTANCE FIXED 2026-09-23 AT 6125260 - the row itself stays OPEN, because this closed the single armed defect the scoping pass could prove, not the class. THE DEFECT: a presence assertion searched for a needle that ALREADY APPEARED IN A DOC COMMENT in the file it checks, so it passed whether or not the real code existed. Proven by flipping only the SECOND occurrence - the actual JSX prop, leaving the comment untouched - and watching all 70 tests stay green. That is a test that had never been able to see the capability it named. THE FIX requires the attribute to sit directly after role=dialog with only whitespace between, which the comment prose cannot satisfy because it separates them with a comma. Proven in both directions: green with the real prop present, and exactly one failure - the target assertion - with it deleted, restored from a cp backup each time rather than any repo-wide git operation. NO NEW STRIPPING CALL SITE WAS ADDED, AND THAT WAS THE POINT. A42 established the same day that this repo comment-stripping idiom treats a MIME wildcard as a comment opener and eats source to the next close, so fixing L9 by copying that idiom would WIDEN A42 blast radius from 85 files toward the 205-file scanning population. A positional regex was sufficient here and invokes none of it. Any later L9 wave must hold that line: the scope records the two rows as adjacent-but-in-tension rather than one fix. WHAT REMAINS ON THIS ROW: the second verified finding, an absence guard that correctly ignores a line comment and FALSELY FIRES on the same import inside a block comment, deliberately left untouched because it is dormant and in another file; and the census itself, which the scope measured as a 113-file floor rather than the row original number, explicitly caveated as not exhaustive. The scoping pass also caught its OWN instrument returning a false clean from a slightly wrong pattern - identical to a true absence - which is the failure class this row is about and is why its numbers carry canaries. FIRST FIX LANDED 2026-09-27 AT 712aa42, and it is the one this row could least afford to leave: THE SHARED STRIPPER DID NOT STRIP. modalAdoptionScan stripComments anchored its line-comment pattern to the start of a line, so a TRAILING comment survived - verified by running the shipped expression verbatim and getting the input back unchanged. Every predicate in that module consumes its output and two test files depend on those predicates, so BOTH inherited this row own defect from the ONE MODULE THE SCOPE NAMED AS THE CONSOLIDATION TARGET. RULING 79: a tokenizer, not a third regex. The other form in the tree is unanchored and has the OPPOSITE defect - it strips the trailing comment and truncates a URL at the double slash, because two slashes inside a string are not a comment. Neither line-wise regex form is correct and none can be. The replacement tracks code, line comments, block comments, all three string forms including nested template substitutions, and regex literals, with its two real limitations documented IN THE FUNCTION HEADER rather than left silent - which is the point, since a silent limitation was the whole defect. THE SECOND SABOTAGE IS THE PROOF THAT MATTERS: making the tokenizer string-blind did not merely fail the synthetic URL case, it went red across four string-shaped cases at once AND CASCADED INTO THREE REAL-FILE ASSERTIONS, misclassifying an actual component and producing an orphaned dialog site. A mutation that changes real classifications proves the defect was LIVE rather than hypothetical. Both sabotages covered their FAMILY rather than one member. AND IT ANSWERED THE QUESTION I MOST WANTED ASKED: no existing assertion was passing BECAUSE of the blindness. It checked every canary in both dependents first - they used whole-line comments only, which the old form handled. So the defect was real but UNTESTED, which is the honest finding rather than the convenient one. WATCH THE CEILING HERE: that file is now 987 of 1000 and the implementer compressed its comments TWICE to fit, once from a draft that would have breached it. Thirteen lines of headroom means the next change to this file needs an extraction first - it reported that rather than ratcheting an allowance. WAVE 1 CLASSIFICATION LANDED AT 2a924ef and amended MY RULING 72: the ruling classifies per ASSERTION, but a stripping call applies per SUBJECT VARIABLE, and 7 of 7 measured mixed subjects carry BOTH kinds - ZERO are pure. So the fix unit is a VARIABLE SPLIT and write sets must be sized in variables, not files. The census was also wrong in BOTH directions, including a file counted as defended because the word appears in its own PROSE; four defence mechanisms exist where the census counts one, so the honest undefended count is 99 of 113 - which is why the wave-3 allowlist is built and NOT frozen. The classification own phantom-stripper detector reported 26 and its control FAILED, because the detector line-comment regex ate the tail of the literal it was hunting: THE INSTRUMENT BUILT TO FIND COMMENT-BLIND SCANNERS WAS ITSELF COMMENT-BLIND. Rebuilt as a tokenizer with 7 of 7 controls; the real answer is 1. THE MIXED-SUBJECT SPLIT LANDED 2026-09-27 AT b53faa6, and its headline is a REFUSAL rather than a delivery. Wave 1 reported 8 mixed subjects; THREE ARE REAL AND ARE SPLIT, FIVE WERE ARTIFACTS OF THE DETECTOR KEYING ON VARIABLE NAME RATHER THAN LEXICAL SCOPE. The implementer refused to move the five instead of producing eight tidy edits, and PROVED each refusal: for four it extracted the exact substring each assertion matches and showed no comment token in it - a single-line object literal, a captured arrow-function body whose only nearby comment sits OUTSIDE the slice, a one-line JSX attribute, and an import clause only the real import statement can match. For the fifth the variable is re-declared per scope under a reused name, so there is nothing shared to split. SO READ THAT 8 AS EIGHT NAME COLLISIONS, OF WHICH THREE ARE REAL SPLITS. A count that means something different from what it appears to mean is the recurring finding of this row, and here the row own wave-1 instrument produced one. The three real splits name the raw variable so the distinction is unmissable, carry a comment saying which assertions need it and why, prove comment residency by sed on the target file, and keep assertion counts IDENTICAL before and after - a repoint, not a rewrite. Both directions are sabotage-proven per file: commenting out the guarded code turns the code-behaviour assertion red across the line, trailing and block families, and DELETING the real comment turns only the file-content assertion red while everything else stays green. AND IT FOUND A LIVE DEFECT IN THE TOKENIZER SHIPPED HOURS EARLIER AT 712aa42, which I verified by driving the real module: a trailing comment after a JSX CLOSING TAG is NOT stripped, because the regex-versus-division heuristic treats the slash in a closing tag as a regex opener and then swallows the comment marker as part of a phantom regex. It matters more than it looks, because this tokenizer exists to stop scanners being satisfied by commented-out code and .tsx component source is exactly what those scanners read - so the blindness lands in the highest-value part of the population. HOW IT WAS FOUND IS THE PART TO KEEP: its first sabotage attempt STAYED GREEN WHEN IT SHOULD HAVE GONE RED, and rather than picking a different mutation and moving on, it traced why. A passing sabotage is a finding, not an inconvenience. It then completed the family proof with a non-JSX-adjacent shape and flagged the defect rather than spending the file remaining 13 lines of headroom without a ruling. It also imported the tokenizer rather than duplicating it, correctly: the cross-test-file import ban does not apply because the tokenizer lives in a non-test module. THE JSX LEAK WAS FIXED 2026-09-27 AT 89980da. Cause, diagnosed rather than guessed: the regex-versus-division heuristic inspects the last NON-WHITESPACE character, and an angle bracket IS operand-introducing because a comparison followed by a regex is valid JS. In a closing tag the slash is GLUED to that bracket, and the heuristic cannot tell the two apart - so it opened a phantom regex, scanned for a closing slash, and consumed the FIRST slash of the following comment marker. The marker was destroyed before the comment check ever saw it. The fix distinguishes a bracket that is literally the previous character from one reached by skipping whitespace: six lines in one predicate, no state-machine rewrite. I verified all eight shapes by driving the real module: the four that leaked now strip - closing tag with and without preceding text, and the block-comment form - while the four that must survive still do, including a URL inside a string, a slash inside a regex literal, and division still resolving as division. SELF-CLOSING TAGS WERE NEVER BROKEN BY THIS DEFECT, established rather than assumed - there is always a space, identifier or quote between bracket and slash, so they already resolved to division - and the family sabotage confirmed it by leaving that one test green while the other three went red. The sabotage reverted the ACTUAL guard line rather than describing a mutation, so the new tests are proven to catch the regression rather than merely to describe it. THAT FIX LEFT THE FILE AT 999 OF 1000 - ONE LINE - so I RULED the extraction rather than leaving the next change to discover the wall, and it landed at 01b2f30: 999 to 687 with a 405-line leaf, 313 lines of headroom rather than one. Test total 179, IDENTICAL to the pre-split baseline, re-run by me rather than taken on report. IT WIDENED THE SEAM I SUGGESTED AND JUSTIFIED IT WITH EVIDENCE: I floated the tokenizer alone, which removes about 150 lines and leaves 850. It cut along generic source-scanning primitives versus modal-adoption policy instead, and the justification is what makes it right rather than merely bigger - THE TOKENIZER WAS ALREADY IMPORTED BY TWO TEST FILES WITH NOTHING TO DO WITH MODAL ADOPTION, so those primitives were being treated as general infrastructure before anyone named them that. Zero references back into the parent, so no cycle - the failure mode where a back-imported constant silently yields undefined and tsc misses it. THE MOST VALUABLE THING IN THE SPLIT WAS RUNNING THE ENUMERATION CHECK BEFORE CUTTING, NOT AFTER. That test file carries a bundle guard failing if any non-test file imports the scanner, because it reaches node:fs and must never enter the client bundle. The new leaf imports node:fs too, and THE OLD GUARD PATTERN WOULD NOT HAVE MATCHED IT - so the split would have produced a second file with identical bundle risk and no guard covering it, with every gate green. Extended in the same change to name both modules. That is the unguarded-new-file hazard, real rather than hypothetical. RESOLVED F1-only (recon docs/l9-scope.md rev4; the broad rev-3 Wave-2a/Wave-3-ratchet/Wave-4 sweeps WITHDRAWN - census proxy wrong 5 ways, 0 live instances, and the row premise "absence assertions must not strip" was already withdrawn by Ruling 72; post-A42 measurement confirms it). Per-scanner classification: 16 scanners already fixed by A42 (tokenizer), 96 handed to A42 residual R-W2-1 (both axes measured non-live, 0 wrong verdicts), only ONE genuinely-distinct LIVE defect. SHIPPED F1+F2 in 83838b88 (3 files): grade-result-doors.wiring.test.ts was a SILENT-GREEN - its presence assertion `\bungraded\b` over RAW text was satisfied ONLY by a comment (steps.grading-draft-flow.ts:598-601), not the real ungradedResults/gradedResults calls (:20,:296-297). Fix: import stripComments + strip before scanning + widen the predicate to ungradedResults\|gradedResults + a canary proving it reads real calls not comments; the mandatory EXCLUSIONS entry in strip-comments-agreement.structure.test.ts (958<1000); F2 one traps-tests.md bullet. SABOTAGE PROOF: with the real calls commented out, the OLD test passed (silent-green real) and the FIXED test reds exactly on steps.grading-draft-flow.ts. INLINE-VERIFIED (2-file test fix): strip+widened predicate+canary present, gate green, file under ceiling. L9 COMPLETE. Owner-confirmable: close as F1+F2 (recommended, acted on) vs also the Wave-3 ratchet (recommend NO - census proxy wrong, 1 live instance already fixed by F1). Residuals RR1 (unprobed presence scanners, triggered not scheduled) + RR2 (runtime-import-graph comment handling unread). | - |

The owner uses this tool and asked for a control that is already on the screen, already labelled Regenerate, and already has an aria-label. That is a finding about the surface, not about the owner. Something is hiding it, and the candidates are cheap to check but MUST be checked against the RENDERED page rather than the source, because NO COMPONENT IS RENDERED BY ANY TEST HERE and every gate is green today. THE CANDIDATES, in the order worth testing. (1) IT MAY BE BEHIND A CONDITION: the control sits inside AnnouncementDraftSlot, which only exists once a slot HAS a draft - if the owner looked before drafting, or at a slot in a non-drafted phase, there was nothing to see. (2) IT MAY BE VISUALLY LOST: it is one of several arm-then-confirm controls in the same slot row, sharing one control contract and one stylesheet, so it can read as part of a cluster rather than as the primary way to redo a draft. (3) THE ARM-THEN-CONFIRM SHAPE MAY ITSELF BE THE PROBLEM: a first click does not regenerate, it arms - and a control that appears to do nothing on click is indistinguishable from a broken one. That shape is deliberate and it is a confirm step, which this repo does NOT trade away for fewer clicks, so the fix is disclosure and placement, never removing the confirm. DO NOT BUILD A SECOND REGENERATE. This repo has a recorded class where a capability shipped twice because the first copy was not found, and a separate recorded class where a feature shipped DEAD with every gate green. This row is the mirror of both: the feature is live and the REACHABILITY is what failed. FIRST STEP, owed before any code: the owner says where they looked and what they expected to click. That is one question, and it is the only instrument this checkout has - the network is blocked, there is no .env, and nothing renders under vitest, so no agent here can observe the rendered control at all. | A17 is a DISCOVERABILITY finding, not a feature (AC docs/a17-acceptance-criteria.md, SHIP). It only renders once a draft exists. OWNER QUESTION (every answer closes the row): open the announcement-from-walkthrough tool, generate a draft, look at the action row under the drafted message - do you SEE the Regenerate button? (a) YES and usable -> momentary miss, close A17, no code (tell me what threw you); (b) NO / would not have found or trusted it -> which matches: OBS-1 no drafted slot was on screen; OBS-2 the row was below the fold; OBS-3 Regenerate read as minor/ambiguous next to the filled Post button; OBS-4 the first click seemed to do nothing (the arm step went unnoticed) - your pick names the one small relabel/reposition/emphasis/disclosure fix (a follow-up row; the confirm step stays). |

Recommended instead: the A40 per-column insert-payload pin as a named template + a write-at-insert-or-say-why convention at the authoring site. | What it costs an instructor to get from rubric to graded, measured against a chat window | RES-FILL-13 | verification | The incremental grading route still does not auto-scroll to its results after the A39 fill, because the identity-keyed effect fires while results is empty and the scroll target does not exist yet - unchanged from today, deliberately, and instrument-less because its only enforcer would be a render | - | - | - | - | docs/a39-incremental-fill-architecture.md section 13, RES-FILL-13 (round 2 of two; uncommitted in the working tree at filing time - a transient push failure per the filing brief, content read directly from disk per docs/DEV_LOOP.md step 0); filed as a row 2026-09-28. | WHAT IT IS: the incremental grading route has a scroll-target NODE (sectionRef, restored by item #8 in section 4.1) but the effect that would scroll to it is keyed to the run identity (this design's section 10, closing the build check's M6 finding about a node without a trigger) - that effect fires once per run, at the moment the run identity is created, which is BEFORE the first result row exists and the node is rendered. So the route still does not auto-scroll, exactly as it does not today; the fill closes the mechanism gap (M6) without closing the user-visible gap. THIS IS DELIBERATE, not an oversight: the design states a first-arrival latch (re-firing the scroll on the first row landing, not on run start) would fix it, and does not build one because it decided the behaviour is acceptable to leave as-is pending owner interest. WHY IT CARRIES NO INSTRUMENT (LEFT DELIBERATELY EMPTY RATHER THAN GIVEN A PROXY): nothing in this repo's vitest renders a component (node env, collects only src/**/*.test.ts per docs/loop/this-repo.md section 6), and the only real enforcer of 'does the page scroll to the right place' is a render - so no source-level grep or pure-function test can stand in for it without claiming more than it measures, which this design and this filing both explicitly decline to do. OWNER: the chunk that next owns GradingTab.tsx, if the owner wants the gap closed at all. INSTRUMENT: none in this repo - recorded as instrument-less rather than assigned a proxy grep that would not actually catch a regression here. STEP: the design lists this behaviour as a note at its own owner-walk item 1 (time-to-first-row) rather than as a gating item - a first-arrival latch is the proposed fix if the owner wants it, not proposed as required work. WHAT BREAKS IF IGNORED: nothing regresses - this is the status quo, carried forward rather than created - but an instructor running a long incremental grade from the top of a long page will not be auto-scrolled to the first arriving row the way they might expect from the feature's own premise (something appears early), and must scroll manually to see it. SHIPPED a914695a (2026-09-29): the scroll effect's dependency gains a first-arrival term (incrementalDone > 0) so it fires once when the first result lands; additive after runResetKey(...) so F25 stays green, not a bare [displayRun] (no scroll-jack), whole-run branch untouched. Happy path unchanged (same rubric, same grades - existing R6 tests green). Incidental win: ANY collision-refused zip now skips the model, not just blank-rubric ones. NOTE: grading.ts is at 977 lines - close to the 1000 ceiling; a future edit there should budget for extraction or expect to split.
